**By works:** [The Organization of Will](works/organization-of-will.md) (15) · [What Survives Translation?](works/what-survives-translation.md) (40) · [luria: nucleating an argument](works/luria-nucleating-an-argument.md) (19) · [The Self as a Recursive Ecology of Meaning and Influence](works/self-as-recursive-ecology.md) (19) · [The Organization of Perspective](works/organization-of-perspective.md) (10) · [The Self Without a Sovereign](works/self-without-a-sovereign.md) (5) · [Who Gets to Say Stop?](works/who-gets-to-say-stop.md) (2) · [The Character That Changed Parties](works/character-that-changed-parties.md) (2) · [Who Makes the Promise When Governments Change?](works/who-makes-the-promise.md) (1)

What the status column means in this scheme — the words are luria's, the meanings are this project's.

| Status | | Means |
|---|---|---|
| `Active` | The current account | The best explanation the record holds for why this happens |
| `Proposed` | Offered | Stated and plausible, on evidence that is suggestive rather than settling — the document says what would settle it |
| `Deferred` | Not yet judged | Filed because the question is real; no position taken on the answer |
| `Rejected` | Disbelieved | Tested and failed, or contradicted by later work — and the thing it explained may still work perfectly well |
| `Superseded` | Replaced | A later account covers the same ground better, and this one names it |

| # | Title | Summary | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [CLAIM-001](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-001.md) | The conditional-diffusion literature already covers directed transport between observational scenarios, including changing covers | The owner at U34, quoting A93's research opportunity: "I think conditional diffusion literature has us covered here." Accepted for compositional conditioning and transport between distributions; never shown for changing covers, and limited by A97 itself. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-002](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-002.md) | A text's identity across retellings is a family of relations, namely identity of the artifact, continuity of transmission and similarity of communicative function, which is why a distant retelling can be recognizably descended from an original it no longer resembles | A52 §13.5 with A50 §7, recovered. A50 called the point that "the act of translation changes the conditions for subsequent translation" possibly "the most consequential". The manuscript keeps a family of transformations but not the three relations. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-003](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-003.md) | A global workspace need not give every subsystem the same access: access is compartmented in brains as in organizations, so "global" overstates it | The owner's point at U26: an organization's permission structure plays the role functional connectivity plays in a brain, and the daydreaming driver shows access within one agent can be compartmented. | Deferred |
| [CLAIM-004](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-004.md) | Not every difference between translations is a difference of frame: some change the act being framed | A18's passive/active distinction, recovered. It outlived the act object it presupposed: A38 §7.2 and A40 §9.2 used it to undercut the relativity analogy. It fell out with the relativity section, which the manuscript omits, not by refutation. The manuscript's four-way split (§4) names structural reorganization but gives no criterion. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-005](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-005.md) | Pragmatic fidelity is the match between distributions of context-indexed communicative judgements across a correspondence of source and target contexts, not the similarity of one canonical meaning vector | A24's D(τ), the origin of the transport framework, and the manuscript's L_obs (§6). One of three terms of the manuscript's fidelity functional. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-006](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-006.md) | What fundamentally distinguishes variations of a translation is the frame from which it is delivered | The owner's first thesis (U6). Narrowed, corrected and restated in speech-act terms over A11–A14, losing "the main thing" on the way, and replaced by the manuscript's weaker claim that proposition preservation is neither necessary nor sufficient. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-061](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-061.md) |
| [CLAIM-007](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-007.md) | In a bridge or chain of progressive reconstruction the path itself is observable: which constraints survive early, which are introduced late, and which relations emerge only through refinement | A97 §3 on Schrödinger bridges, recovered. The manuscript keeps the bridge only as "a complementary path-space viewpoint" (§7); the programme of observing the path was dropped without critique. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-008](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-008.md) | The abstract similarity between renderings is similarity of higher-order latents, which can survive large differences in lower-order ones | The owner's U27 request and A73's answer: a hierarchy of latents up to a relational configuration, with D_prag, D_dyn and local versus global distances. Superseded at U28 when the owner made the levels levels of observables. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-042](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-042.md) |
| [CLAIM-009](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-009.md) | Pragmatic judgements may be formally contextual: their context-relative distributions may admit no global extension once direct context effects are accounted for | A24's "contextuality hypothesis, which remains open", and A30's H2. The manuscript keeps it as a possibility its empirical programme tests (§3, §9, §10) and disclaims any presumption that it holds. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-010](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-010.md) | On this account every control system has a will | The permissiveness objection. Raised by the assistant at A112, and re-derived at least six times afterwards as though new. Answered by agent-indexing ([CLAIM-080](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-080.md), [CLAIM-116](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-116.md)); one code is what makes the recurrences one objection. | Rejected |
| [CLAIM-011](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-011.md) | Intertwining with an uninformative correspondence is vacuous or lossy, so the source–target correspondence must be anchored to independently measured distinctions and evaluated on tasks other than those it was estimated on | The assistant's own objections at A34 §3 and A40 §6.4, with the remedy of A40 §VIII. Recovered: the manuscript keeps the intertwining identity but states neither limit nor remedy, apparently by inadvertence. | Active |
| [CLAIM-012](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-012.md) | Will is a second- or higher-order attractor in an agent's dynamics | The account of will as the exchange first stated it, with the owner's operational definition at U34. Superseded by the agent-indexed [CLAIM-027](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-027.md). | Superseded — by [CLAIM-027](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-027.md) |
| [CLAIM-013](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-013.md) | Existing text-to-image benchmarks evaluate object presence and attributes, and need extending to pragmatically consequential relations and social uptake | The manuscript's §10, Case III, which calls T2I-CompBench and GenEval object-centered baselines. [CLAIM-097](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-097.md) qualifies that for T2I-CompBench. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-014](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-014.md) | Krifka's performative updates fix which changes in conversational standing count, and Blackwell comparison tests whether a rendering preserves the information needed to recover them | A93 §5, recovered: "could become one of the manuscript's distinctive theoretical integrations." Krifka was in C6's references and A93's priority list (ninth) and is absent from the manuscript and the record. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-015](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-015.md) | Classical structuralism studies relations within a system, whereas this theory makes the transport of relational systems its object and does not require structure to remain invariant | A102's contrast under U36, objected to by the owner at U37 ("symmetry is a kind of invariance, no?") and conceded at A105: "My earlier contrast between structural invariance and structural transport was too sharp." | Superseded — by [CLAIM-113](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-113.md) |
| [CLAIM-016](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-016.md) | An organized agent's will is its own to the degree that its operative commitments arise through, remain integrated with, and are answerable to its constitutive organization | Organizational authenticity (A252), proposed at the owner's request at U70. Set aside with its whole line at the audit of 2026-10-05 (U76–U77): "ownership criteria remain insufficiently defended". | Deferred |
| [CLAIM-017](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-017.md) | What survives translation is the invariants, and approximately preserved relations, of communicative transformation | The crystallized argument's final thesis (A110, U38). The manuscript's answer, written three minutes later in C7, is receiver-relative: what "a receiving system can still reconstruct and act upon". No turn announces or argues the change. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md) |
| [CLAIM-018](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-018.md) | Canguilhem's distinction between functioning under a norm and capacity for norm-changing adaptation corresponds to transformation capacity | An interpretation, not a premise (Section 6.8): "Nor does the present argument depend upon importing Canguilhem's account into viability theory." Its absence from the premises of [ARG-007](../../record/arguments.d/ARG-007.md) is how the record says so. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-019](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-019.md) | Lévi-Straussian transformational analysis, as Descola reads it and as it has been modelled computationally, is direct prior art for structural transport, not only a philosophical precursor | A102 §2, recovered: Lévi-Strauss treated myth variants as related by transformations, and "Recent computational work has explicitly modeled his analysis". The manuscript §2 mentions Lévi-Strauss's transformations but cites neither Descola nor the computational work. | Active |
| [CLAIM-020](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-020.md) | A translation's quality is the number of formal and semantic constraints it preserves | Hofstadter's starting criterion, as A4 reports it, and the Pareto- frontier picture of fidelity around it. Refuted in the same reply by the mother's translation. | Rejected |
| [CLAIM-021](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-021.md) | The mechanisms composing a will-organization are not novel | Conceded to [CLAIM-024](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-024.md): the claimed novelty is a common explanatory role across realizers, not a mechanism (Sections 3.7, 7.1). | Active |
| [CLAIM-022](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-022.md) | Explaining practical efficacy does not settle whose the will is | Section 7.2, "No. The theory explains practical efficacy, not ownership." What the authenticity line tried to supply, the paper now disclaims; [QUESTION-011](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-011.md) holds the question. | Active |
| [CLAIM-023](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-023.md) | The contribution is not that translation is pragmatic or that speech acts change contexts, but a specified, testable account of which communicative distinctions a translation preserves | Disclaimed from A35 §9 on and sharpened at A40 against Gutt and Krifka. The manuscript §1 keeps it ("not the claim that each individual ingredient is novel") and cites Gutt, but drops Krifka, whom four replies named as the closest prior art. | Active |
| [CLAIM-024](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-024.md) | Nothing here is new: planning theory, control theory, executive control and institutional theory already study every mechanism the account names | "The most important objection" (Section 7.1), re-derived three times in the exchange with different criteria each time. Proposed: the paper concedes the mechanisms ([CLAIM-021](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-021.md)) and stakes everything on an explanatory gain not yet shown. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-025](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-025.md) v2 | An operative regime, the governing organization among regimes, and the capacity to transform that organization can persist or fail separately | Section 6.2, from the emergency variant of Northstar. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-026](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-026.md) | Translation is not the transmission of an invariant semantic object but the selective preservation of decision-relevant information across changing observational and interpretive structures | A86's "deeper thesis" under U31, with its dynamical complement. Renamed the framework "contextual pragmatic information transport", the manuscript's subtitle from C6 on. Superseded at U35 by proposal v6 (A100); the manuscript's closing sentence is its descendant. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-048](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-048.md) |
| [CLAIM-027](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-027.md) v3 | Which of an agent's available regimes becomes effective depends on maintained relations — accessibility, precedence, persistence, jurisdiction, suspension and transformation — that are not reducible to the contents of the competing regimes | The paper's central thesis. Proposed, not Active: the audit called it the strongest original contribution and also the one still owing a comparison with planning, executive control and institutional decision procedures. It is the main claim of the work while not yet believed outright — credence and inclusion disagree, as they may. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-028](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-028.md) | No result from the exchange's four experiments reached a later draft: the authored chains were excluded by rule, and the synthetic constructions were dropped without a stated reason | What became of E1–E4. C6 Appendix F made the provenance caveats a rule ("Do not use assistant-authored examples as independent LLM samples"), and A89 called C6 "a theoretical manuscript with a specified empirical program". The synthetic results of E1 were legitimate existence constructions and their loss is not explained. | Active |
| [CLAIM-029](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-029.md) v2 | A language model's response to a context is functionally a parameterized pragmatic frame | The owner at U17, offered as a motivating example "maybe even just for notational purposes". The assistant made it the primary formal example (A43), then one running case of three (A52). Its formal statement, frame as a parameterized family of contexts, did not survive outline v4. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-030](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-030.md) v2 | An organized whole's operative intention is not the expectation of its members' intentions; it is produced by systemic mechanisms that can evolve apart from what the members intend | The owner's thesis at U12, and the one that ran through the whole exchange. List and Pettit's result that a group judging proposition by proposition from its members' judgments can fail to be rational is its formal anchor, proved for the court agenda ([THEORY-725](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-725.md)); the general impossibility is [THEORY-127](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-127.md), still Proposed. The fictional cases illustrate it. | Active |
| [CLAIM-031](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-031.md) | A reconstruction chain reorganizes observational constraints rather than only accumulating metric error: a text can change greatly while keeping a few consequential constraints, and one changed constraint can change the act | A75's self-critique of the metric drift bound, "something our earlier metric formulation obscured". The manuscript keeps the bound and a soft form of the limit (§8: invariants, decision risks and compatibility structures "need not coincide"). | Active |
| [CLAIM-032](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-032.md) | Northstar's procedures are not morally or historically analogous to the regime Fraenkel described; the Dual State supplies one structural point and nothing more | The caution the paper states before using Fraenkel (Section 5.6). As an objection to the inference it narrows the respect in which the analogy holds, to structure alone. | Active |
| [CLAIM-033](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-033.md) v3 | In published translations, renderings that lose distinctions along one dimension commonly gain specificity along another, so loss is not one-dimensional | A75, recovered: transport maps region to region, and "the translation can broaden conceptual possibilities while narrowing interpersonal interpretation". Dropped with the region picture's replacement by sections, without critique. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-034](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-034.md) | Fidelity is one evaluative goal among adaptation, critique and parody, and a deliberately adaptive translation calls for one-way simulation of the source's relevant possibilities rather than equivalence | A38 §11.5 and A40 §7.3, §14.2–14.3, recovered. Dropped with the bisimulation section and the ethics discussion, apparently by inadvertence; the manuscript keeps only that transport is directed. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-035](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-035.md) | Translation operations are not invertible, need not compose by a fixed rule, and preserve no structure given in advance, so the relativity analogy holds only as a research programme | A18's own limit on its analogy: "the physics analogy has a hard limit." Accepted at once. Its content reached the manuscript as the semigroup of §4. | Active |
| [CLAIM-036](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-036.md) | Communicative meaning is constituted through systems of distinctions and relations, and translation transports these systems, selectively preserving or transforming their differential, probabilistic and interactional structure | A102's thesis under U36 ("feels like there's a connection to structuralism here"). Structuralism became the philosophical foundation; its contrast with transport was overstated and conceded at U37. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-108](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-108.md) |
| [CLAIM-037](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-037.md) v2 | Formal contextuality is a failure of global extension, distinct from ordinary context dependence, and needs Contextuality-by-Default when marginals shift with context | The manuscript's §3 and §9. It separates three things: context dependence, stochastic noncommutativity, and contextuality as an obstruction to a global section. [CLAIM-069](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-069.md) names the version of CbD it should cite. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-038](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-038.md) v2 | A communicative object is a context-indexed system of empirical constraints over a cover of contexts, and these need not extend to one globally realizable interpretation | A78, after the owner's U29 pointer to this record's sheaf- contextuality readings, which the assistant said it "couldn't reopen". The manuscript §3: "an empirical model can remain well-defined even without a global hidden meaning state." | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-039](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-039.md) | Affective states can be properties of organized systems, characterized functionally as Frijda characterizes emotions, without implying subjective experience | The owner's move at U19–U20: an organization can be "angry" or "blind" as a system is "happy" in a stable orbit, mapped through Frijda. The line the will paper grew out of; set aside from it. | Deferred |
| [CLAIM-040](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-040.md) | Symmetry is a kind of invariance, and Noether's theorem is the point: invariance under transformation yields conservation | The owner at U37, quoting A102's "We do not require the structure to remain invariant": "symmetry is a kind of invariance, no? isn't that part of the point of Noether Theory?" Conceded at A105. Its Noether half was limited by the assistant in the same reply. | Active |
| [CLAIM-041](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-041.md) v2 | Context-sensitive interpretation, order effects and noncommuting framing operations do not by themselves establish nonclassical probability or quantum contextuality | Built over A21–A30, against the assistant's quantum move and then the owner's U13. The manuscript §9 states it. Its strongest ground, the record's [THEORY-013](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-013.md), is not cited in the manuscript. | Active |
| [CLAIM-042](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-042.md) | Levels organize observables, not objects: a communicative object is identified by the intersection of constraint regions picked out by observables at several levels | The owner's correction at U28, accepted by A75 as "a more fundamental formulation". The manuscript §2: observations "are not ordered into one strict ontological ladder", and "An identified communicative object may be represented by an intersection of such regions." | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-043](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-043.md) | Equal entropy does not entail equal relational structure, and a collection of local entropies need not characterize global compatibility | A86 §4. The manuscript §5 keeps its example: perfect correlation and perfect anticorrelation of two fair binary observables have equal entropy and reverse a fundamental relationship. | Active |
| [CLAIM-044](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-044.md) v2 | Ambiguity and contextuality are different phenomena: an ambiguous utterance admits several compatible global assignments, a contextual one admits none | A81 §3.4 and A82 §8.7, recovered. The manuscript keeps the contrast between context sensitivity and contextuality but not this one, between too many global readings and none. Dropped at C6 without critique. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-045](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-045.md) v2 | Architectural, dynamical and goal-directed accounts of distributed agency are complementary components of one account, each answering a different sub-question | The owner's correction at U7: the three views are "perfectly mutually compatible ... complementary components of one theory". The case that made the record want a `complements` relation. | Active |
| [CLAIM-046](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-046.md) | Different hidden-state models can produce identical observations, so pragmatic identity and fidelity should be defined on observable responses before any equivalence of internal states is assumed | The reason for the A39 restructure, made under U16: put "observational equivalence at the center of the formal theory". The manuscript is built on it: observables are operational, no latent coordinate is primitive, and latents are validated by prediction (§2–3). | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-047](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-047.md) | Possessing an action-generating regime is not the same as its being practically effective | The distinction the paper inherits from Frankfurt ([THEORY-120](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-120.md)) and generalizes beyond desires: the audit's "established foundation". | Active |
| [CLAIM-048](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-048.md) | Communication transports structures of observational constraints rather than invariant semantic objects, and translation, language-model reconstruction and conditional image generation are realizations of one process | Proposal v6 (A100, under U35), *Contextual Information Transport Across Language, Images, and Communicative Frames*. It made conditional generation "part of the central theory, not merely an application at the end". Revised at A102 with structuralism. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-036](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-036.md) |
| [CLAIM-049](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-049.md) | An adaptive interpreter's state includes its message, context knowledge and learned parameters, and learning can make some relational invariants more stable and others less | A110 §22, recovered, with its case: a translator familiar with an underworld genre conserves its affiliative humour, a naive one turns it into a moral statement. The manuscript keeps one sentence (§11) on adapted receivers. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-050](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-050.md) v3 | Fidelity is relative to the communicative decisions the receiver must make, and Blackwell's order of experiments makes that comparison precise | The manuscript's §5: rate–distortion makes coding cost explicit, Blackwell comparison (restricted to a family Q of communicative decision problems) makes fidelity operational, and decoder side information is a Wyner–Ziv problem. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-051](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-051.md) | Fidelity is always fidelity with respect to some selected structure: a specified family of communicative observations, interventions and permitted continuations | A35 §8, "Hofstadter's central insight at a more abstract level", and A40's "Revised central proposition". The manuscript makes fidelity relative to the receiver's decisions and states the choice of observables to be normative. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-050](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-050.md) |
| [CLAIM-052](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-052.md) v3 | Interpretive framing operations generally do not commute, and this can be investigated without committing to quantum probability | A30's H1, "pragmatic noncommutativity", what the owner's U13 claim became. Manuscript §9: order-dependent framing "is central to pragmatic reconstruction". | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-053](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-053.md) | The manuscript argues only that preserving the proposition is insufficient; that it is also unnecessary rests on an argument the exchange gave and the manuscript leaves out | The crystallized argument §1 said "not sufficient"; the manuscript §1 says "neither necessary nor sufficient". Its own example shows only insufficiency. The case against necessity is A12's asymmetry and A24's compensation hypothesis, neither in the manuscript. | Active |
| [CLAIM-054](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-054.md) | A translation can preserve interpretive content while failing as a social act: interpretive and performative fidelity come apart | A35 §7, recovered. Last present at A35; the distinction is not named again in A38–A40, which speak of social constitution and joint states instead, and "performative" does not occur in the manuscript. Apparently dropped by inadvertence, never argued against. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-055](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-055.md) | The pragmatic identity of an utterance is partly constituted by the context-dependent transformations it induces, so pragmatic fidelity is an approximate structure-preserving correspondence between source and target dynamics | The thesis of the U14 replies (A33–A35), which shifted the invariant from attributes to transformations. Revised at A40 into an observation-relative form, and replaced in the manuscript by the observables thesis. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-083](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-083.md) |
| [CLAIM-056](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-056.md) v2 | Local transport errors propagate through later reconstructions according to the dynamics of those reconstructions, so the same local error can be damped, accumulated or amplified, and local similarity can coexist with large global drift | A50 §2–3, kept nearly verbatim in outline v4 and the manuscript §8. The manuscript later restricts the expansive regime: for stochastic kernels in total variation, contraction coefficients do not exceed one. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-057](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-057.md) | Precedence, jurisdiction and suspension can vary independently | Shown by the classification variants of Northstar (Sections 5.3–5.5): a formally absolute rule can be weak because another process controls entry into its domain. | Active |
| [CLAIM-058](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-058.md) v2 | Context-conditioned language models give a controlled computational setting in which the theory's frame, order and transport effects can be tested before human studies | A45 §8: "Put the three LLM experiments before the human-participant studies, positioning the former as controlled computational tests and the latter as external validation." The conclusion of the U17 analogy, limited by the circularity objection. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-059](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-059.md) v2 | Repeated reconstruction chains fall into different regimes under conditions assigned in advance, contracting toward conventions, accumulating neutrally or amplifying, and amplification needs a metric other than total variation, an enlarged state or state-dependent dynamics | A50 §3, A52 §5.3 and the crystallized argument §19, recovered. The manuscript and C7 Appendix B keep the corrected bound and the remark that κ ≤ 1 in total variation; the taxonomy and its third experiment's test were dropped. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-060](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-060.md) | An encompassing organization's success does not establish the well-being, viability or autonomy of its constituents — and the converse holds too | The owner's thread at U37 and U39: corporations trample human wellbeing though made of humans; and cancer, autoimmunity and ecosystem collapse run the other way. The audit's "strong negative argument" — believed, and not in the paper, which leaves cross-scale relations to future work. | Active |
| [CLAIM-061](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-061.md) | Renderings that share a loose situation model can differ in footing and illocutionary force, so preserving the proposition is neither necessary nor sufficient for preserving the communicative event | Manuscript §1, and the Abstract's "retain its topic while reversing the speaker's stance, and ... change almost every word while preserving the interpersonal relation". The descendant of the owner's frame thesis ([CLAIM-006](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-006.md)) without its ranking; the central thesis ([CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md)) rests on it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-062](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-062.md) | That every regulator with a higher-order attractor counts as having a will is acceptable once agency is kept apart from phenomenal experience | The owner's reply at U34: minimal wills for simple systems are no reductio, and corporations can coherently act freely or have their hand forced. Superseded when the paper chose to suppose its agent instead. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-080](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-080.md) |
| [CLAIM-063](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-063.md) v2 | A transport that approximately intertwines each framing operation approximately preserves their commutator, so the degree to which interpretive perspectives interfere can itself be part of what a translation preserves | A34 §4, made approximate at A40 §6.4 and stated in the manuscript §9 as the commutator-distortion identity. | [CLAIM-064](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-064.md) | A translation reconstructs not only what is said but the situation of its saying | A12's one-line thesis, recovered. | [CLAIM-065](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-065.md) | Pragmatic fidelity is approximate preservation of the ways an utterance conditions, transforms and constrains later interpretation and interaction, across transient framing and learned interpretive dispositions | The thesis of the U17 replies and outline v3 (A45 §10, A48), adding learned dispositions and language models to A40's observation-relative thesis. | [CLAIM-066](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-066.md) | Kirby, Cornish and Smith show structure emerging under transmission with an experimenter's filter, not filtering by measured inductive biases, and Griffiths and Kalish's convergence to the prior holds only for sampling learners | From the readings of Griffiths and Kalish ([NOTE-599](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-599.md)) and Kirby, Cornish and Smith ([NOTE-596](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-596.md), Skimmed). | [CLAIM-067](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-067.md) | If a transport exactly intertwines a generating set of communicative operations, it preserves every trajectory composed from them | A49's "compositional preservation theorem", proposed as the paper's organizing result. | [CLAIM-068](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-068.md) | Repeated reconstruction is attraction toward conventional regions of communicative possibility: pragmatic stances, not only lexicon and grammar, can be stabilized by transmission | A50 §3 and §6, A52 §5.5 and §7.4–7.5, recovered. | [CLAIM-069](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-069.md) | The 2016 Contextuality-by-Default criterion was being replaced by multimaximal couplings as it appeared, and the change matters for observables in more than two contexts | From the reading of Dzhafarov and Kujala 2016 ([NOTE-600](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-600.md)). | [CLAIM-070](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-070.md) | Transport induced by one global stochastic kernel carries a global extension of the source model to a global extension of the target model | Proposition II of A84, Proposition B2 of C6, the manuscript's Proposition 2. | [CLAIM-071](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-071.md) | Conditional generation shares a mathematical vocabulary with translation, not a mechanism: denoising time is not telephone-game time, and a generative failure is not a proof of sheaf contextuality | A97's own two limits, which the manuscript §7 keeps. | [CLAIM-072](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-072.md) | Saussure says purely differential only of signifier and signified taken apart; the sign as a whole is a positive fact, and signs stand in opposition | From the reading of the Cours ([NOTE-594](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-594.md)). | [CLAIM-073](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-073.md) | Demonstrating that interpretive framings do not commute establishes that the judgements cannot be described by one ordinary joint probability distribution | The owner at U13, answering A24. | [CLAIM-074](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-074.md) | Fidelity is relative to the interpreter's learning history: a frame given in context is transient while an adaptation to it persists, so fidelity under a fixed interpreter, across interpreters and across states of adaptation are different quantities | A44 §4 and A48 §5.5, recovered. | [CLAIM-075](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-075.md) | Viability does not establish what ought to persist | Sections 6.10 and 7.3: "An unjust institution may be highly viable." Viability licenses conditional functional judgment only. | Active |
| [CLAIM-076](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-076.md) | Cross-modal chains expose reconstruction because media differ in expressive affordances, and each interpreter, such as a captioner, may add motives and relations that were not present | A110 §21, recovered. | [CLAIM-077](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-077.md) v2 | A transport is admissible only if it keeps specified observable distinctions apart, which excludes trivial collapse by construction | A49's answer to the collapse objection, recovered: d_O(s_1, s_2) > δ implies d_O(Φ(s_1), Φ(s_2)) > δ′. | [CLAIM-078](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-078.md) v2 | The theory succeeds if its observational and decision measures predict changes of communicative identity that text similarity misses and follow its compositional predictions across reconstructions; it needs neither quantum contextuality nor exact Noether laws | A110 §X's success criterion and its closing statement of what remains to be shown, recovered. | [CLAIM-079](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-079.md) | Stability does not entail viability: a system can occupy a stable regime while losing access to trajectories that keep satisfying its constraints | Applied from viability theory ([THEORY-134](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-134.md)) and the stability/resilience distinction ([THEORY-132](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-132.md)) (Section 6.3). | Active |

| [CLAIM-081](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-081.md) v2 | Retaining information about the ancestor and drifting in the population's distribution are different profiles: a chain can forget where it started while staying far from the original, so convergence is not preservation and transmission is not degradation | C1 Appendix D's two profiles and E1's two forgettings (A59). | [CLAIM-086](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-086.md) v2 | A constraint may govern conduct without governing the conditions under which it remains authoritative | The structural lesson the paper takes from Fraenkel (Section 5.6), with two independent lines of support: the historical analogy and the constructed Northstar case. | [CLAIM-087](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-087.md) v3 | Cross-modal reconstruction often improves a receiver's access to an implicit communicative relation beyond the text-only baseline, as when posture or expression conveys solidarity better than the source text's literal content, though a rendering made without the situation is a garbling and cannot improve what an ideal receiver could do | A97 §6 and A100 §11.10, recovered. | [CLAIM-090](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-090.md) v2 | Serial transmission is reconstructive and filtered by the transmitters' inductive biases, so a stable endpoint can be far from the ancestral utterance while remaining interpretable | The manuscript's §8, citing Bartlett, Griffiths and Kalish, and Kirby, Cornish and Smith together as showing the same thing. | [CLAIM-091](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-091.md) | A translation can reach pragmatic equivalence by compensating for changed social coordinates with changed linguistic ones, so lexical infidelity can be required for pragmatic fidelity | A24's compensation hypothesis, recovered. | [CLAIM-093](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-093.md) | Not every invariance of a stochastic or communicative process implies a Noether conservation law: the symmetry principle can be borrowed, a conservation analogue has to be derived | A105, limiting the owner's U37 appeal to Noether in the same reply that conceded the rest. | [CLAIM-099](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-099.md) | Maintaining a regime of effective action and maintaining the capacity to govern among regimes are not the same achievement | The paper's main result (Section 6.11), concluded by [ARG-007](../../record/arguments.d/ARG-007.md) from [CLAIM-079](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-079.md) and [CLAIM-025](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-025.md). | [CLAIM-101](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-101.md) | Translation is a context-conditioned transport process that preserves selected structures of communicative meaning to varying degrees while introducing distortions that may accumulate, attenuate or amplify under composition | A50 §7 and outline v4 (A52), after the owner's U20 objection. | [CLAIM-106](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-106.md) | Pragmatic fidelity is a graded, potentially asymmetric property of transport, not an equivalence relation; equivalence is its zero-distortion limit | The owner's objection at U20, accepted in full by A50 ("an important correction") and made the thesis of outline v4. | [CLAIM-107](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-107.md) v3 | Under Markov reconstruction kernels, an observable's expectation is invariant when the observable is harmonic (K f = f), and drifts by at most Σ ε_i when ‖K_i f − f‖∞ ≤ ε_i; on a finite irreducible chain only constants are harmonic | The manuscript's §4, citing Baez and Fong. | [CLAIM-108](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-108.md) | Communicative identity is characterized by the observational structures and relations invariant or equivariant under specified classes of transformation, and fidelity by their preservation, deformation or loss | A105's thesis after the owner's U37 objection, with the title question restated: "What are the invariants of communicative transformation, which classes of transformation preserve them, and what happens when those symmetries are broken?" | [CLAIM-109](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-109.md) | In translation the pragmatic frame does not merely select a description of meaning but participates in determining which meaning becomes actual | A21's "preferred formulation", reached by the quantum-cognition analogy. | [CLAIM-114](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-114.md) | Drift along a reconstruction chain can be marked by a pragmatic phase change, the first generation at which a different communicative configuration dominates, which per-step error does not show | A73, recovered: track local reconstruction error D_local(i) = W(q_(i-1), q_i) apart from cumulative departure D_global(i) = W(q_0, q_i), and look for the generation at which the configuration flips. Dropped when the latents were demoted at U28, without critique. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md) | What survives translation is the set of communicative distinctions and relations a receiving system can still reconstruct and act upon, not the wording or the proposition | The manuscript's central thesis (Abstract, §1, §12). It does not say every relation must survive, or that there is one privileged set of observables: the choice of observables and decision tasks is stated to be normative and contestable (§11). | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-116](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-116.md) | Regulation, even higher-order regulation, does not establish a will | The boundary the paper draws against its own most-repeated objection: thermostats, adaptive controllers and organizationally closed systems regulate without warranting an agent. | Active |
| [CLAIM-117](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-117.md) v2 | Pragmatic fidelity requires preserving the dynamics of interpretation, not only final judgements: a good transport approximately intertwines source and target framing operations | A30's commuting diagram for pragmatic fidelity, developed in the manuscript §9 as intertwining defects and the commutator-distortion identity. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-118](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-118.md) v2 | Context and interpreter are separate sources of interpretive change: in-context conditioning changes the effective decoding context, adaptation changes the decoder, and improving the signal differs from improving the interpreter | A43's first point about the U17 proposal, developed at A44–A45 into three sources of change (evidence, transient computation, parameters). The manuscript §5 keeps it in two sentences. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-119](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-119.md) | Communicative categories form a concept lattice rather than a hierarchy: categories such as affectionate teasing and sarcastic condemnation share attributes and differ in a few social-relational constraints | A75 §7, recovered: formal concept analysis as the direct formalization of the owner's "objects are intersections of regions". Absent from A81, C6 and the manuscript without critique. The record holds Ganter and Wille unread. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-120](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-120.md) | Conditional generative systems realize stochastic transports between constrained distributions, and their compositional fidelity depends on preserving the compatibility and relational structure of constraints across media | A97's "strongest new proposition", made central by proposal v6 (A100). The manuscript's Abstract calls conditional diffusion and Schrödinger bridges "generative analogues", and §7 claims only a shared vocabulary; no turn up to U37 argues for the demotion. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-071](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-071.md) |
| [CLAIM-121](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-121.md) | A transport whose local kernels commute with restriction maps overlap-consistent source models to overlap-consistent target models | Proposition I of A84, Proposition B1 of C6, the manuscript's Proposition 1. It concerns marginal consistency, not global noncontextuality. | Active |
| [CLAIM-122](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-122.md) v2 | Will-organization is not one more higher-order preference: changing the conditions of efficacy changes behaviour while the competing preferences stay fixed | The reply to [CLAIM-089](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-089.md) (Section 3.5), by precommitment in a person and escalation authority in a firm. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-123](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-123.md) v4 | In conditional generation, adding scores composes two conditions exactly when the ratio p(y₁,y₂\|x)/(p(y₁\|x)p(y₂\|x)) does not vary with the noisy state x, which conditional independence of the conditions at that state is sufficient for but does not require | The manuscript's §7, citing Composable Diffusion, T2I-CompBench and Schrödinger bridges. The readings support it, and the manuscript is more careful than the paper it cites. | Active |
| [CLAIM-124](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-124.md) | Communicative objects are characterized through overlapping constraints on observables at partially ordered levels; translation transports these locally constrained structures, and repeated reconstruction changes both local interpretations and their global compatibility | The thesis of proposal v5 (A81–A83, under U30), which put observational structure first: "a global communicative object is no longer stipulated in advance." Revised at U31 into the selective preservation of decision-relevant information. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-026](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-026.md) |
| [CLAIM-125](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-125.md) v2 | Directed transport between empirical models whose covers change has two open problems: when it preserves decision-relevant information, and how it extends to signalling data | [CLAIM-100](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-100.md) narrowed. Simulations between empirical models already give directed stochastic transport between scenarios whose covers change, and settle when it preserves global compatibility. What is left open is decision-relevant information and signalling data. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-126](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-126.md) v2 | Model stitching tests whether a transport preserves decision-relevant information only in its reconstruction form: a map fitted to reproduce the target representation certifies one direction of Blackwell sufficiency, while task-loss stitching compares one decision problem through a fixed head, and a failure to stitch is no evidence against sufficiency | Proposed by the record on 2026-10-09 as an operational handle on the decision-relevant half of [CLAIM-125](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-125.md), and qualified by the reading of Bansal, Nakkiran & Barak before it was filed: only reconstruction-fitted stitching certifies sufficiency, and only within the map class. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-127](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-127.md) v3 | Conditioning on a communicative situation, intervening on it and intervening on what the interpreter is told are three different operations, so a framing intervention should index the empirical model, e_C^a = P(Y_C \| do(a)), not enter its cover as one more context | The owner's do() move at U41, formalized at A129 §1 and §5 and restated in the review relayed the same day. A129 keeps three operations apart: conditioning on the situation, intervening on it, and intervening on what the interpreter is told. It keeps one cover, with the empirical laws indexed by intervention. The link to signalling is the record's extrapolation, and A129's own graph gives a second route to it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-128](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-128.md) v2 | No single ranking of translations holds across tasks: where distortion profiles trade off, only a task's priorities rank them, and the theory of fidelity fixes no weighting | A129 §3, after the owner's U41 "agreed." to unifying the fidelity lineages, and restated at A151 §5, A178 Ch9.7 and A203 §22. A translation that keeps social footing and loses rhyme is not ranked above or below one that does the opposite until a task says which matters. It does not say how the components are measured, nor that they are independent. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-129](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-129.md) v2 | A factorization recovers only the structure visible in the relations it is fitted to, so a topic can survive a transformation that changes the communicative act, and a kind must be tested against probe families chosen for their bearing on the act | The assistant's, at A210 §5 after the owner's U58, and again at A214 §3–§4. It recurs from U56 (A203 §37) to U60 (A218 H3), so it gets a code. It is [CLAIM-004](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-004.md)'s distinction between changing the frame and changing the act, in a factorization, and [TERM-014](../../record/terms.d/TERM-014.md)'s relativity to a family of measurements, stated for factorizations. It does not say lexical structure is irrelevant to the act, or that a richer factorization cannot capture acts. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-130](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-130.md) v2 | Telling an interpreter about a communicative situation, do(I = a), is a different intervention from changing the situation, do(S = s), so the effect of a cue is evidence about interpretation under instruction, not about whether the act changed | The owner proposed Pearl's do() at U41, and A129 §1 drew the distinction. It recurs at A151, A191, A203, A214 and A218. [CLAIM-127](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-127.md) is about where the index sits in the formalism; this claim is about what an experiment manipulates. The sharp edge: in a vignette or rating study every manipulation is a do(I). | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-131](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-131.md) | A compatible family of sections of the event sheaf always glues, so where a communicative object lacks a global realization the obstruction lies in its supports or distributions, not in the event sheaf | The assistant at A125 §2 and A129 §2, after the owner's U41 "double click on this". It is the standard sheaf property of E(U) = ∏ O_x (Abramsky and Brandenburger). Granted, because it is elementary; it is used against [TERM-002](../../record/terms.d/TERM-002.md)'s wording, not against [CLAIM-038](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-038.md)'s thesis, which version 2 restates to agree with it. | Active |
| [CLAIM-132](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-132.md) | Characters classify finite-dimensional representations of a finite or compact group up to isomorphism only over a field of characteristic zero; they forget the isomorphisms, fix neither the group nor a permutation action, and classify kinds, not tokens or positions | Standard representation theory, which the exchange states correctly with its main hypotheses (A194 §1, A198 §§2 and 4, A203 §§5–6, A218 §3.2). This entry adds the limits the exchange left out, and the exact link to Yoneda: a character is a counts-only relational profile, complete because the category is semisimple. Granted, because it bounds the owner's analogy: priority for classifying kinds up to isomorphism, in the most favourable setting, and no further. | Active |
| [CLAIM-133](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-133.md) | Communicative identity is relational continuity under admissible transformation: translation transports relationally organized observational structure, correspondence fixes what can be compared, admissibility which transformations count, and fidelity which communicatively consequential distinctions remain recoverable | The thesis of the assistant's two consolidations after the U40–U42 review: the state of the argument at U43 (A151) and proposal outline v7 at U49 (A178). The owner asked for "the current state of our argument" and for the proposal to be updated "to account for our progress", and argued no change. A151 also brings back the identity clause of Superseded [CLAIM-108](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-108.md). Filed so that the change of thesis is visible, as [CLAIM-017](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-017.md) was. It does not replace [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md), which no draft has given up, and the October thesis replaced it in turn. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md) |
| [CLAIM-134](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-134.md) v2 | A non-eliminative structural realism that keeps relata but denies them any intrinsic identity-fixing feature is the position Chakravartty's dilemma targets, and granting numerical plurality without relational differentiation, as A194 and A198 do, concedes a non-relational ground of identity | The record's objection to the owner's U54 individuation thesis. No one raised it in the exchange. Chakravartty's fn. 9 names Esfeld and Lam, the position A194 calls "close" ([NOTE-149](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-149.md)). Leitgeb and Ladyman's edgeless two-node graph is the hard case the exchange's examples avoid. The owner's character move answers the dilemma for kinds and not for identity. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-135](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-135.md) | Recovering latent structure from relational data is three achievements, reconstruction, identification up to stated ambiguities, and constitution of a communicative kind, and identifiability theory reaches the second at most | The assistant's, at A207 §5 (three profiles) and A214 §1 (three problems, two warrants), kept as A218 §5's five objects of inquiry. A214 asked for it as a THEORY; it is the record's own distinction, so it is a claim. It answers [QUESTION-001](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-001.md) in part, by naming a failure that question did not. It does not say identified factors are never kinds, or that the second step is open. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-136](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-136.md) | Communicative acts and their participants exist as relata but have no identity beyond what their relations differentiate: non-eliminative structural realism without primitive identity | The owner's U54 position, its second part: "Individuated objects exist, but only in so far as they are differentiated by their relata." Filed scoped to communicative acts and their participants. The assistant placed it "close to" Esfeld and Lam (A194), then weakened it without saying so, from "only in so far as differentiated" to numerical plurality without differentiation (A194 §5, A198 §3, A203 §2). The owner has not chosen between the two versions. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-137](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-137.md) | A communicative kind is identified by an invariant that does not depend on any one realization, as a group character identifies a representation up to isomorphism, so that invariant has classificatory priority over any particular realization | The owner's own position at U54, its first part: "Character's are "more essential" than the representations that are comprised of them." It is the first owner-stated answer to [QUESTION-021](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-021.md) that takes neither horn. The assistant kept "more essential" in the sense of classificatory priority (A194 §1, A198 §I). A218 weakens it further, without argument, to "may be better guides to its kind". | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-138](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-138.md) | The Newman objection is the collapse objection at the level of ontology, and the remedy offered for both, independently specified relations, concedes that relational structure alone does not fix communicative identity | The exchange drew the parallel itself, four times (A187 §6, A191 §10, A203 §19, A218 Ch2.10). What it did not draw is the cost. The standard answer to Newman adds non-structural input, and so does admissibility, which sits uneasily with the claim that relations constitute identity. The objection presses on the owner's individuation thesis only if its relations are read extensionally, and it does not touch the "partly" of relational constitution. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md) | A communicative kind's identity is grounded in its relational organization rather than in any expression or encoding; statistical methods recover aspects of that organization from restricted observations, translation transports it, and fidelity is which independently identifiable, decision-relevant relational distinctions persist | The thesis of the assistant's second crystallized argument (A203, at U56), of A214 (U59) and of the October proposal and outline (A218, U60). The owner asked to "layout the full argument", to "tie it all together" and to "generate a new proposal and outline", and argued none of it. Unlike [CLAIM-017](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-017.md) → [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md), the change of question was announced (A203 X); the change of criterion was not. A218 replaces the receiver with the analyst's identifiability ([CLAIM-140](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-140.md)). Filed scoped to communicative kinds. No draft carries it, so it complements the manuscript's thesis rather than superseding it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-140](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-140.md) | The October thesis replaces what a receiver can reconstruct with what the analyst's probe system can identify as the test of what survives, and no turn argues the change | [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md) makes survival relative to "a receiving system". The October thesis (A218, U60) makes it relative to "independently identifiable" distinctions, and A218 defines identifiability on the analyst's probe system. The receiver is left in one sentence of A218 §8 and in Ch13.5. Filed by the record's reading of A218; no turn of the exchange says it. It objects to [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md) as stated, not to its truth, and it is granted because it is a comparison of texts. | Active |
| [CLAIM-141](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-141.md) | Causal-loop consistency and sheaf contextuality are both local-to-global problems, but passing to distributions affects them oppositely: a loop with no deterministic solution has a distributional fixed point, while a model with no global section in its supports has no global distribution either | A161, after the owner's U46, sharpened. A161 saw that a causal loop and a contextual model are "not the same", but not that they run in opposite directions under passage to distributions, or why. Its parity pattern is [LIT-278](../../record/literature.d/LIT-278.md)'s identification of Liar cycles with contextual support tables, which the record holds and has read ([NOTE-251](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-251.md)). A173 and A178 kept the three kinds of global consistency apart; A218 Ch10.9 keeps the feedback case. | Active |
| [CLAIM-142](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-142.md) v2 | Two communicative realizations can agree on every probe in a restricted family without being structurally identical, so observational equivalence is relative to the probes, and only independently justified probes evaluated on held-out cases are evidence that structure was preserved | It began at the owner's U45 pointer to Yoneda, and A158 §5 gave it its first form. A173, A178 §3.2, A184 §2, A203 §§8 and 14, A214 §3 and A218 Ch7 restate it; A203 calls it "our central epistemic distinction". The mathematical half is elementary and exact. The substantive half is the realist commitment that communicative identity is not exhausted by any probe profile. It makes [TERM-014](../../record/terms.d/TERM-014.md)'s equivalence class probe-relative, and puts a realist reading over [CLAIM-046](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-046.md)'s methodological ordering. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-143](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-143.md) | Local descriptions that disagree are not thereby incompatible: whether there is an obstruction can be assessed only after the correspondence between their observations is fixed and independently validated | A164, after the owner's U47, restated by A173, A178, A203, A214 and A218. Three classes of disagreement (coordinate difference, structural transformation, global obstruction) and an order of tests: fix and validate the correspondence, then ask whether the descriptions extend jointly. A173 proposed it as a design principle; it is a thesis of the line. The relativity example motivates it and is not evidence for it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-144](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-144.md) | Inertial observers who disagree about temporal order differ in coordinates, not in a failure to glue: only spacelike-separated events change order between frames, and frames are global charts related by a group action | A164's correction of the owner's U47, which is standard special relativity, with two repairs. A164 left out the reading of the owner's scenario whose answer is invariant (two passings of one marker). And it described frames as patches glued by a cocycle, which they are not: each inertial frame charts all of spacetime, and the frames are related by the Poincaré group. | Active |
| [CLAIM-145](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-145.md) | Trope analysis of this kind is Propp's and Lévi-Strauss's, and trope annotations are written to fit their examples, so agreement with TV Tropes labels cannot by itself validate a structural account | The record's objection to [CLAIM-147](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-147.md); no one raised it in the exchange. A155 presents TV Tropes as "the clearest large-scale empirical setting" for the structuralist side of the theory and names neither Propp nor Lévi-Strauss, whom the record reads as the direct precursors. The circularity half recurs in the exchange's own controls (A178 Study B and Ch12.9), and A218 drops the control. The prior-art half is close to granted. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-146](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-146.md) | Recovering latent relational structure from an interaction matrix, with no privileged latent coordinates, is decades-old prior art, so the manuscript's contribution cannot be that recovery | The owner's prior-art objection at U58 to A207's closing framing, conceded by A210 in its first sentence and extended by A214 to identifiability and cross-lingual alignment. It is the line's third in-band prior-art retraction, after [CLAIM-019](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-019.md) and [CLAIM-100](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-100.md) → [CLAIM-125](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-125.md). The concession holds for structure. It does not hold for individual topics, which are recovered only by privileging the nonnegative representation, and uniquely only under separability or anchor-word conditions. | Active |
| [CLAIM-147](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-147.md) | A trope is a family of observational profiles joined by admissible transformations around a core of preserved relations, so trope instances can separate structural preservation from pragmatic preservation | A155, at U44, after the owner proposed TV Tropes. A trope is taken to be neither an invariant token pattern nor a semantic label, but a family of observational profiles related by recasting, adaptation, inversion, subversion and parody. Comparing such families tests whether relational measures track communicative identity where surface similarity fails. A178 made it Study B; A191 §8 gave it five manipulations. The record objects that the analysis is Propp's and Lévi-Strauss's, and that the catalogue cannot validate it ([CLAIM-145](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-145.md)). | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-148](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-148.md) v2 | A matrix factorization is identified only up to an invertible change of latent coordinates, which weight decay narrows to rotations and nonnegativity to rescalings and permutations plus a data-dependent remainder; such a gauge is not a symmetry of the relational system, and automorphisms of the data act on the factors only when the factorization is identifiable | Drawn by the assistant at A207 §3, made explicit at A214 §6 and kept in A218 (§4.2, Ch5.4–5.9). Granted, because it is elementary, and the record already held its mathematics. The record adds the full list of invariants, the data-dependent freedom of nonnegative factorization, and why identifiability must come before automorphisms and characters. It does not say latent axes are meaningless, or that observed data have non-trivial automorphisms. | Active |
| [CLAIM-149](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-149.md) | A probe family that supplies a transformation's training signal cannot also certify what the transformation preserves: preservation must be shown under probes that were not used to train it | The owner set the frame at U51 ("a useful case study ... the projected GAN work"); A184 §§3–4 argued it, with its Experiment C and closing maxim, and A203 §30 and A218 Study 6 keep it. It carries [CLAIM-011](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-011.md)'s remedy from correspondences to training objectives. Kynkäänniemi et al. ([CASE-041](../../record/cases.d/CASE-041.md)) is a documented instance, which the exchange did not know of. One case, in image generation, so Proposed. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-150](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-150.md) | Identification is not constitution: neither the Yoneda lemma, nor the invariance of a character, nor predictive or interventional success shows that relations constitute what a communicative act is, because an ontology of intrinsically individuated objects with relational properties can reproduce the same predictions | The exchange's own guard, stated six times (A187, A191 §4, A198 §VI, A203 §35, A214 §13, A218 §13). The record adds the stronger, formal form from [THEORY-032](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-032.md): the Yoneda lemma presupposes the relata. That suits the non-eliminative choice and gives elimination no support. The ontological thesis needs its own conceptual argument; the mathematics and the experiments cannot supply it. | Active |
| [CLAIM-151](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-151.md) v2 | Which communicative act an utterance performs is partly constituted by its relations to participant roles, norms and expected uptake, so a transformation that changes those relations can change the act and not only its realization | The assistant at A187 §3, after the owner's U52 question about ontic structural realism, restated at A191 §1, A203 §3, A214 §13 and A218 §2. It is a thesis about social acts, not about fundamental ontology. Its "representational against constitutive transformation" is [CLAIM-004](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-004.md)'s distinction re-derived, so it rests there. Searle's status functions ([LIT-482](../../record/literature.d/LIT-482.md)) are the nearer held prior art, and "moderate OSR" is the wrong name for it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-152](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-152.md) v5 | Any individuation built on sufficiency is relative to the target it is sufficient for: fix the outcomes differently and a different partition wins, so no boundary drawn this way is universal | Dual-filed from [MOM-POS-001](https://github.com/dmarx/mathematics-of-meaning/blob/main/record/positions.d/POS-001.md) ([ADR-037](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-037.md)). A sufficient statistic is sufficient for a target Y, so every partition chosen as minimal sufficient inherits Y. MOM's example: fix the outcomes differently and "organism" wins instead of "animal". In this record it is the standing caution on every boundary criterion filed beside it ([CLAIM-154](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-154.md), [CLAIM-161](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-161.md)). It does not say boundaries are arbitrary, only that each is relative to a stated target. | Active |
| [CLAIM-153](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-153.md) | Failure of a self to hold together across contexts is a monodromy around a cycle of contexts: any two locally consistent contexts glue, and the minimal model of the obstruction needs three | Dual-filed from [MOM-CLM-165](https://github.com/dmarx/mathematics-of-meaning/blob/main/record/claims.d/CLM-165.md) ([ADR-037](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-037.md)). Model contexts (roles, personas, sub-agents) as a cover, and the offsets that reconcile neighbours as a cochain. A global section, one coherent self, exists exactly when the monodromy Φ around a cycle vanishes. Two contexts always glue, so compartmentalization needs a loop. The frustrated XY or Kuramoto energy finds the obstruction: its minimum is 0 exactly when Φ ≡ 0. Abelian case only; re-checked here. | Active |
| [CLAIM-154](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-154.md) v2 | A candidate structure is worth naming as a unit when its effect-to-noise ratio outruns the Occam cost of keeping it: reify when d'² exceeds the log prior-to-posterior variance ratio | Dual-filed from [MOM-CLM-160](https://github.com/dmarx/mathematics-of-meaning/blob/main/record/claims.d/CLM-160.md) ([ADR-037](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-037.md)). By the Savage–Dickey ratio, ln BF(keep) = ½(d'² − Occam), so a direction, block or sector is real enough to name exactly when δ/ε > τ with τ² = ln(s²/σ²), the information the data added about it. In this record it prices the boundary tests for a combined form: a near-decomposable block ([LIT-905](../../record/literature.d/LIT-905.md)) earns a name when its gap beats its Occam factor. Re-checked here in the conjugate case. | Active |
| [CLAIM-155](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-155.md) | Willed agency is a kind between agency and selfhood: a control system whose behaviour is governed by a will-organization over several regimes is more than an agent and closer to a self, in the self-governing sense, without thereby being a subject | The owner's proposal of 2026-10-11, stated with the assistant. Agency can be had without will: a thermostat with its designers, or a training process. A control system that can properly be said to have a will is a narrower kind. It is the jurisdiction of a will-organization, and it is closer to a self in [ADR-024](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-024.md)'s self-governing sense, not in the subject sense. With [CLAIM-174](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-174.md) version 2, it answers the will paper's permissiveness objection on its merits. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-156](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-156.md) | Mindfulness integrates a population of interests by changing which of them may act, not by changing what they value: noticing an impulse as an impulse suspends the step from its momentary dominance to its control of action, and leaves the discount curves as they were | The record's own conjecture, made in answer to the owner's prompt of 2026-10-11. It places mindfulness among [CLAIM-027](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-027.md)'s conditions of efficacy (suspension, accessibility), so it is a second individual-scale instance of that thesis. The one direct study read as summarised ([LIT-906](../../record/literature.d/LIT-906.md)) points the other way, and the claim says so. It does not say mindfulness never changes valuation. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-157](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-157.md) v7 | Relative to a stated target, a combined form is one system while its joint dynamics are nearly decomposable with the form as a block whose gap beats the Occam factor, and it lasts as long as that block persists; the unit is the gap-isolated block, not any member | The record's proposed answer to [QUESTION-035](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-035.md), joining the dual-filed MOM results. Simon's near-decomposability says what to compare: interaction inside a candidate block against interaction across its edge. The gap theorem says the unit is the block, and the Occam factor says when the gap is big enough to name it. Persistence is how long the block keeps clearing the threshold. It answers the owner's "privileged nomenclature" for the dancing couple with a test. Proposed: nothing has been measured. One-way drive passes, rightly (CLAIM-167), and the direction of coupling gives the system's architecture. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-158](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-158.md) | Systems differ in whether "who drives?" has an answer at a given level of description, and the kinds form a spectrum: fully top-down, bidirectional, emergent from the whole, and between | The owner's thesis of 2026-10-11: "it's probably worth differentiating between systems where 'who drives?' has an answer wrt a particularly level of description or not. I think there's is probably a spectrum/gradient/distribution of kinds here." The record grounds it in the information decomposition ([LIT-025](../../record/literature.d/LIT-025.md)), whose downward-causation and causal-decoupling terms separate the kinds, and in proportional causation ([THEORY-133](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-133.md)), which makes a higher level the cause system by system. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-159](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-159.md) | The record's rival accounts of will are claims about drive profiles in different cells: Ainslie's interests are aggregate or emergent, the expected value of control is top-down, and will-organization is the maintained organization that governs how the drive profile changes | The assistant's reading, offered in chat on 2026-10-11 and filed at the owner's prompt. Ainslie's will has no governor: the person is "just the resultant" of bargaining interests, which is aggregate, or emergent if resolve is a property only the recursion has. The expected value of control posits a dedicated specifying system, which is top-down. The will paper's will-organization is the maintained organization of relations that decides which regime is effective, so it governs the drive profile's changes over time. That is the owner's point that "who drives" is not constant. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-160](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-160.md) | Data written for a potential future receiver has its intended meaning fixed by the write: even if it is never read, what it was meant to say was settled when it was stored | The owner's claim of 2026-10-11, in their words: "data is written with a potential future receiver in mind. even if the data is never read, its intended meaning was fixed by the write." It separates when identity is fixed (at the write, relative to a projected receiver) from when fidelity is measured (at a read, relative to an actual one), which the line had left unsettled. It does not say what a text means to a given reader is fixed. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-161](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-161.md) v2 | A system persists as the same self over an interval when a coherence measure is approximately conserved along its own trajectory, not merely matched by some map between its states | Dual-filed from [MOM-CLM-153](https://github.com/dmarx/mathematics-of-meaning/blob/main/record/claims.d/CLM-153.md) ([ADR-037](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-037.md)). The corpus's selfhood condition, that some map φ preserves a coherence score γ, is satisfied by any two systems with similar scores. Requiring the map to be the system's own evolution Φ_Δt makes selfhood approximate conservation of γ along the trajectory: γ is an approximate first integral, and the defect is a number. Proposed here because γ is unspecified. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-162](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-162.md) | Flow is intrapersonal synchronization: when an activity's immediate reward and its longer-run value coincide, no interest has to be overridden, and that is why focused action in flow is reported as effortless | The record's own conjecture, made in answer to the owner's prompt of 2026-10-11. It is grounded only weakly: the effort-cost account is a reading, and the flow literature is registered unread. It says that in flow there is nothing for will to do. It does not say flow is the best or the normal way to act as one. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-163](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-163.md) | "Synchronization" is an analogy without a mechanism: phase synchrony among coupled oscillators has a coupling, an order parameter and transitions, valuations that fail to cross have none of these, and the word adds nothing to "the curves do not cross" | The record's own anticipated objection to the owner's framing, and the kind [ADR-032](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-032.md) found most often real: same structure, not the same mechanism. Open. [TERM-050](../../record/terms.d/TERM-050.md) defines synchronization without dynamics, so the claims survive with the word replaced, but then the word is only a name. The body says what would turn the analogy into a mechanism. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-164](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-164.md) v3 | What a spectral gap makes definite is the subspace of the cluster it isolates, not a single direction: the gap picks the object, and the object is a vector only when its own gap is wide | Dual-filed from [MOM-CLM-144](https://github.com/dmarx/mathematics-of-meaning/blob/main/record/claims.d/CLM-144.md) ([ADR-037](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-037.md)). Under a perturbation δT, the subspace spanned by a cluster of singular values separated by a gap δ rotates by at most ‖δT‖/δ (Davis–Kahan), whatever happens to the vectors inside it. So the well-defined unit is the gap-isolated projector. Re-measured here. With [CLAIM-154](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-154.md) it is a criterion: the gap picks the object, and the Occam factor says whether the gap is big enough. | Active |
| [CLAIM-165](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-165.md) v2 | A combined form constituted by mutual entrainment is not thereby a self in the subject sense: two dancers in phase are one coordination, an autonomous process, and still two subjects | The record's own objection to [CLAIM-172](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-172.md), narrowed in version 2 after the owner's reply ([CLAIM-175](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-175.md)). It concedes that the dancing pair is a combined form and an autonomous process. It holds only that nothing in the coupling makes that form a subject, which [ADR-024](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-024.md) reserves "self" for. Open: whether that is the sense of "self" worth asking about is itself in question. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-166](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-166.md) v2 | A lower-level collective within the composite person, such as the gut microbiome, can at times drive higher-level behaviour and even experience: the routes exist and the effects are causal in rodents, while the human evidence is modest | The owner's thesis of 2026-10-11: "'who drives' isn't a constant. Considering myself as a multi-level agent, part of my composition is my microbiome ... that lower level collective is capable of influencing my higher level behavior and even phenomenological experience." The time claim is filed as the owner's. The microbiome claim is grounded in two reviews read the same day. The routes exist, and the effects are causal in rodents. The human evidence is modest: associations, small effects on subjective stress, and nulls. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-167](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-167.md) | One-way drive rightly passes the threshold criterion: a metronome and its musician, or a nerve and its muscle, form one system relative to a target that needs both, and the direction of coupling gives the system's internal architecture, not its membership | The owner's challenge to the open objection, 2026-10-11: "a metronome driving a musician, or a nerve driving a muscle, passes — and why shouldn't they?" Three reasons, set out by the assistant and accepted for filing by the owner. First, the objection repeats the coupling-constitution worry that [CLAIM-172](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-172.md) had already set aside: the criterion makes a new system, not parthood in the organism. Second, its intuitions are about agency, which [QUESTION-035](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-035.md) keeps apart. Third, whether a one-way driver is inside the block or an input is a choice relative to the target. Direction tells peers from hierarchy. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-168](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-168.md) | Identifying agents by their will-organization is circular: will-organization is defined for an agent already given, and the agent-versus-device comparison presupposes an identified system, so neither can produce the factorization it is meant to find | The record's anticipated objection to the owner's thesis. [TERM-023](../../record/terms.d/TERM-023.md) is "agent-indexed": a role in an agent already warranted. The will paper supposes its agent ([CLAIM-080](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-080.md)) so as not to face [CLAIM-010](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-010.md). Orseau et al.'s formal agent-device comparison likewise "assume[s] a clearly identified system". To identify agents by will-organization reverses an order of explanation that was chosen deliberately.
