Open: [CLAIM-174](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-174.md) replies that the test is run on candidate factors, not on agents. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-169](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-169.md) v2 | On Ainslie's account the population of interests is produced by desynchronized valuation, not given by a fixed architecture: an agent is many interests over a choice only as far as its valuations of that choice cross within the choice's horizon | The record's reading of [THEORY-067](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-067.md), made in answer to the owner's prompt of 2026-10-11. Interests are individuated by the delay at which each dominates, so where curves do not cross there is nothing to individuate them. The self's intertemporal multiplicity is then a variable, not a constant. It does not say that all multiplicity of self is intertemporal. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-170](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-170.md) v3 | The agents in a system are a factorization of it relative to a level of description and a target; agents in general are identified by adaptation, and willed agents are individuated by their will-organization: a willed agent is the jurisdiction of a will-organization | The owner's thesis of 2026-10-11, stated in the record's terms after discussion. It is a collaboration, so it is not a quotation: "the agents in a given system are how we factorize that system relative to a given level of description", and agents are "identified by their will (and/or will-organization)". In the discussion, the owner chose will-organization over operative will, in the form "jurisdiction of a will-organization". Operative will flickers with the drive profile; a will-organization is maintained. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-171](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-171.md) | A stored text means only what its readings make of it: the writer's projected receiver is one more reading, so an unread text has possible meanings and no fixed one | The record's anticipated reception-side objection to the owner's storage claim. Its nearest support in the line is [CLAIM-111](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-111.md), a text supporting a distribution over possible communicative situations. No reception theory is held or read. Open: the reply (the joke changed for the reader, not in what it was made to do) works only if the writer's frame is itself recoverable, and that is [QUESTION-031](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-031.md)'s problem. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-172](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-172.md) v8 | Mutual entrainment is a sufficient mark of a combined form, and marks it as one of peers: when each side adjusts the other's timing the pair carries a collective variable neither has alone; one-way drive also makes a combined form, with a hierarchical architecture | The record's reading in answer to the owner's prompt of 2026-10-11, "connect extended self to entrainment". Clark & Chalmers' marks of extension are availability marks, and the coupling-constitution objection says coupling does not entail parthood. Mutual entrainment sidesteps the objection rather than answering it: it individuates a new system by its collective variable. It does not cover a passive store like Otto's notebook, which is coupled across time by writing and reading, not by timing. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-173](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-173.md) v3 | The threshold criterion measures coupling strength, and coupling is not constitution: a metronome driving a musician, or a nerve driving a muscle, forms a strongly coupled block that clears the threshold without being one system | The record's anticipated objection to [CLAIM-157](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-157.md), and Adams & Aizawa's coupling-constitution objection ([LIT-455](../../record/literature.d/LIT-455.md)) aimed at the new criterion. Near-decomposability is about the size of couplings, not their direction. One-way drive can make a tightly coupled block. The entrainment claim excluded exactly this case. Rejected on the owner's challenge (CLAIM-167): they should pass, and direction gives a system's architecture, not its membership. | Rejected |
| [CLAIM-174](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-174.md) v4 | A candidate factor is an agent at a level when it is one system there, when a goal-directed description beats a device description by more than the Occam cost, and when its policy would adapt if its actions' consequences changed; it is a willed agent when, further, a will-organization over several regimes describes it better than none, and what passes that is the jurisdiction of a will-organization | The assistant's admission test for the owner's thesis, filed at the owner's request. It has three tests on a candidate factor, at a level and for a target. (1) System: it clears the threshold criterion. (2) Description: a model in which it governs its own regimes for goals beats a device model by more than the Occam cost (Orseau et al., priced as in [CLAIM-154](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-154.md)). (3) Adaptation: its governance would change if its actions' consequences changed (Kenton et al.). It answers the circularity objection by running on candidates whose boundaries come from (1), not from agency, and it answers the permissiveness objection without the will paper's supposition. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-175](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-175.md) v2 | A partner-dancing couple, while dancing, is one combined cognitive form on the extended-mind thesis's own terms, more clearly than Otto and his notebook, and merits a name of its own | The owner's reply of 2026-10-11 to the dancers objection, which they had not read: "under extended mind thesis, I'd argue that the pair, while dancing together, comprises a combined form that merits some privileged nomenclature of some kind". Clark & Chalmers endorse socially extended cognition, and the objection's own source calls the interaction an autonomous process. So the objection fails against the combined form and survives only as a question about selfhood. The name is not yet chosen. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-176](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-176.md) v3 | Recurrence is the trigger of persistence this record is built around: coding a thing the second time it turns up captures exactly the drift that defeats an argument, and keeps capture from flooding the record | The filing rule of [ADR-032](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-032.md) ("a thing gets a code the second time it turns up"), read as one trigger of persistence ([CLAIM-185](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-185.md)), and the one this record is built around. Narrowed to that on 2026-10-11, on the owner's reframing. The same threshold appears in three places in luria and was found independently each time. Proposed: the rule has been applied, but no other threshold has been compared with it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-177](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-177.md) | An argument developed with collaborators who keep no memory fails at identity, not at structure: concepts are redefined silently, objections and readings come back as though new, and the thesis changes without anyone arguing for the change | Stated as a finding in [ADR-032](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-032.md), from seven readers coding the will exchange, and found again in the What Survives Translation? exchange by the workbench reconstructions of 2026-10-09 and 2026-10-10. Filed on 2026-10-11 as the first premise of the manuscript. Two exchanges with one owner and one assistant family, so Proposed. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-178](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-178.md) | A manuscript should be a view over the argument and not its only copy: a draft written straight from an exchange drops commitments and changes its thesis without saying so, and both become visible only against a record where claims carry status and inclusion separately | Luria's distinction between sources and generated views, carried over from files to argument. The evidence is the two "crystallized arguments" of the What Survives Translation? exchange and the manuscripts drafted from them (workbench 2026/10/09/025539, 2026/10/10/040457). Proposed: the record is kept beside the manuscript, and nothing generates a manuscript from it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-179](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-179.md) v2 | When an agent does the filing, the cost of capturing argument falls on the party that would otherwise pay to re-derive it, which removes the disparity that design-rationale systems were abandoned over | The answer to the cautionary literature the owner filed before the argument schemes were built ([ADR-030](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-030.md)): design rationale goes uncaptured because whoever captures it is not whoever benefits. [ADR-032](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-032.md) noted that agent filing changes the risk from capture stopping to capture flooding. This claim states why. Proposed: the record has run for one week. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-180](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-180.md) v2 | Drift is retrieval failure outside a critical distance: a restatement close enough to an idea retrieves it, one that has drifted too far is taken for new, and a code bypasses retrieval by content altogether | Proposed by the assistant on 2026-10-11, when the owner raised sparse distributed memory and Hebbian learning after [CLAIM-182](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-182.md), and filed at the owner's request. It reads the drift of [CLAIM-177](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-177.md) through SDM's critical distance (read in [NOTE-242](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-242.md)) and Hopfield's basins of attraction (unread when filed; read 2026-10-14 in NOTE-819). The analogy is the claim; whether restatement distance predicts re-derivation has not been measured. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-181](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-181.md) | The record fixes the grammar an argument is expressed in, though not its shape: its closed vocabulary of schemes, roles and relations formalizes early, and a move that fits no CLAIM, TERM, ARG or declared relation is either forced into one or lost | Shipman and Marshall's objection to formal representation ([LIT-756](../../record/literature.d/LIT-756.md)), turned on this record, and narrowed on the owner's correction of 2026-10-11. As the assistant first put it ("a crystal takes the shape of its seed"), it was wrong about crystals. A seed templates the lattice, but the habit is set by growth conditions. The objection therefore reaches the grammar of the argument, and not which claims it comes to hold. Some evidence for it is already in the record: the flourishes it declined to code, and the literature roles that "recurred" but stay in prose ([ADR-032](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-032.md)). | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-182](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-182.md) | Storage is not anchoring, addressability is: a structure that is kept but cannot be pointed at part by part does not stop re-derivation, and the same content becomes an anchor once its parts get addresses | An answer to [QUESTION-038](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-038.md), proposed by the assistant on 2026-10-11 and filed at the owner's request. The evidence is in the record already: the two shared transcripts persisted throughout, and ideas were re-derived inside them anyway. Their moves became citable only when the workbench reconstructions gave each turn a label. Two exchanges, and the comparison is not controlled, so Proposed. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-183](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-183.md) | Generated views of the record, fed back into the exchange, become instruments of the argument: they show where its terms evolved faster than its formalism | From U40 of the translation exchange, the first time a generated view went back into the conversation (workbench 2026/10/10/040455). The assistant read the term-revisions chain as evidence about the manuscript, and made five moves in reply. One of them was "Nucleation itself is a case study". One instance, so Proposed. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-184](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-184.md) | Anchors entrench: a structure that persists early can lock in its framing, so that later structure grows on it and alternatives become hard to reach, and retirement by status is the record's remedy only if superseded anchors stop attracting new structure | The cost side of the owner's reframing ([CLAIM-185](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-185.md)), proposed by the assistant on 2026-10-11 and filed at the owner's request. If persistence is what lets structure grow, it also lets early framings grow structure that is costly to undo. Grounded on Dunitz & Bernstein (unread) and Ostwald (read). The test: do Superseded entries keep drawing new citations? | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-185](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-185.md) v2 | The nucleation threshold of an argument is whatever makes a structure persist: a persistent structure is available as an anchor, and more complex structure can evolve only on anchors | The owner's reframing of 2026-10-11, after two readings showed that no physical nucleation threshold is a count. The threshold is not recurrence as such but whatever triggers persistence. Recurrence is the trigger this record is built around ([CLAIM-176](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-176.md)), and there are others. The readings fit the reframing: a physical nucleus is defined by persistence, and seeds that do not persist lose their power. Proposed: the generalization is stated, not tested. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-186](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-186.md) v3 | How fast an argument grows sets how it grows: drift tracks how fast new material arrives relative to how fast it is anchored, so a fast, prolific exchange branches, as a crystal grown far from equilibrium does, and a project memory stabilizes an argument by keeping anchoring up with arrival | Proposed by the assistant on 2026-10-11, while correcting its own crystal analogy ([CLAIM-181](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-181.md)), and filed at the owner's request rather than on a second occurrence. The analogy is to morphological instability. Near equilibrium a growing crystal faces over, and far from it the faces branch. The evidence so far is anecdotal. The fastest stretch of the translation exchange carried two moves of thesis, and drift has not been measured against pace. There is no slow exchange to compare it with, so the claim is Proposed and untested. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-187](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-187.md) | Whether two systems compute the same way can be tested by quotienting the full admissible symmetry group and measuring what remains: a residual barrier marks a different mechanism, and a feature with no counterpart blocks the comparison | The record's one fully measured case of "factor out the admissible symmetries, then test what survives", which the pragmatic-transport line's questions about invariants under admissible transformation ([QUESTION-027](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-027.md), [QUESTION-029](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-029.md), [QUESTION-036](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-036.md)) ask for in the abstract. From five mode-connectivity THEORYs held without links to the line. The carry-over from trained networks to communicative kinds is the filer's, and is the claim's weak point. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-188](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-188.md) v2 | Where the model with a candidate structure is singular at the parameters that switch the candidate off, with real log canonical threshold λ below d/2, the reification rule's Gaussian Occam term overcharges keeping the candidate by a term that grows as (d/2 − λ) log n, so the rule is exact only where that model stays regular | A narrowing of [CLAIM-154](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-154.md), filed on 2026-10-11 from a synthesis review that found the record already held the readings that limit it. [CLAIM-154](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-154.md)'s rule prices a candidate with a Gaussian Occam factor, which is exact in the conjugate case it was re-checked in. The candidates it is meant for (blocks, sectors, couplings with hidden structure) are plausibly singular, where [THEORY-087](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-087.md) puts the penalty at λ log n, not (d/2) log n, and [THEORY-079](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-079.md) says the Laplace form errs by a term that grows with log n. Both THEORYs are Proposed, so this is too. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-189](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-189.md) | Fidelity is the wrong measure of a developing argument: maturing drops and changes things legitimately, so what a record can secure is traceability, change that is announced and located, not preservation | The counter to [CLAIM-191](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-191.md), filed with it. The transport line measures fidelity, and [CLAIM-034](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-034.md) already says fidelity is one goal among several. A developing argument aims at maturing, and [TERM-052](../../record/terms.d/TERM-052.md) says maturing drops things legitimately. A fidelity profile would score legitimate dissolution as distortion. What the record secures is that change is announced and located: traceability. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-190](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-190.md) | The anchoring measures are computed in the grammar that shapes the argument, so by themselves they cannot certify that drift fell: drift must also be judged under standards the record did not supply | An evaluation counter to the persistence and growth-rate claims, distinct from [CLAIM-181](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-181.md). [CLAIM-181](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-181.md) says the record shapes the argument. This says it also shapes the measures. Inbound references per code and turns per anchor are counted in the record's own grammar, so by [CLAIM-149](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-149.md) they cannot also certify that what they encourage reduced drift. Proposed from the synthesis review. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-191](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-191.md) | Restating an argument across memoryless sessions is iterated transport with only a weak channel back to the source: without anchors, restatements drift toward the collaborators' own framings rather than at random, and a code restores continuity of transmission | The first claim joining the nucleating-argument and pragmatic-transport lines, which shared no edges. A sequence of memoryless sessions restating an argument is a chain of retellings, the telephone game of [CASE-015](../../record/cases.d/CASE-015.md). The transport line's results then predict that drift has a direction, toward the transmitters' prior ([THEORY-155](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-155.md), [CLAIM-090](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-090.md)), and that convergence is not preservation ([CLAIM-081](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-081.md)). A code supplies [CLAIM-002](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-002.md)'s continuity of transmission. Proposed from a synthesis review; the counter is [CLAIM-189](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-189.md). | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-192](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-192.md) v2 | A multi-session argument's thesis can move without any participant arguing for the move, which is a case of an organized whole's course coming apart from its members' intentions | Links the nucleating-argument line to the distributed-agency line. The translation exchange's thesis moved from [CLAIM-017](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-017.md) to [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md) to [CLAIM-133](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-133.md) with no turn arguing either move ([CLAIM-177](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-177.md)). [CLAIM-030](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-030.md) says an organized whole's operative intention can evolve apart from what its members intend. The owner, the assistant sessions and the record make up such a whole, and its thesis moved that way. [CLAIM-186](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-186.md)'s test is an intervention test of both. Proposed from the synthesis review; the owner's memory and veto are the main reason to doubt it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-193](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-193.md) v2 | A linear probe family can tell apart only realizations whose representations differ in normalized kernel, and kernels fix a representation only up to the symmetry of what is observed: any distinction beyond that is supplied by the analyst | A partial answer to [QUESTION-031](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-031.md) for the linear case, from four THEORYs the record held without linking them to the line. What a regularized linear readout decodes is a function of the normalized kernel ([THEORY-008](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-008.md)); kernels fix a representation only up to the symmetry of what is observed ([THEORY-002](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-002.md)); an inner product beyond that is a stipulation ([THEORY-018](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-018.md)); and spectral degeneracy does not identify the symmetry ([THEORY-019](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-019.md)). Proved results for learned representations; the carry-over to communicative realizations is the filer's. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-194](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-194.md) v2 | A gap or coupling criterion cannot tell a boundary a system produces from one imposed on it: a membrane the cell makes and a beam joining two pendulums pass alike, so the block test cannot by itself supply the individuality minimal agency needs | A counter to the block criterion and to the agency test's first condition, found by two separate reviews of the unlinked THEORYs in the third synthesis pass. It grants the target-relativity [CLAIM-152](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-152.md) states. Its point is different: with the target fixed, a near-decomposability gap is a statistic of coupling, and coupling is the same whether the system made the boundary or something else did. [THEORY-072](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-072.md) and [THEORY-071](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-071.md) place the line at production and self-distinction instead. Proposed: no case has been measured. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-195](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-195.md) | For an argument, the constraint set that persistence is relative to is fixed by an amendable decision, not discovered: the record stipulates what counts as the same claim persisting, which makes the choice explicit without making it non-arbitrary | Files the point the workbench of 2026-10-11 (214308) left as notes on [CLAIM-185](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-185.md) and [CLAIM-181](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-181.md). Viability is relative to a constraint set the theory does not supply ([THEORY-134](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-134.md)), and the will-organization line leaves "who fixes the constraint set" open ([CLAIM-079](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-079.md), [ARG-007](../../record/arguments.d/ARG-007.md)). For an argument the record fixes it by decision ([ADR-032](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-032.md) and its successors), so the set is explicit and amendable. That shows how one system fixes its set; it does not show the choice is non-arbitrary. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-196](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-196.md) | A person's roles in contexts that do not meet cannot contradict one another: role conflict needs an overlap, which segregating audiences prevents and collapsed contexts create | The MoM manuscript models a person's roles ("daughter", "teacher", "friend") as sections over family, professional and social contexts, with identity as their global section, failing when the roles contradict. Roles in contexts that do not meet glue ([CLAIM-197](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-197.md)), so contradiction between them is not yet a failure. Conflict needs an overlap: an occasion on which two audiences are present at once. People manage the cover. They segregate audiences, which keeps the contexts disjoint, and they script the occasions where audiences must meet. Broadcast and networked media collapse contexts, which creates overlaps the person did not choose (boyd 2008, after Meyrowitz 1985). | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-197](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-197.md) | Sections over contexts that do not overlap always glue, so a sense that differs between disjoint contexts is not a gluing failure: a failure needs an overlap carrying sections that disagree, and which families count as covers decides whether anything glues | Dual-filed from [MOM-CLM-088](https://github.com/dmarx/mathematics-of-meaning/blob/main/record/claims.d/CLM-088.md) ([ADR-037](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-037.md)). The sheaf condition says a family of sections that agree on overlaps glues to one global section. Where two contexts do not overlap, agreement is vacuous and they glue. So "tall", which takes one threshold for people and another for trees, is one global section with the comparison class part of the meaning, not a failure of gluing. A failure needs contexts that overlap and carry sections that disagree there. Which families count as covering is the piece that decides whether anything glues, and a sheaf picture of meaning that does not state it has decided nothing. | Active |
| [CLAIM-198](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-198.md) | Preference reversal is not yet disunity: interests that govern a choice at disjoint times glue, and a self is divided over a choice only on an overlap, which the agent's prediction of its own later valuation creates | Take an agent's interests as sections over contexts of choice, each governing the windows of time in which its valuation ranks first. If the windows do not overlap, the sections glue ([CLAIM-197](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-197.md)): someone who prefers the diet in the morning and the cake at night is a time-indexed preference, not a divided self. Division needs an overlap, a choice on which both interests bear at once. On Ainslie's account that overlap is made by recursive self-prediction: the present choice is valued as a forecast of the later ones, so the later interest's valuation enters the present context. That is where intertemporal bargaining happens, and it is the coupling [CLAIM-163](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-163.md) found missing. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-199](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-199.md) v2 | Blackwell's order bounds what a rendering makes possible for any receiver, and a given receiver's access is usable information relative to the rules it can apply, which a garbling can raise for that receiver but never above what the source makes possible | The record's reconciliation, on 2026-10-10, of [CLAIM-225](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-225.md). Both standards are kept, in different roles: Blackwell's order is the ceiling, and [TERM-034](../../record/terms.d/TERM-034.md)'s interpreter-relative fidelity ("relative to an interpreter and its available side information") is the receiver's. [CLAIM-087](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-087.md)'s improvement is a gain in usable information. The nearest held reading is [THEORY-176](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-176.md)'s "Its loss bounds r's best attainable risk for that problem from above", for restricted rules. The example is checkable; whether real receivers show such gains is a proposal. The usable-information literature was not held when it was filed; Xu et al.'s V-information is now read (note of 2026-10-21). It does not say which rule class real readers have. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-200](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-200.md) | The procedure-level fidelity thesis's title says observational fidelity is not defined for a single rendering of a single item, which is more than it shows: one source–target pair does not identify the procedure-level score, and a single rendering can still be judged, as a sample of its procedure or by another measure stated as such, as its own body says | The interlocutor's second refinement at U62 (A240), checked against [CLAIM-228](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-228.md)'s text and granted on 2026-10-10. Version 1's title ended "for a single rendering of a single item it is not defined"; its body says only that this measure does not judge a single rendering, and that one is judged as a sample of its procedure or by another measure. Granted because it can be checked by reading. Repaired in [CLAIM-228](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-228.md)'s version 2, which changes the title only. It does not touch the derivation or the defeat condition. | Active |
| [CLAIM-201](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-201.md) | Over a fixed scenario, a change of global compatibility at small local distortion needs contextual judgements, which the behavioural data read so far lack; the form of the headline those data leave open is a change of cover, in which a rendering makes some of the source's questions undecidable for its readers and decides others the source left open | The record's narrowing, on 2026-10-10, of [CLAIM-105](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-105.md) after [CLAIM-219](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-219.md). It concedes that the record's reading of [LIT-264](../../record/literature.d/LIT-264.md) finds no contextual behavioural data, so the fixed-scenario form of "Translation may preserve local meanings while changing the global structure of meaning" has no case but a constructed one. It shows why the fixed-scenario form is sharp and why it needs contextuality. It keeps the form the record already names: "Only a change of scenario can make it jump" ([CLAIM-105](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-105.md)), and manuscript §11's "A transport may preserve overlaps but change the available measurement cover". A proposal; its test is the discriminating study. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-202](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-202.md) | What the line adds to functional and relevance-theoretic accounts of translation is measured structure beyond a static pragmatic profile: the context-indexed profile, its response to later framings and its decision relevance predict receivers' recovery beyond the similarity of static stance and footing ratings | The record's reply, on 2026-10-10, to [CLAIM-233](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-233.md). [CLAIM-023](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-023.md) places the line's contribution in "a specified, testable account of which communicative distinctions a translation preserves", so the test of that contribution must be against the accounts that already hold the pragmatic point. This restores [CASE-005](../../record/cases.d/CASE-005.md)'s three-way selection (semantic, static pragmatic and trajectory similarity), which was gone by A52, with human receivers. A proposal, untested. It does not say the functionalist rivals are wrong. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-203](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-203.md) v2 | The only design for the October thesis builds its outcome in: the act is fixed by whoever writes the items, and the topic-only baseline is built to fail on the critical cells | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. [CASE-040](../../record/cases.d/CASE-040.md) "makes the act a manipulated factor rather than an outcome", so recovering the act recovers the item writer's intent, close to a manipulation check. Its held-out items are built to "break the familiar correlation", so a lexical topic model fails on them by construction. And its baseline is topic-only NMF, not the embedding or surface similarity of [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md)'s own defeat condition. Through [CLAIM-135](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-135.md) it reaches [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md)'s epistemic clause. [CASE-040](../../record/cases.d/CASE-040.md)'s "What it cannot show" covers neither point. It does not say the design is useless as a first pilot. | Active |
| [CLAIM-204](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-204.md) | A communicative kind's identity is grounded in its relational organization, for which competent participants' uptake is the standard; statistical methods recover aspects of it from restricted observations; translation transports it; and what persists is what the receivers can still recover, of which the analyst can certify the independently identifiable part | The record's restatement, on 2026-10-10, of [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md) after the audit's objections to it ([CLAIM-233](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-233.md), [CLAIM-232](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-232.md), [CLAIM-244](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-244.md), [CLAIM-206](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-206.md), [CLAIM-222](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-222.md), [CLAIM-246](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-246.md), [CLAIM-211](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-211.md)). It keeps the four clauses, adds [CLAIM-217](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-217.md)'s standard to the first and [CLAIM-223](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-223.md)'s reading to the fourth, and replaces the third defeat disjunct ("no relational structure beyond the observed response profiles is needed", which [CLAIM-206](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-206.md) showed is always met or undecidable) with an observable comparison on transfer. Filed to complement [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md); whether it supersedes it is the owner's decision. A proposal, not a finding. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-205](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-205.md) | A win over the static pragmatic profile shows that context matters, not that the transport formalism contributes: functional and relevance-theoretic accounts need not fix one context, so the rival the incremental thesis must beat is a strong contextual baseline of rich context-response profiles and supervised prediction | From the interlocutor's reading of PR [#121](https://github.com/dmarx/nucleation/issues/121) and its reconciliation, relayed by the owner on 2026-10-12. It accepts [CLAIM-202](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-202.md) as the line's principal empirical thesis and objects to its rival set. Open: no study has been run, and the strong baseline is not specified beyond the reading's table. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-206](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-206.md) | If a communicative kind can outrun every probe, human same-kind judgements, themselves one probe family, can neither confirm nor defeat the October thesis; if it cannot, the kind does in the tests only what an observational class does, which the record's term for a kind says it is not | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (structure lens, with one point from the empirical lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. The dilemma reaches the epistemic and fidelity clauses [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md) says the programme does test, not only the ontological clause its "What it does not say" sets aside. Two defeat conditions misfire under it: [CLAIM-142](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-142.md)'s is met trivially if the judges' verdicts count as a probe, and [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md)'s third disjunct is either always met or never decidable. It does not say kinds are unreal, and it is not [CLAIM-150](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-150.md)'s underdetermination of the ontology. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-207](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-207.md) | The transports of Propositions 1–2 and of D_obs act on judgement outcomes, not texts, and for any one source–target pair a constant kernel transports any overlap-consistent target, so they constrain a translation only once one kernel family is fixed across many utterances and derived from the text channel | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (formal lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. The manuscript's kernels K_C map readers' judgements to readers' judgements, while a translation is a channel on texts, T(v \| u), and [TERM-008](../../record/terms.d/TERM-008.md) records that the two levels are never formally related. For one source and any overlap-consistent target, the constant kernel makes D_obs zero and satisfies naturality. Granted, because it is elementary. It does not say [CLAIM-121](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-121.md) or [CLAIM-070](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-070.md) is false; it bears on what they are used to support. | Active |
| [CLAIM-208](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-208.md) v2 | The compositional predictions are upper bounds whose constants cannot be estimated from serial-reproduction data in a metric fixed beforehand, so the compositional half of the success criterion cannot fail | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. The recursion e_n ≤ (∏κ_j)e_0 + Σε_i∏κ_j is a theorem once its constants are the true ones, so a chain cannot depart from it; it can only show a constant was misestimated. A sensitivity κ_i belongs to a reteller's kernel and needs many retellings of each of several inputs at every generation, where a chain gives one. And [CLAIM-059](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-059.md) says amplification "needs a metric other than total variation" (now held as CLAIM-267), so the regime depends on a metric chosen afterwards. [CLAIM-078](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-078.md) records that the compositional half was dropped from the manuscript, not that it cannot fail as stated. It does not say error propagation is unimportant. | Active |
| [CLAIM-209](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-209.md) | Probes and statistical models recover only the distinctions identifiable from the relational evidence they are given, and a signature sufficient for a stated family of communicative decisions outside the observed probes can be identified when the full latent factorization is not | The assistant's second thesis at U62 (A240 T2), the positive answer to [QUESTION-027](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-027.md). It has now been asserted four times: A214 §7, A218 (RQ3 and the priority theorem target), A232 (§3.2 and F2) and A240. Filed with [QUESTION-027](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-027.md)'s scope: the unrestricted claim is answered by standard results, so its content is in decisions outside the observed probes. No signature is yet named. A proposal; the owner argued none of it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-210](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-210.md) | Sharing a kind and being a rendering are different relations: kind-sharing is symmetric and says what is to be preserved, while fidelity belongs to a transport from a given source, so an utterance of the same kind made independently of the source shares its kind and has no fidelity to it | The record's reply, on 2026-10-10, to [CLAIM-246](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-246.md). The October thesis puts fidelity in what persists "through those transformations", and [TERM-043](../../record/terms.d/TERM-043.md) makes fidelity the distortion profile of a transport. So the objection's independent utterance I shares the source's kind and has no fidelity to it, since no transport relates them; the translation T has both. Descent is a fact of provenance that the transport clause supplies, not something the kind criterion must recover. [CLAIM-002](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-002.md)'s continuity of transmission concerns the identity of a work, which [TERM-040](../../record/terms.d/TERM-040.md) keeps apart from the kind. Active, because it is read off the record's own texts. It does not say judges track descent. | Active |
| [CLAIM-211](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-211.md) | The manuscript's thesis and the October thesis give rival answers to what survives, which come apart in both directions on the cases of the receiver objection, yet the record links them by `complements` and scopes the October thesis to a question its fidelity clause does not stay inside | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (structure lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. [CLAIM-140](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-140.md) shows that the receiver's test and the analyst's identifiability come apart in both directions, and stops at "It does not say the October thesis is false". On those cases [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md) and [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md)'s fidelity clause give opposite verdicts on what survived, yet the two are joined by `complements`, which says they answer different parts of one question and do not compete, and [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md) answers only [QUESTION-029](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-029.md). Granted: it is a comparison of texts and of the record's relations. It does not say which criterion is right. | Active |
| [CLAIM-212](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-212.md) v2 | Fidelity as a report and fidelity as a re-performance are different decision targets: Blackwell's order compares a rendering with its source as evidence about the source's situation, which both readers decide about, while a rendering that re-performs the act in the target's situation is compared only through a correspondence of situations | The record's reply, on 2026-10-10, to [CLAIM-258](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-258.md), which it reconciles rather than refutes. In report fidelity the parameter is the source's situation, common to both readers, so Blackwell's order is defined; the objection's worked case then gets a precise verdict, incomparability, as [CLAIM-128](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-128.md) expects, and a context-aware translator's non-garbling is compared by the order and quantified by [CLAIM-259](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-259.md). The objection is right about re-performance, where [CLAIM-091](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-091.md)'s compensation lives: there a correspondence of situations is needed ([QUESTION-005](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-005.md)). For report tasks the task correspondence is the identity, which removes two of [CLAIM-218](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-218.md)'s points there. A proposal; it does not say which kind of fidelity literary translation aims at. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-213](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-213.md) | A proposed relational kind is a hypothesis about invariance across admissible changes of measurement and context, not an equivalence class under the measurements now available, so realism about kinds gains empirical content as extrapolation to probe families, populations and environments not used to construct them | From the interlocutor's reading of PR [#121](https://github.com/dmarx/nucleation/issues/121) and its reconciliation, relayed by the owner on 2026-10-12. The middle position it proposes between the realist reading of [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md)'s ontological clause and an operationalist one, which it would leave open as a tension rather than resolve. Proposed and not adopted. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-214](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-214.md) | The distribution-over-situations criterion compares each language's own listener posterior, so it needs no correspondence of alternative sets; that a literal rendering can fail it is the criterion detecting a lost implicature, and a target language may admit no rendering that passes | The record's reply, on 2026-10-10, to [CLAIM-236](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-236.md). [CLAIM-111](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-111.md)'s criterion is P_o(z \| c_o, u_o) ≈ P_t(τ_z(z) \| c_t, u_t). On the rational-speech-act model each posterior is computed with its own language's alternatives, so comparing the posteriors needs no map between the alternative sets. The objection's worked case is right, and it shows the criterion rejecting a rendering that keeps the literal content and loses a scalar implicature. Active, because it is read off the criterion's form and checked by arithmetic. It does not say the model is right or that the criterion applies to poems. | Active |
| [CLAIM-215](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-215.md) | Communicative kinds are repeatable organizations of relations among participants, norms, commitments and possible consequences, which concrete events realize without being one another, and which kind an event realizes is not exhausted by the intrinsic properties of the expression token | The assistant's first thesis at U62 (A240 T1; A232's first proposition and §2), which splits the ontological clause of the October thesis ([CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md)) out from the rest and narrows it. The owner asked how to update the theses and argued none of it. Its first two sentences are [TERM-040](../../record/terms.d/TERM-040.md)'s definition. Its third is weaker than [CLAIM-151](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-151.md): an intention-based account meets it too, so it does not tell relational constitution from that rival. The study cannot test it. A proposal. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-216](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-216.md) | A thesis that conjoins relational constitution, statistical recovery, transport and receivers' recovery is defeated whole by any one of its empirical findings, though its clauses can hold or fail apart, so the line is better put as separate theses, each with its own defeat condition, resting on one another only where one is a premise of another | The interlocutor's objection at U62 (A240) to the October thesis ([CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md)) and to the record's restatement of it ([CLAIM-204](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-204.md)), given as the reason for superseding [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md): "Separates ontology, statistical recovery, and empirical transport instead of making them rise or fall together". The record's replies of 2026-10-10 had already separated the receiver thesis and the incremental thesis; the restatement joined the four clauses again under one defeat condition. Open. It says what would answer it, and does not say an integrative statement is useless. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-217](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-217.md) | For a communicative kind, the standard of sufficiency is the uptake of competent participants who know the standings and norms in play, so the judges are the criterion a probe family is tested against, and a kind outruns only the families coarser than that standard | The record's reply, on 2026-10-10, to [CLAIM-206](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-206.md), and the argument [CLAIM-247](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-247.md) found missing. Calling a probe family insufficient ([CLAIM-142](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-142.md), [CLAIM-135](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-135.md)) presupposes a standard beyond it. For an act partly constituted by uptake ([CLAIM-151](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-151.md)), competent participants' uptake is that standard. The kind's extension is the class the standard fixes, and analysts' families are insufficient when coarser. No test needs a kind that outruns every family. A proposal, resting on a Proposed premise. It does not touch [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md)'s ontological clause, which the programme does not test. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-218](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-218.md) | The task-paired risk difference the record uses in place of Blackwell's order, D_Q = sup_q \|R_q(U) − R_τ(q)(V)\|, is a different quantity: it reduces each risk set to one number, needs the losses of paired tasks on one scale, and its absolute value counts improvement as distortion | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (formal lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. [CLAIM-050](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-050.md) is stated in Blackwell's order, and the record's decision term is A86's D_Q. A risk per task needs a prior or a minimax summary, where Blackwell's order compares whole risk sets. Subtracting the risks of two tasks needs their losses on one scale, which nothing supplies. The absolute value makes D_Q symmetric, against [CLAIM-106](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-106.md). Granted, because each point is elementary. It objects to [CLAIM-106](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-106.md) by undermining the measure meant to realize it, not [CLAIM-106](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-106.md)'s truth, and it does not say a decision term is unworkable. | Active |
| [CLAIM-219](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-219.md) v3 | The headline that translation changes global structure is either about contextuality, which has no case but a constructed one while the behavioural data show none, or about inconsistent connectedness, the ordinary context dependence the line says it goes beyond | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (structure lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. Dzhafarov, Zhang and Kujala ([LIT-264](../../record/literature.d/LIT-264.md)) find no contextuality in five kinds of behavioural data, and [CLAIM-125](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-125.md) reports that 34 of 90 language systems signal too much to be contextual. If pragmatic data are noncontextual as a rule, [CLAIM-105](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-105.md)'s defeat condition is met by default; what is left to change is overlap consistency, which is the "trivial sense of contextuality" and is fixed by the local distributions. The only instance of the headline is the stipulated [CASE-037](../../record/cases.d/CASE-037.md). Among the claims [LIT-264](../../record/literature.d/LIT-264.md) is cited only by [CLAIM-041](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-041.md), not by [CLAIM-105](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-105.md). The objection is open; it does not say a contextual pragmatic data set cannot exist. | Active |
| [CLAIM-220](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-220.md) | For a named population of receivers and a stated family of tasks, what survives a translation, retelling or rendering is the set of task-relevant communicative distinctions those receivers can still recover from it, and measured preservation of the pragmatic observables predicts that recovery better than preservation of the wording or of the proposition, with or without its force | The record's reply, on 2026-10-10, to the audit's objections to the tests of the manuscript's thesis ([CLAIM-233](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-233.md), [CLAIM-232](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-232.md), [CLAIM-243](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-243.md), [CLAIM-244](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-244.md), [CLAIM-226](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-226.md), [CLAIM-222](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-222.md)). It restates [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md) as an empirical claim indexed to receivers and tasks, tested against the rival [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md) itself names, a proposition with its force, on a behavioural criterion and with an interlingual arm that decides. Filed to complement [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md); whether it supersedes it is the owner's decision. It is a proposal, not a finding. It does not claim the line's distinctive machinery is needed: that is [CLAIM-202](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-202.md). | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-221](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-221.md) | The relational-constitution thesis's defeat condition describes a standard rival the record holds no counter for, intention-based accounts of illocution, which plausibly meet it for every act in the four-speakers case except the institutional one | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (structure lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. [CLAIM-151](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-151.md) is defeated by a held account that individuates teasing, reproach and reprimand by the utterance plus speaker-internal states. That is the intention-based line of Grice, Strawson and Bach and Harnish, none of whom the record holds. On it [CASE-018](../../record/cases.d/CASE-018.md)'s friend, parent and comedian differ in communicative intention, and only the judge needs norms, which Searle ([LIT-482](../../record/literature.d/LIT-482.md)) already supplies. The record's own model for [CLAIM-111](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-111.md) is of this family. The objection is open; it would narrow [CLAIM-151](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-151.md) to institutional acts unless an argument from uptake meets it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-222](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-222.md) v2 | No design in the programme includes an interlingual translation, and the defeat conditions are disjunctive, so English retellings and model chains could settle a thesis about what survives translation | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical lens). The workbench entry for 2026-10-10 notes that the title's question "is left with no translated text in the programme"; no CLAIM says it, and none draws the consequence. Every stimulus set the designs name is English, or names no language; Villon→Henley and Marot→Hofstadter never enter a design. [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md)'s defeat condition ranges over "translations, retellings or cross-modal renderings", so a result on English paraphrase alone can meet it. It does not say intralingual results are irrelevant. | Active |
| [CLAIM-223](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-223.md) | Whether a distinction survived is fixed by what the receivers can recover, and whether it can be shown to have survived is fixed by what the analyst's probes can identify, so the manuscript's thesis and the October fidelity clause, read so, answer different parts of one question | The record's reply, on 2026-10-10, to [CLAIM-211](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-211.md), and the argument [CLAIM-140](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-140.md) asked for: "the thesis now needs both conditions, or an argument for dropping one". It keeps both, in different roles. On [CLAIM-140](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-140.md)'s cases: a distinction identifiable but unusable did not survive for those receivers; one usable but unidentifiable survived without evidence of it. Read so, `complements` is right on content. A proposal: it is a reading of [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md), not what A218 says. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-224](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-224.md) | Under partial relational constitution, a translation always changes the participants, audience and norms that the relational-constitution thesis makes constituents of the act, so whether the act survives turns on a correspondence of relations the structure cannot supply from inside | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (structure lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. A translation is addressed to a new audience under new norms, so on [CLAIM-151](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-151.md) it changes constituents of the act, and the record's way out is always a correspondence ("the appropriate relationships", "corresponding relational organizations"). That makes [QUESTION-005](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-005.md) a question about what the act is, not only how to measure it, and the record's model of translation holds the audience fixed. [CLAIM-138](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-138.md) spares [CLAIM-151](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-151.md); this applies its point to [CLAIM-151](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-151.md) through translation. The objection is open. It does not say acts never survive translation. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-225](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-225.md) v3 | Blackwell's order measures what an ideal decision-maker could do, not what a given receiver can reconstruct, and the line's claims that a rendering can improve access describe garblings that real receivers do better with, which is the shape of the Blackwell thesis's own defeat condition | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (formal and structure lenses); no turn of the exchange connects the two claims. A generator that sees only the text makes its image a garbling of the text about the situation, so by Blackwell's theorem the image is no better for any decision. [CLAIM-087](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-087.md) says such an image can improve access. Either that effect is impossible, or judged access is not Blackwell informativeness, and then [CLAIM-050](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-050.md)'s standard is not the one [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md)'s receiver and its defeat condition use. It meets the shape of [CLAIM-050](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-050.md)'s defeat condition, not its letter. It is distinct from [CLAIM-140](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-140.md), which sets the analyst against the receiver: this sets the ideal receiver against an actual one. | Active |
| [CLAIM-226](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-226.md) v2 | The defeat tests pool unnamed judges and treat their disagreement as noise, though the theory makes the act partly constituted by its audience; for historical sources there is no source audience at all, and for interlingual ones population differences are read as distortion | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. A theory on which the act depends on its audience predicts structured variation between judges, so averaging a convenience sample measures an unnamed population's act. The source term of D(τ) for Villon, Henley or Marot can only be a modern reading, and for a live interlingual case the source and target judges are different communities, so the distance confounds population with transport. [CLAIM-074](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-074.md) says fidelity differs across interpreters, as a thesis; it is not applied to the tests. It does not say cross-population fidelity is meaningless. | Active |
| [CLAIM-227](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-227.md) | No validated measure of footing or stance exists in the record, and the one repeated assignment of footing, the analyst's own, changed between passes | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. Footing and stance are the observables the central theses turn on, yet the only footing judgement made twice of the same texts, the assistant's labelling of its own four refrains, changed between A38 and A39 without comment ([CASE-012](../../record/cases.d/CASE-012.md)), and no design gives judges a definition, a response format, a reliability target or a validation against a known contrast. Granted, because it is a reading of the record. It does not say footing cannot be measured. | Active |
| [CLAIM-228](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-228.md) v3 | Observational fidelity, as D_obs measures it, is a property of a rendering procedure over a class of source items, tested by one kernel family fitted on some items and scored on others, with the target term the readers' response averaged over the procedure's output; one source–target pair does not identify it, and a single rendering is judged as a sample of its procedure or by another measure | The record's repair, on 2026-10-10, of the manuscript's L_obs and the profile's D_obs after [CLAIM-207](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-207.md), [CLAIM-230](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-230.md) and [CLAIM-235](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-235.md). For one pair a constant kernel makes D_obs zero ([CLAIM-207](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-207.md)); fixed across items, it fits only if the target readers' responses do not vary with the item. The repair ties the outcome-level kernels to the text channel, makes the transport a property of a procedure ([CLAIM-230](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-230.md)), and makes deficiency a finite computation over judgement outcomes ([CLAIM-235](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-235.md)). A proposal: the derivation is elementary, but whether the restated measure is informative is the defeat condition's empirical question. It does not say a fitted kernel is the translator's process. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-229](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-229.md) | The footing, compensation and constitution theses ground on invented cases whose readings the assistant assigned, and the case filed for the proposition's non-necessity holds the words fixed, so it shows only insufficiency | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical and structure lenses); no turn of the exchange raises it. [CLAIM-061](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-061.md), [CLAIM-091](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-091.md), [CLAIM-151](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-151.md) and [CLAIM-084](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-084.md) list fictional cases in `grounds`, which [ADR-032](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-032.md) makes evidence, while the cases' own bodies say they show a possibility, and [CLAIM-151](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-151.md)'s defeat condition asks a rival to predict a variation nobody observed. [CASE-018](../../record/cases.d/CASE-018.md), filed as the necessity half of [CLAIM-061](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-061.md), keeps the words fixed, so it shows only that the proposition is not sufficient. Granted: it is a reading of the record. It does not say the theses are false or that invented cases are useless. | Active |
| [CLAIM-230](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-230.md) v2 | Blackwell comparison needs the state each rendering is about, which natural texts do not supply, so a decision measure is fixed by whoever labels the state, and it orders translation procedures, not single renderings | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. Testing Blackwell's order needs the joint law of the situation and the signal. For natural texts the situation is what interpretation recovers, so it comes from the stimulus design, the analyst's labels or the judges, and decision-relevant information is measured against whoever fixed it. An experiment is a family of distributions, one per state, so a single rendering of a single poem is not one. [CLAIM-050](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-050.md)'s defeat condition, "one rendering is a garbling of another", is then not well typed. It does not say a design-based state is illegitimate. | Active |
| [CLAIM-231](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-231.md) | On observed responses, a rendering that keeps the response distribution of each order of two framings within ε of the source's, under an outcome correspondence, keeps the observed order effect within 2ε, a bound identified from data, while the intertwining identity bounds it only through defects of an unidentified state model | The record's repair, on 2026-10-10, after [CLAIM-253](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-253.md). The commutator identity of the manuscript's §9 holds, and its defects are not identified from data, by the objection's hidden-bit construction. What can be estimated is the response distribution under each order, so the bound is restated on those: a special case of observational distortion with contexts indexed by framing order. Active, because it is elementary and checkable. It does not say the state-model account is wrong, and it does not identify the defects. | Active |
| [CLAIM-232](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-232.md) v2 | Predictor and criterion in the central defeat tests are both human pragmatic judgements of the same renderings while the baseline is a machine score, so the theory's measures can win by shared method variance alone | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. Ratings of stance and footing share rater construal, scale use and the reading of one stimulus with ratings of fidelity, and an embedding score shares none of that. The manuscript's split annotators (§10, as [QUESTION-005](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-005.md) reports) remove the shared rater, not the shared construal. It is a second reason, beside [CLAIM-233](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-233.md), that the central tests are easy to pass. Open, because a design could answer it. | Active |
| [CLAIM-233](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-233.md) | The central theses' defeat conditions pit them only against embedding, propositional or surface similarity, which no rival account holds, so surviving them would not tell the thesis from the functional and relevance-theoretic accounts the record says it must be told apart from | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (structure and empirical lenses); no turn of the exchange raises it. Every thesis-level defeat condition in the line has one form: human judgements of fidelity or of shared kind are predicted as well by a text-similarity score as by the theory's measures. [CLAIM-023](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-023.md) says the pragmatic point is not new, and a functional-equivalence, relevance-theoretic or speech-act account passes the same test. Granted, because it is read off the texts. It does not say the theses are false, only that their stated tests cannot separate them from the positions they claim to improve on. | Active |
| [CLAIM-234](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-234.md) v2 | The record's iterated-learning readings bear on lexical, structural and functional transmission, and none measures stance, footing or speech-act function along a chain | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. [CLAIM-068](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-068.md) extends iterated learning "to speaker footing and communicative conventions", and no reading the record holds measures a stance or a speech-act function under transmission. Bartlett is unread, and the transmission-chain work on social and affective content is not in the record. Granted: it is a reading of the record. It is minor because [CLAIM-068](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-068.md) is off the central chains, and it does not say stances are not stabilized by transmission. | Active |
| [CLAIM-235](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-235.md) v2 | The line's proposed quantities have no finite-data estimators, and its evidence that signalling is typical of language data rests on plug-in estimates from one or a few occurrences, which signal even when the true marginals agree | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (formal and empirical lenses); no turn of the exchange raises it. The plug-in signalling of two empirical marginals is positive in expectation when the true marginals agree, so raw tables signal, and the contextual fraction is undefined on them. With one occurrence per context, a non-signalling rank-2 system gives Δ of 0, 2 or 4, with expectation 2: the values [NOTE-654](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-654.md) finds in 51 of the 90 systems behind [CLAIM-125](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-125.md)'s "69 of the 90 systems signal". The data are author-assigned corpus senses, not judgements. Deficiency over text-valued renderings has no estimator. It does not say signalling is rare in pragmatic judgements, where order effects are themselves signalling. | Active |
| [CLAIM-236](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-236.md) v2 | Under the rational-speech-act model the distribution-over-situations thesis grounds on, a form's interpretation depends on what else the speaker could have said, so matching source and target posteriors needs a correspondence of alternative sets that translation changes and no entry names | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. [CLAIM-111](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-111.md) makes a translation faithful when the target's posterior over communicative situations matches the source's, and its worked model, [THEORY-172](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-172.md), makes that posterior depend on the set of alternative utterances. A translation replaces the source language's alternatives with the target's, so a rendering with the same literal content can carry a different posterior. Granted, because it follows from the model as the record states it. It does not say the model is wrong or [CLAIM-111](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-111.md) false. | Rejected |
| [CLAIM-237](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-237.md) v2 | The first open problem of directed transport is not yet posed: a contextual model is a menu of incompatible experiments, and under the natural order on menus any state-independent classical simulation is dominated automatically, so what is open is only the converse or a deficiency bound | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (formal lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. Blackwell's order compares experiments, each a family of distributions over one parameter. An empirical model has no parameter, and if it is contextual it has no joint distribution, only one per context. Posing [CLAIM-125](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-125.md)'s first part needs models indexed by situations and an order on menus of experiments. Under the natural order, the preservation direction is automatic for a classical simulation that does not depend on the situation. Granted, because the argument is elementary. It narrows what is open; it does not close it. | Active |
| [CLAIM-238](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-238.md) v2 | Many of the line's defeat conditions cannot in practice be met: the comparative ones are null results with no margin over an observable set the thesis may re-choose, and the possibility theses' are universal negatives that no finite study establishes | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical and structure lenses); no turn of the exchange raises it, though [CASE-029](../../record/cases.d/CASE-029.md) records the decision rules that were dropped. Three shapes of defeat condition cannot be met. "As well as" is a null result, which needs an equivalence margin and power, and no entry states either. The observables are "not fixed in advance" ([CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md)). And the "can" theses are defeated only by "never" or "always", so one construction confirms them and no study refutes them, while what the main thesis needs from them is prevalence and magnitude, which none states. Granted: each point is read off the texts. It does not say the theses are false or untestable in principle. | Active |
| [CLAIM-239](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-239.md) | A defeat condition that says something cannot be done is met by an argument that it cannot, not by a record of failures, so the admissibility and correspondence theses can fail by their own conditions, though no study the programme runs can make them fail | The record's reply, on 2026-10-10, to the fourth point of [CLAIM-238](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-238.md). luria.yaml's blurb for `defeated_if` is "what would defeat the thesis — a kind of result or argument". [CLAIM-077](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-077.md)'s condition and [CLAIM-143](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-143.md)'s second disjunct ("[QUESTION-005](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-005.md) answered negatively") are met by a showing that the distinctions or correspondences cannot be fixed independently. What the objection gets right is that no finite study meets them, so the programme's experiments do not test these two theses; argument does. Active, because it is read off the rule. | Active |
| [CLAIM-240](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-240.md) | A holistic fidelity judgement is fidelity under the judges' default task, a weighting of the profile's components that can be estimated on some renderings and tested on others, so it is well defined and consistent with task-relativity, and a defeat condition that uses it must name the population and fix the weighting before the test | The record's reply, on 2026-10-10, to [CLAIM-244](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-244.md). Task-relativity ([CLAIM-128](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-128.md)) denies a ranking that holds across tasks, not that a population has a default task. A weighting estimated on held-in data and tested on held-out data is a finding, not a fit, and a weighting fixed before the test leaves no room to blame the task after a failure. A proposal: whether holistic judgements are such a weighting is itself empirical. The objection is right that the stated defeat conditions name neither task nor receivers; [CLAIM-220](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-220.md), [CLAIM-204](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-204.md) and [QUESTION-075](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-075.md) address that. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-241](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-241.md) | Partial constitution by uptake makes competent participants' uptake a defeasible standard of a communicative kind, not the criterion: where the act's other constituents, such as its institutional conditions, diverge from how even informed participants take it, their verdicts can be wrong, and a standard must say whose competence counts, for which class of acts, and how divided verdicts are combined, which a majority does not | The interlocutor's first refinement at U62 (A240), with its row on [CLAIM-217](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-217.md), pressed at full strength. [CLAIM-217](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-217.md) moves from "partly constituted by ... expected uptake" to "the judges ... are the criterion". Partial constitution gives uptake a vote, not the last word, so where the act's other constituents diverge from informed uptake the verdicts can be wrong. The claim also names no rule for combining divided verdicts and no class of acts. Open: answering it means saying whether a defeasible standard re-opens the horn [CLAIM-217](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-217.md) closed. It does not say uptake is not a standard. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-242](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-242.md) | The use-level counterpart of Saussure's value is an utterance's contrast with the alternatives its speaker could have used in context; values do not cross between languages, which is why fidelity is graded transport through an anchored correspondence rather than identity of value | The record's reply, on 2026-10-10, to [CLAIM-254](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-254.md). It grants the objection's premises: value is internal to one language state, and it is a fact of langue, while the line's observables are facts of use. It denies the conclusion. What the premise rules out is identity of value across languages, which the line does not claim ([CLAIM-106](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-106.md)), and transport through a correspondence fixed from outside both systems is what [CLAIM-082](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-082.md)'s own qualification already requires. At the level of use, contrast with alternatives is the rational-speech-act model's premise ([THEORY-172](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-172.md)) and Bergen, Goodman and Levy's finding ([LIT-809](../../record/literature.d/LIT-809.md)). Saussure becomes precedent, not premise. A proposal, not a refutation. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-243](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-243.md) v2 | Read as description, the manuscript's thesis is close to true by definition; read as a criterion, it is a schema until the decision family Q is fixed, and its descriptive defeat condition can test neither reading | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (structure lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. A distinction no receiver can recover has not survived for anyone, so the descriptive reading is near-analytic. The normative reading leaves which distinctions count to the task family Q, which [CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md)'s summary calls "normative and contestable". The defeat condition fixes observables the thesis declines to fix, and tests them descriptively: [CLAIM-085](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-085.md)'s split of normative from descriptive, applied to the thesis rather than to model judges. It does not say the thesis is empty. | Active |
| [CLAIM-244](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-244.md) | The defeat tests take one holistic, task-free judgement of fidelity as ground truth, though the line's own theses say fidelity is task-relative, interpreter-relative and not a single ranking | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. A judge asked "how faithful?" or "the same kind?" supplies an unstated task and weighting, and is one interpreter. The theory's measure must then be fitted to that weighting, which is circular, or a mismatch blamed on the judges' task, which leaves nothing that defeats. It does not say the judgements are useless, only that the defeat conditions lack the task and receiver index the line's own theses say fidelity needs. [CLAIM-140](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-140.md) is its neighbour: that one moves the criterion to the analyst, and neither says it is task-free. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-245](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-245.md) | Under Contextuality-by-Default, a deterministic context-wise recoding can make a noncontextual signalling source maximally contextual, or turn a contextual source into pure signalling, so per-content signalling is not monotone under such maps and the quantity to bound is joint overlap discrepancy | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (formal lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. Two worked rank-2 systems answer [NOTE-654](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-654.md)'s open question negatively: [THEORY-177](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-177.md)'s per-content signalling can rise under deterministic recodings, once the source signals, and CbD contextuality can be created or removed by them. By data processing, the joint overlap discrepancy cannot rise, so that is the quantity [CLAIM-125](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-125.md)'s second part should name. Granted, because the examples are elementary. It does not say [THEORY-174](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-174.md) is wrong, which is about no-signalling models. | Active |
| [CLAIM-246](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-246.md) v2 | Sharing a kind is symmetric and blind to which work is the source, so the October thesis cannot say a translation is of its original, against the line's own directed, graded fidelity and identity through descent | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (structure lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. [CLAIM-137](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-137.md) makes realizations one act by a shared invariant, "not resemblance to an original", and [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md)'s defeat condition tests judgements of shared kind. Sharing a kind is symmetric and says nothing of descent, so an utterance made independently of the source, of the same kind, scores as a translation does. The line's own theses make fidelity directed and graded ([CLAIM-106](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-106.md)) and identity partly a matter of transmission ([CLAIM-002](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-002.md)). The objection is open; it does not say descent must be part of fidelity, only that the kind criterion cannot state it. | Rejected |
| [CLAIM-247](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-247.md) | The bearer of communicative identity reversed, from an operational object defined on observed responses to a kind that can outrun observation, and no turn argues the change; both stances stand in the record | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (structure lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. It is [CLAIM-140](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-140.md)'s point for the bearer of identity rather than the receiver. The manuscript defined the communicative object on observations, and [CLAIM-112](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-112.md) says that was "a choice of what to measure, not a theory of what meaning is". [CLAIM-139](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-139.md) is a theory of what identity is, with a kind that "may be real while remaining indistinguishable" under the probes. [TERM-014](../../record/terms.d/TERM-014.md) records the change and [CLAIM-142](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-142.md) names it in one sentence; no claim records it as unargued. Granted: it is a comparison of texts. It does not say the realist reading is wrong. | Active |
| [CLAIM-248](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-248.md) v3 | A language model agreeing with humans in direction is weak evidence, because models are trained on human text about teasing and reproach and the founding stimuli are famous, and the language-model test bed's defeat condition fires only on a reversal | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (empirical lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. Agreement in direction is what a model trained on human descriptions of teasing and reproach would show anyway, and Villon, Henley and Hofstadter's Marot are likely in training data, so a model's "interpretation" of them may be recall. [ARG-008](../../record/arguments.d/ARG-008.md) and [CLAIM-085](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-085.md) already bound what model results show about human pragmatics.
