What is new is that [CLAIM-058](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-058.md)'s validity bar is too low, and the contamination point. Minor. | Active |
| [CLAIM-249](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-249.md) | Comparing characters across realizations needs one group identified on both, which is fixed only up to automorphism, and automorphisms permute the irreducible characters, so "the same character" is relative to a chosen correspondence: the collapse problem again, at the level of the symmetry group | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (formal lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. To say a visual realization has the same character as a spoken one, the same group element must be named on both sides. Changing that identification by an automorphism α turns χ into χ∘α, and for the cyclic group of order 3 the automorphism g ↦ g² swaps the two nontrivial characters. The transformations the line studies form a semigroup, so the group is a symmetry group the analyst chooses, with its action on each medium fixed independently: [CLAIM-011](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-011.md)'s anchoring once more. Granted, because the argument is elementary. [CLAIM-132](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-132.md) and [TERM-041](../../record/terms.d/TERM-041.md) hold neighbouring limits, not this one. | Active |
| [CLAIM-250](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-250.md) | What a developing argument should preserve through legitimate revision is its accountability structure, what was asserted, why, how it was challenged and what justified its revision, not its propositions, so fidelity is defined over justificatory and provenance relations while propositional content changes | From the interlocutor's reading of PR [#121](https://github.com/dmarx/nucleation/issues/121) and its reconciliation, relayed by the owner on 2026-10-12, on the third open tension ([CLAIM-189](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-189.md) against [CLAIM-252](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-252.md)). It reads the tension as mostly reconcilable and moves the fidelity object from propositions to justificatory relations. Proposed and not adopted. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-251](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-251.md) v2 | A change in which questions a receiver population can answer determinately is not by itself a change of cover in the sheaf sense: the cover belongs to the measurement scenario and the responses are an empirical model on it, so such a change is a change of empirical model, which can create or remove a gluing obstruction on a fixed cover but does not change what the cover is, and the operational notion needs its own name until a functorial link is shown | From the interlocutor's reading of PR [#121](https://github.com/dmarx/nucleation/issues/121) and its reconciliation, relayed by the owner on 2026-10-12, on the open tension between [CLAIM-197](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-197.md) with [CASE-048](../../record/cases.d/CASE-048.md) and the operational cover of [CLAIM-201](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-201.md). It keeps the empirical notion and denies it the sheaf-theoretic name. Open. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-252](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-252.md) | The account does not say which distinctions a translation ought to keep: it says how to tell which ones a rendering kept for stated tasks and receivers, and what ought to be kept is set by the purpose the translation serves | The record's reply, on 2026-10-10, to the criterion horn of [CLAIM-243](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-243.md). The manuscript calls the choice of observables and decision tasks "normative and contestable (§11)" ([CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md)'s summary), and [CLAIM-085](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-085.md) separates a normative theory of fidelity from a descriptive one. This disclaims the first for the account, so the thesis is not a schema awaiting Q: Q comes from the translation's brief. Active, because it is a statement of what the account claims. Purpose-relative translation norms (skopos theory) are the tradition that states the normative part; they are not held in the record. | Active |
| [CLAIM-253](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-253.md) v2 | The intertwining defects are defined on unobservable interpretive-state spaces, so they are not identified from judgement data, and the commutator identity bounds observed order effects only with a further, unstated readout-intertwining defect | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (formal lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. The commutator identity is correct, but Φ, A and A′ act on interpretive states. [CLAIM-046](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-046.md) says hidden-state models that give identical observations can differ, and a worked case shows two such models with defects 0 and the largest possible, so the defect is not a function of the data. What is observed is responses, and the identity reaches the observed order effect only through a readout defect between outcome spaces, which no draft names. It is distinct from [CLAIM-011](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-011.md), which is about collapse: this is about identification. | Active |
| [CLAIM-254](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-254.md) | Read as the Saussure correction has it, the structuralist premise argues against transport: value is internal to one language state, so what can cross between languages is signification, the aspect the premise demotes | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (structure lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. [CLAIM-072](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-072.md) corrects the manuscript's Saussure to value and says the correction "does not undercut the manuscript's use". But value is fixed inside one language state, so a change of language is a change of the system that fixes every value, and Saussure's own example has a term and its translation share a signification and differ in value. Value is also a fact of the language system, while the line's observables are facts of utterances in use. The objection is open. It does not say relational identity is false, only that Saussure, read correctly, does not support its transport. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-255](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-255.md) | Intending to perform an act, performing it and being recognized as having performed it are distinct, coupled variables: intended meaning is fixed at production, the act partly by roles, standing and norms, and recognition by uptake, so competent uptake is constitutive for some acts, evidential for others and irrelevant to the existence of still others | From the interlocutor's reading of PR [#121](https://github.com/dmarx/nucleation/issues/121) and its reconciliation, relayed by the owner on 2026-10-12, on the first open tension ([CLAIM-160](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-160.md) against [CLAIM-151](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-151.md) and [CLAIM-217](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-217.md)). It gives each claim its own object rather than picking one. Proposed and not adopted: the owner has not taken it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-256](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-256.md) | D_obs sums over source contexts only, so a target context outside the image of τ appears in no term: a rendering that adds a relation pays nothing, and a change of cover registers only as loss | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (formal lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. L_obs and D_obs are sums over source contexts, so a target context that is not the image of one appears in no term. The line says interpreters add relations ([CLAIM-076](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-076.md)) and that a transport can add a distinction ([CLAIM-087](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-087.md)), and D_obs is the one component with a formula ([CLAIM-128](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-128.md)). Granted, because it is read off the definition. It does not say no other component could catch an addition, or that additions are distortions. | Active |
| [CLAIM-257](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-257.md) | Observational distortion should be summed over the contexts both covers can host, with the target's new questions asked of the source too, and the questions only one side can decide reported as a separate change of cover, so that a rendering that adds a relation pays for it | The record's repair, on 2026-10-10, after [CLAIM-256](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-256.md). D_obs sums over source contexts only, so a target context outside the image of τ is free. Asking the target's new question of the source, where its readers can answer it, puts it in both covers, and an addition then costs. Questions only one side can decide are a change of cover, which [CLAIM-201](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-201.md) makes the line's headline; they are reported, not summed. A proposal: the restatement is a definition, and whether its added term predicts anything is the defeat condition. It does not say additions are distortions. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-258](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-258.md) | Blackwell's order compares experiments about one parameter, so it does not reach the normal translation case: source and target readers decide about situations with changed coordinates, and where the state is common a translator with context knowledge yields a rendering that is not a garbling of the source, which the order typically leaves incomparable with it and about which the direction the manuscript uses is silent | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (formal and structure lenses); no turn of the exchange states it. [CLAIM-050](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-050.md)'s comparison is over one state Z. Compensation ([CLAIM-091](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-091.md)) changes the social coordinates, so the target reader decides about a different situation. Where the situation is common, a translator who knows the context makes a rendering that depends on Z other than through the source, so it is not a garbling. Blackwell's order is still defined then, but typically leaves source and target incomparable, and the direction the manuscript uses says nothing. It does not say decision-relative fidelity is wrong. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-259](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-259.md) | The decision term should be the positive part of the risk increase on the same decision problem, with losses in [0, 1] and a stated prior; so restated it is directed, counts no improvement as loss, and vanishes for every problem and prior exactly when the rendering is at least as informative as the source in Blackwell's order | The record's repair, on 2026-10-10, of A86's D_Q after [CLAIM-218](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-218.md). Define D⁺_Q(U → V; π) = sup over q in Q of [r_q,π(V) − r_q,π(U)]⁺, with r the Bayes risk under the prior π. It is directed and blind to improvement. With Q all finite problems and π all priors, it is zero exactly when V is at least as informative as U in Blackwell's order, by the Bayes form of Blackwell's theorem for finitely many states. A86's D_Q, with the identity on tasks, is its symmetrization. Under report fidelity ([CLAIM-212](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-212.md)) the paired task is the same task, so no scale between tasks is needed. The mathematics is checkable; the claim that this is the right decision term is a proposal. It does not say Q is fixed. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-260](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-260.md) | Approximate transports compose into a category only if the distortion obeys a triangle inequality and later kernels are nonexpansive, so the semigroup claim's "category or semigroup" holds for exact kernels, and for approximate ones only under those conditions | Found in the record's audit of the line on 2026-10-10 (formal lens); no turn of the exchange raises it. [TERM-008](../../record/terms.d/TERM-008.md)'s founding sense is a transport that gets "within some eps", and [CLAIM-106](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-106.md) makes fidelity graded. Kernels always compose, but the composite of an ε₁-transport and an ε₂-transport is an (ε₁ + ε₂)-transport only if the distortion obeys a triangle inequality and the second kernel is nonexpansive. In total variation both hold. [QUESTION-017](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-017.md) holds the triangle inequality open and does not tie it to the category claim. Granted, because it is elementary. It does not say the category claim fails for exact transports or in total variation. | Active |
| [CLAIM-261](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-261.md) | Synchronization of an agent's interests is desirable, but not necessary for acting as one: internal conflict shows a human agent acting while its interests stay divided | The owner's answer to [QUESTION-078](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-078.md). A human agent who acts against a standing temptation acts as one agent while its interests stay divided, so synchronization is not what acting as one consists in. Synchronization is still better when it is available: nothing has to be overridden, and [CLAIM-162](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-162.md) says that is why flow is effortless. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-262](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-262.md) | Whether an agent's regimes are coordinated by discrete switches or by continuous reorganization depends on how the agent is constituted; both occur | The owner's answer to [QUESTION-079](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-079.md): both forms are possible, and which one an agent shows depends on its constitution. An agent made of a population whose choices accumulate like a drift-diffusion process reorganizes continuously. Twitch Plays Pokémon approximates one, best in democracy mode with the tally visible as votes arrive. The consequence for [CLAIM-163](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-163.md)'s transition test is that it must be stated per constitution. A missing switch counts against coordination only in an agent built to switch. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-263](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-263.md) | Whether two trained networks compute the same way can be tested by quotienting the architecture's full symmetry group and measuring what remains: a barrier that survives alignment marks a different mechanism, connection after alignment means shared feature structure, and the test cannot run where features have no counterpart | The general content of [CLAIM-187](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-187.md), filed alone in learned-structure under [ADR-040](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-040.md): a measured protocol for asking whether two trained solutions are the same solution. Quotient the full symmetry group, including the orthogonal map on a transformer's residual stream ([THEORY-112](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-112.md)). Read a barrier that survives as a difference of mechanism ([THEORY-116](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-116.md)). Inside a basin, every layer's features interpolate ([THEORY-111](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-111.md)). The test cannot run where features have no counterpart ([THEORY-113](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-113.md)) or the objectives share no structure ([THEORY-115](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-115.md)). It is the first claim in the learned-structure line. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-264](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-264.md) v2 | Two objects can agree on every probe in a restricted family without being isomorphic, so equivalence under a probe family is coarser than identity unless the family is rich enough | The general content of [CLAIM-142](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-142.md), filed alone under [ADR-040](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-040.md) so that it does not rest on [CLAIM-046](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-046.md) and [CLAIM-011](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-011.md), which are about pragmatic identity. The restricted Yoneda functor is fully faithful exactly when the probe family is dense. A strong generator makes it conservative, which is weaker: a morphism the probes see as invertible is invertible, but objects with isomorphic profiles need not be isomorphic. Matching every probe's marginal leaves the joint unconstrained. The evidential rule is [CLAIM-322](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-322.md)'s. | Active |
| [CLAIM-265](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-265.md) v3 | Local descriptions that disagree are not thereby incompatible: whether they obstruct a joint description is decided only relative to a specified class of admissible correspondences between their observations, and a correspondence admitted only because it removes the disagreement decides nothing | The general content of [CLAIM-143](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-143.md), filed alone under [ADR-040](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-040.md) so that the order of tests does not rest on [CLAIM-011](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-011.md), [CLAIM-004](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-004.md) and [CLAIM-035](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-035.md), which are about translation. Disagreement between local descriptions sorts into a coordinate difference, a structural transformation or a global obstruction, and the sort cannot be made until a class of admissible transition maps is given. Inertial frames ([CASE-039](../../record/cases.d/CASE-039.md)) show disagreement with no obstruction once the maps are known. | Active |
| [CLAIM-266](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-266.md) | A family of probes that supplies an optimized system's training signal cannot also certify what the system preserves: optimization pushes toward whatever the probes reward, including what they cannot see, so preservation must be shown under probes not used to train it | The general content of [CLAIM-149](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-149.md), filed alone under [ADR-040](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-040.md) so that it does not rest on [CLAIM-011](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-011.md)'s account of correspondences in translation. Training against a probe family pushes a system toward whatever the family rewards, including differences it cannot see, so the family is not a certificate of what was preserved. [CASE-041](../../record/cases.d/CASE-041.md) is a documented instance: a Projected FastGAN trained against ImageNet features matches StyleGAN2's FID, also computed in ImageNet features, while doing worse in CLIP space and with human judges. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-267](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-267.md) v3 | Per-step discrepancies between a chain of maps and a reference chain compound through the sensitivity of the later steps, so the same local error can be damped, accumulated or amplified, and closeness at every step can coexist with large end-to-end divergence | The general content of [CLAIM-056](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-056.md), filed alone under [ADR-040](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-040.md) so that the line-neutral recursion does not rest on [CLAIM-106](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-106.md)'s account of fidelity. It is elementary: with e_(i+1) ≤ L_i e_i + ε_i, the end-to-end error is bounded by the discrepancies weighted by the later Lipschitz constants. Three regimes follow. For stochastic kernels in total variation, contraction coefficients do not exceed one, so amplification needs another metric or a nonlinear, adaptive state. | Active |
| [CLAIM-268](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-268.md) v2 | Model stitching certifies an order of information only in its reconstruction form: a map fitted to reproduce the target layers shows the source representation is at least as informative for every decision on the label, task-loss stitching compares one decision problem through a fixed head, and a failure to stitch is no evidence against sufficiency | The general content of [CLAIM-126](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-126.md), filed alone under [ADR-040](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-040.md) so that the result about stitching does not rest on [CLAIM-050](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-050.md)'s account of fidelity. Reconstruction-fitted stitching makes the target a garbling of the source given the label, which certifies one direction of Blackwell sufficiency ([THEORY-176](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-176.md)). Task-loss stitching ([LIT-850](../../record/literature.d/LIT-850.md)) compares one decision problem through a fixed head, orders neither representation, and is not invariant under reparametrisation. In both forms a failure says only that no map in the class works. | Active |
| [CLAIM-269](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-269.md) v2 | A regularized linear readout can tell apart only representations that differ in normalized kernel, and kernels fix a representation only up to the symmetry of what is observed, so any distinction beyond that is supplied by the analyst, not by the readouts | The general content of [CLAIM-193](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-193.md), filed alone under [ADR-040](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-040.md) so that the linear result does not rest on [CLAIM-142](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-142.md), whose realist reading is about communicative identity. What a regularized linear readout decodes is a function of the normalized kernel ([THEORY-008](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-008.md)). Kernels fix a representation only up to the symmetry of what is observed ([THEORY-002](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-002.md)). An inner product beyond that is a stipulation ([THEORY-018](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-018.md)). And spectral degeneracy does not identify the symmetry ([THEORY-019](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-019.md)). These are proved results for learned representations. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-270](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-270.md) v2 | Retaining information about where a Markov chain started and staying close to the starting distribution are different properties: a chain can forget its initial state while its distribution stays far from where it began, so convergence is not preservation and losing information about the origin is not degradation | The general content of [CLAIM-081](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-081.md), filed alone under [ADR-040](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-040.md) so that the distinction does not rest, through [CLAIM-056](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-056.md), on [CLAIM-106](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-106.md)'s account of fidelity. I(U_0; U_n) and D[P(U_n), P(U_0)] answer different questions. A contractive chain loses information about its initial state while converging to a stationary distribution that can be far from the start. The data-processing inequality bounds the first and says nothing about the second. | Active |
| [CLAIM-271](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-271.md) v2 | A collective's reasons need not be any of its members' reasons, so aggregating members' conclusions, as polling does, can yield verdicts the collective's own reasons do not support | The owner's thesis of 2026-10-13, given in conversation by refining "the good of a higher level agent need not align with the respective goods of the parts" to reasons. The assistant drew the polling consequence. On [THEORY-725](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-725.md) a group that judges linked propositions must choose between aggregating premises and aggregating conclusions, and the two can disagree. A poll aggregates conclusions. It does not say that polling is illegitimate, nor that the collective knows better than its members. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-272](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-272.md) | Giving every member an equal budget of actions does not equalize their influence, because coordination lets a member's plan direct other members' actions, so a cap on self-allocation constrains action and not authority | A8 §3 on the owner's r/place case. Every account had the same cooldown, a ban on self-allocation at the level of pixels, yet "a participant who creates a successful design template might have almost no direct pixel- writing capacity beyond any ordinary user, yet exercise enormous influence". A8: "Reddit's software imposed a cooldown and common action budget, but did not give participants equal *effective* influence." It qualifies [CLAIM-285](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-285.md): the budget constrains moves, and endorsement moves authority. It does not say the inequality is illegitimate. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-273](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-273.md) v4 | A maintained r/place artwork is a dissipative structure in Prigogine's structural sense, order held only by a sustained flow of repair against rival coalitions and against unaffiliated placement whose rate falls over the event, but its order is imported from a design and not born of an instability | The owner, on reading Wu and Silva's finding that lasting artworks are those whose coalitions keep working under continual overwriting: "sounds like there's likely a connection to dissipative structure here". It holds for half of Prigogine's criterion ([NOTE-424](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-424.md)): an open system kept from the state it would drift to by a sustained flow, here of placements. The flow works against two sources of corruption. One is rival coalitions. The other is unaffiliated placement, a real decay whose rate falls over the event (version 2, the owner's correction, [CLAIM-274](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-274.md)). It fails for the other half, since an artwork's order comes from a template, not from an instability (Adams et al.). No thermodynamic quantity is defined. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-274](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-274.md) | Participation on a shared canvas thins selectively: people keep coming back while art they invested in survives, so unaffiliated corruption falls over the event and the remaining participants are weighted toward maintainers, a cooling schedule the population produces for itself | The owner's correction to [CLAIM-273](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-273.md). Over days, random participation is a real decay, and it is not constant. Uninvested participants drop out, and invested ones stay while their art persists, which "creat[es] a survival function that biases towards user's sustaining participation if the art they are invested in persists". So the "temperature" of random corruption is high early and low late. This is a feedback from pattern to flow, and it answers [QUESTION-085](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-085.md) in part. Untested on the logs. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-275](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-275.md) | An intervention can come before the interpretation it makes available, so in an organization of processes meaning, policy and influence co-evolve and none of them is simply upstream of the others | A4 §6, on the record's two accounts of emotion, Frijda's appraisal upstream of action readiness and Barrett's construction, and the owner's two replies at U5: "yes, exactly" to "meaning, policy, and influence can co-evolve", and "so much yes" to "An operator may act not because it already possesses an interpretation, but because its intervention creates the conditions under which a particular interpretation becomes available." The first filing left both out. It does not say interpretation never comes first. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-276](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-276.md) | A shared interpretation's coherence, its fidelity to evidence and the plurality of live alternatives are separate quantities, and a population can raise the first while lowering the other two, so a collective interpretation's stability is no evidence of its truth | A1 §6, before the influence canvas: meaning as "metastable patterns of mutually reinforcing constraints", with the caveat that "delusions, dogmas, and self-reinforcing errors can also become metastable". So coherence C(M), evidential fidelity F(M, Y) and plurality P(M) must be measured apart. Zollman's result that sparser communication helps a community settle on the better option ([THEORY-143](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-143.md)) and Holman and Bruner's industrial selection ([THEORY-146](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-146.md)) are the record's readings that bear on it. It does not say coherence is bad. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-277](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-277.md) v2 | Durable authority among an organization's processes need not be a primitive: trust can be the temporal regularity of endorsement that policies earn by being right often, and the same loop entrenches whatever gained influence early | The owner's objection at U5 to A4's call to keep "persistent authority structures" distinct from "transient endorsement updates": "I'd expect persistent authority to be an emergent property here as 'right a lot' policies become distinguished into 'trusted' policies." A5 granted it. Momentary endorsement, established trust and institutionalized authority are regimes of one process, set by how slowly endorsers forget. A5 added the converse: the same loop turns an early lead into durable authority. The claim does not say which of the two a given organization does. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-278](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-278.md) | An operator framework flexible enough to redescribe any dynamical process explains none of them until it forbids some outcome, and the shared-organization thesis has not yet said what it forbids | A7 §14's own first limitation, which the exchange granted and the record files as the standing objection to the explanatory thesis: "A sufficiently flexible operator framework could redescribe almost any dynamical process. The model must earn its value through restrictive assumptions, predictive compression, and intervention-sensitive predictions." It is [CLAIM-024](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-024.md)'s objection to the will paper, met by a second account. It is answered by a prediction the framework makes and its rivals do not. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-279](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-279.md) | Whether an intervention stabilizes or destabilizes a pattern is relative to a policy and a frame, so denoiser, critic and re-noiser are regimes an operator falls into, not kinds of operator | The owner at U3 proposed operators that may "*re*noise the region it has influence over e.g. because it disagrees with the direction of the current work", and at U5 that named operator types are "just different regimes in the dynamics of our general process", with niches emerging. A5 granted it. A8 grounded it in the owner's r/place case: restoring a pixel is denoising for the group whose image it is and destruction for the group overwriting it. The claim does not say specialization always emerges. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-280](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-280.md) | A pattern can persist while every constituent is replaced and some constituents work against it, as a high-turnover regime of damage and repair, so the persistence of a product and the persistence of the organization producing it are separate facts | A8 §4 and §9, on the owner's r/place case. An artwork defended for days has its pixels repeatedly replaced, its defenders come and go, and its attackers keep working, yet the image stays recognizable. Its stability is continuous activity, not the absence of it. A8: "the distinction between a persistent product and a persistent producer". An image can outlast the coalition behind it, and a coalition can outlast the images it makes. The claim does not say that the pattern, or the coalition, is a self. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-281](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-281.md) | Endorsement indexed by role lets a process empower another as a critic while opposing its preferred policy, so an organization that allocates authority by role can keep disagreement working inside it instead of sorting into factions | A2 §8, on the owner's U2 design. If processes endorse only those whose policies match their own, the influence structure "risks degenerating into factions of mutually reinforcing beliefs". Indexing endorsement by role, E_i(j, r, c, t), lets i back j as an error detector without backing j's authority over the final choice. [CASE-080](../../record/cases.d/CASE-080.md) is the worked example. It does not say role boundaries hold under pressure. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-282](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-282.md) | Forbidding self-endorsement does not stop an operator from amplifying its own authority: once endorsement can carry authority more than one hop, reciprocal and cyclic endorsements recycle it, and only one-hop allocation within an epoch makes an operator's authority independent of its own endorsements | A2's own analysis of the owner's U2 design, conceded in the exchange and checked here. With damped recursive delegation (a PageRank-like fixed point), two operators that endorse each other pass authority back and forth, so each one's authority depends on its own outgoing endorsements even though neither endorses itself. With one-hop allocation from budgets fixed independently of outgoing endorsements, that dependence is exactly zero. No accounting rule removes the slower route: an operator can change its authority by changing what others endorse. | Active |
| [CLAIM-283](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-283.md) | The precedence a motive claims over action and the authority the rest of the organization grants it are different quantities, and internal conflict is the gap between them | A4 §2, reading Frijda through the record: "control precedence also suggests a useful addition to our parameterization", a priority p_i claimed by a process, which "is not the same as granted influence I_i. An operator can urgently demand control without receiving it. That distinction between *claimed precedence* and *endorsed authority* may be essential to modeling internal conflict." It joins [THEORY-055](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-055.md) to the endorsement scheme. It does not say which wins. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-284](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-284.md) v2 | A self, in the self-governing sense, is a historically continuous organization of processes that takes part in governing the conditions of its own persistence, differentiation and transformation, not merely one that persists | A10 §11's central hypothesis, reached after the owner asked at U10 for a research programme on a constitutive theory of selfhood. It treats the endogeneity of governance as the distinguishing mark: the processes do not only run inside an architecture, they change which processes can act and how the organization will operate. A11 named its falsifier. "Self" is in [TERM-045](../../record/terms.d/TERM-045.md)'s self-governing sense, with persisting and integrated senses nearby, and not the subject sense. Proposed, and under the record's objections: [CLAIM-116](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-116.md), [CLAIM-165](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-165.md), [CLAIM-194](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-194.md) and the counter filed with it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-285](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-285.md) v2 | An endorsement scheme that denies each operator control over its own authority can make delegation do work: rewarded for how far their preferred policies are realized and not for authority held, operators delegate to aligned others, and the two conditions need not be confined to one-hop allocation | The owner's design at U2 of the 2026-10-13 exchange: operators vote on one another's influence, cannot significantly manipulate their own, and gain by finding aligned operators and delegating authority to them. A2 made it precise. Two conditions are imposed: each operator's own authority is insensitive to its own endorsements, and its payoff is how closely the collective decision matches what it would endorse. Under them, empowering a better-placed ally is a way to get one's policies realized without becoming more powerful. It does not say such schemes resist collusion, or that alignment makes delegation wise. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-286](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-286.md) | Habit, akrasia, rumination, rigidity and insight can be modelled as regimes of one organization, in which processes transform shared meaning and allocate one another's influence, with trust, specialization and higher-level agency arising from its history and not imposed | A7's central thesis, the explanatory half of the manuscript, kept apart from the constitutive half ([CLAIM-284](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-284.md)) as A7 itself asked. Its novelty is the unification, not any ingredient: "multi-agent cognition, competing control systems, temporal discounting, self-exciting processes, and organizational emergence all have substantial literatures". It is the claim [CLAIM-278](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-278.md) objects to. It does not say these processes are a self. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-287](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-287.md) v2 | What persists when a self persists through inactivity is the capacity to reinstate an organization, not its continuous running, and continuity of that kind can branch and merge, so it is not an identity relation | A6 §7 and A10 §4.3. A6 separated activation continuity, organizational continuity and policy continuity, and put identity in the second: "A dormant coalition can remain available for reactivation because learned parameters, memory traces, and endorsement structures persist." A10 added that continuity of descent "is not automatically an equivalence relation. Organizational histories can branch, merge, and partially overlap." It sits beside [CLAIM-161](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-161.md), which asks for a coherence measure conserved along a system's own trajectory, and qualifies it for gaps. Its second half is Parfit's branching argument (THEORY-447) applied to organizational continuity. It uses [TERM-045](../../record/terms.d/TERM-045.md)'s persisting sense. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-288](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-288.md) v2 | A simulation whose operators each maximize an objective the modeller fixed cannot show self-constitution, because the norms the organization appears to maintain are then the modeller's, not set by the organization itself | The record's objection, raised by no one in the exchange. A2 gives each operator an objective J_i, its evaluation of the collective decision, and A7 and A10 propose sufficiency tests run on such populations. Barandiaran, Di Paolo and Rohde hold that systems optimizing an externally fixed function are not models of agency ([NOTE-458](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-458.md), C5), and Di Paolo (2005) that a system's norms must be norms for the system. The objection undermines the evidence route; it does not refute the thesis. It is answered if the operators' objectives are themselves produced and revised by the organization. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-289](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-289.md) | Non-stochastic strings stay non-stochastic under total recursive models: their minimal randomness deficiency agrees with the finite-set one to within O(log n) in argument and value | The step that [THEORY-260](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-260.md)'s promotion (version 2) names as the record's own: carrying [LIT-239](../../record/literature.d/LIT-239.md)'s non-stochastic strings, proved for finite-set models, to total recursive function models through Propositions B.1(b) and B.2(b) with Remark B.3. Remark B.3 asserts wholesale that every result of the paper extends to total functions up to a logarithmic term. This writes the extension out for this one result. It holds at O(log n) precision only, and does not reach [LIT-229](../../record/literature.d/LIT-229.md)'s sophistication, which is defined at constant precision. | Active |
| [CLAIM-290](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-290.md) | A positive-definite kernel's minimal realizations are unique up to a unitary, and two finite representation matrices with the same centred Gram matrix differ by a translation and an orthogonal map | The record's own proofs of two steps that [THEORY-004](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-004.md) rests on and that its sources state without proof. (a) Jorgensen & Tian's Cor. 1.35: minimal realizations of a positive-definite kernel are unique up to a unitary. The book leaves this "as an exercise", and [NOTE-829](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-829.md) supplied the book's own proof of Thm 4.35, transferred. (b) The finite case that [LIT-250](../../record/literature.d/LIT-250.md) §5.3 states as known: XXᵀ = YYᵀ implies Y = XQ with Q orthogonal, with no assumption that the rows span R^d. With centring this becomes a translation and an orthogonal map. It does not say Q is unique, which fails when the rank is below d. | Active |
| [CLAIM-291](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-291.md) | The adjoint of a square-integrable integral operator is the integral operator with the conjugate-transposed kernel | The record's own step, written down in [THEORY-009](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-009.md) and marked there as not the Riesz article's, which covers matrices and bounded operators only. For a kernel k in L²(μ⊗ν), the operator (Tf)(x) = ∫ k(x,y) f(y) dν(y) from L²(ν) to L²(μ) has adjoint (T*g)(y) = ∫ k̄(x,y) g(x) dμ(x), by Cauchy–Schwarz and Fubini. So T is self-adjoint, when μ = ν, exactly when k(x,y) = k̄(y,x) almost everywhere. It does not say where the symmetry of k comes from, which is [THEORY-009](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-009.md)'s question, and it is not claimed for kernels that are not square-integrable. | Active |
| [CLAIM-292](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-292.md) | Retrieval that depends on current belief is not a bias in itself: adaptive search can be rational when inference accounts for how the evidence was selected, and calling a search biased needs a standard outside the search | Granted against [CLAIM-301](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-301.md), as A21 insisted ("That counter is essential. Without it, we'd be using 'anchoring' as a blanket criticism of adaptive search."), and said again at A20 §5, A21 §4 and A23 V.E. Sequential experimental design chooses its next observation by its current belief and is not thereby biased. | Active |
| [CLAIM-293](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-293.md) | The topology of a support graph does not fix how a conclusion should be revised when one of its supports fails: two records with the same edges can carry evidence of different strength and call for different revisions | A6's compensating-evidence case ([CASE-089](../../record/cases.d/CASE-089.md)), granted against reading [CLAIM-295](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-295.md) as saying a graph of justifications is enough. Two pieces of evidence support H in both worlds. Discount the first, and posterior odds are 20:1 in one world and 1.1:1 in the other. `rests_on` and `grounds` record that support exists, not how much. | Active |
| [CLAIM-294](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-294.md) v2 | Two inquiry histories can agree on every current conclusion and still call for different revisions of it, so a current marginal belief is not a sufficient state for updating on evidence about what it depended on | A5's central claim, made exact by A8's construction ([CASE-093](../../record/cases.d/CASE-093.md)) and checked here. Two joint distributions over a hypothesis H and a condition C share P(H) = 0.9; learning C = 1 sends one to 1.0 and the other to 0.8. Keeping only the marginal merges histories that need different updates. It does not say a full posterior falls short ([CLAIM-300](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-300.md)), or that a dependency graph is the fix ([CLAIM-293](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-293.md)). | Active |
| [CLAIM-295](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-295.md) | A record of an inquiry's justifications adds nothing for decisions its current state already suffices for: what it can add is a wider family of decisions for which what is kept suffices, and decisions a bounded agent can make that it otherwise could not | A10's conclusion, with A5's second claim and A10 §5's three values of history: informational, computational and option value. It places [CLAIM-250](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-250.md) without settling it. [CLAIM-250](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-250.md) says what accountable inquiry ought to preserve; this says what preserving it could buy. The cases make the first half exact ([CLAIM-294](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-294.md), [CLAIM-300](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-300.md)). The second half, that real inquiries change their questions in ways that make the purchase worth its cost, is not shown. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-296](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-296.md) | What a system has, what it knows it has, and what it can deploy within a task's deadline are different, and the last is a relation among the resource, the agent, its environment, the task, the deadline and the cue, not a property of any one of them | A18's first candidate, from the owner's EMT case ([CASE-085](../../record/cases.d/CASE-085.md)): the drug was stocked, the EMT knew it, and it could not be found before the medic moved on. "If I can't find it, it isn't there" is false of the inventory and right of the decision. [TERM-055](../../record/terms.d/TERM-055.md) names the relation. A19 added the cue: the same memory under a different request may retrieve it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-297](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-297.md) | When an operator's authority is updated by how well it performs on the interpretation it helped make dominant, the coupled meaning and influence of an organization can amplify an initial interpretive advantage without any gain in fidelity to independent evidence | A22's narrow claim and A23 VIII.E's central hypothesis, from the owner's U22: connect retrieval-mediated anchoring to the record's distributed agent as dual meaning and influence canvases ([CLAIM-285](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-285.md), [TERM-053](../../record/terms.d/TERM-053.md)). An existence hypothesis until a model ([CASE-092](../../record/cases.d/CASE-092.md)) is analysed. If the influence field adds nothing to ordinary adaptive retrieval, [CLAIM-278](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-278.md) stands against it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-298](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-298.md) v2 | A frame can change a judgement by changing which of an interpreter's stored distinctions are reachable, without supplying any evidence about what is being judged | A20's proposed claim, after the owner tied addressing to anchoring at U20, and A19's "addressing conditions". Its psychological form is the selective-accessibility account of anchoring, and the readings of 2026-10-15 weaken it: Strack and Mussweiler (1997, read) infer accessibility from moderations and latencies without measuring it, and their model lets the same retrieved knowledge push a judgement either way; Harris et al. (2019, read) find the most direct measure of the accessibility step does not replicate and cannot detect priming at all. The retrieval step is inferred, not shown. The mechanism the record holds, attention as sparse distributed memory ([LIT-269](../../record/literature.d/LIT-269.md)), is a model, not evidence about judges. The extension to communication in general is the record's and is not shown. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-299](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-299.md) | Decision sufficiency, historical accountability and correctness are three properties of a record of inquiry, and each can hold while the other two fail | A8's "small mathematical structure", with A5's "Epistemic traceability and epistemic sufficiency are different properties". Shown by construction: a sufficient posterior without history ([CASE-082](../../record/cases.d/CASE-082.md)), a perfectly accountable record that is wrong ([CASE-084](../../record/cases.d/CASE-084.md)), a current marginal that is right and cannot be revised ([CASE-093](../../record/cases.d/CASE-093.md)). [CLAIM-189](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-189.md) and [CLAIM-190](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-190.md) hold neighbouring points about measuring an argument. This names the three predicates so the others can say which they mean. | Active |
| [CLAIM-300](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-300.md) | A state sufficient for a family of decisions under a model makes the rest of an inquiry's history worthless for those decisions, and it can stop being sufficient when a source is discredited, the model is revised or the decisions change | A10's answer to the owner's U10, with A8's fourth case and A10's normal constructions ([CASE-082](../../record/cases.d/CASE-082.md), [CASE-087](../../record/cases.d/CASE-087.md)). Under a fixed, correctly specified model a sufficient posterior makes the history redundant for those decisions. The same sample mean that sufficed cannot say which reading remains when one instrument is discredited, or test a mixture. Sufficiency was always relative to the model and the family. [CLAIM-152](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-152.md) says the same of individuation by target; this says it of a retained state over time. | Active |
| [CLAIM-301](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-301.md) | Iterative retrieval can turn a provisional interpretation into a premise: a framing selects what is encountered, what is encountered reinforces the framing, and the question being investigated drifts as well as the queries | A21's proposed claim, from the owner's U21 link between retrieval-mediated anchoring, agent harnesses and older information retrieval. Classical IR named query drift ([LIT-1079](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1079.md)). An agent that rewrites its own task adds [TERM-056](../../record/terms.d/TERM-056.md), which A21 judged "arguably more consequential". The exchange did this itself between A2 and A13. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-302](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-302.md) | Equivalence under feasible tests can be much coarser than statistical equivalence: there are ensembles almost disjoint from uniform that no polynomial-size test tells apart from it, and efficiently samplable ensembles of this kind exist if pseudorandom generators do | A15's witness for "Complexity changes what we mean by an observable", which the owner asked to have developed at U15, corrected by the reading of Goldreich (NOTE of 2026-10-15). [CLAIM-264](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-264.md) says agreement on a restricted probe family is coarser than identity; feasibility gives a restriction that is forced, not chosen. The separation itself is unconditional: a counting argument gives ensembles supported on at most 2^(n/2) strings that no circuit of size 2^(n/8) tells from uniform. Only an efficiently samplable witness needs an assumption, and a secure pseudorandom generator supplies one. All of it is asymptotic, about ensembles indexed by n. | Active |
| [CLAIM-303](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-303.md) | Filing an argument moves work across time: it is paid for now, in filing, indexing, tracking dependencies and checking it is still current, against reconstruction later, so it is worth its cost only when expected reuse covers it, and recurrence is evidence of reuse, not the criterion | A11, at the owner's request for "content to the effect of computation-storage tradeoffs". A11's four expenditures (materialization, indexing, dependency tracking, refresh) and its break-even condition. The arithmetic is trivial. The thesis is about the record: that its second-occurrence rule ([ADR-032](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-032.md)) and the nucleation threshold of [CLAIM-185](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-185.md) are approximations to this trade, and can fail in both directions. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-304](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-304.md) | A rendering can keep a distinction recoverable in principle and make it infeasible to recover: if pseudorandom generators exist, a uniformly random bit can be encoded so that an unbounded receiver recovers it except with probability at most 2⁻⁽ⁿ⁺¹⁾, while no efficient receiver does non-negligibly better than chance | A14's first failure mode, computational inaccessibility, and A18's fourth candidate: "Recoverable in principle ≠ recoverable within budget." [CLAIM-199](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-199.md) shows a garbling can help a bounded receiver. This is the other direction: a nearly lossless rendering can defeat every efficient one. The construction is the record's, from [CLAIM-302](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-302.md)'s witness; A18 asked for one and warned against "a vague appeal to an inverse being hard". | Active |
| [CLAIM-305](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-305.md) | Resolving a singularity by blow-up reparametrizes the states and adds no observation, so it cannot make indistinguishable states distinguishable: every point over a singular point gives the same distribution, and the Fisher information can stay degenerate there; what the resolution buys is a normal form for the divergence, and so the learning coefficient | The owner's LEGO monograph says, in Part I (chapters 4, 6, 7, 9, the Overview and "Definitions and Theorems"), that blow-up "restores identifiability", "introduces new observables" and makes the Fisher information non-degenerate, and maps it onto splitting a LEGO bin. The pulled-back model is p(x \| π(u)), a function of u only through π(u), so every point of the exceptional divisor gives the distribution of the singular point. In p(x \| a, b) = N(ab, 1), the chart a = u, b = uv gives mean u²v and a Fisher matrix of zero all along u = 0. Splitting a bin is refining the observable, which can separate states. [MOM-CLM-099](https://github.com/dmarx/mathematics-of-meaning/blob/main/record/claims.d/CLM-099.md) records that the monograph uses "blow-up" in three senses; this records why the analogy cannot hold in any of them. | Active |
| [CLAIM-306](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-306.md) | Under a deadline, search can establish presence by finding one item but absence only by exhausting the collection, so "If I can't find it, it isn't there" acts on an unfinished search as negation as failure acts on a finished one: a default stronger than either the closed-world assumption or negation as failure, safe only when the arrangement makes the full search finish within the deadline | The medic's "If I can't find it, it isn't there" ([CASE-085](../../record/cases.d/CASE-085.md)) and the owner's "if I can't find it quickly enough, it may as well not be there" ([CASE-094](../../record/cases.d/CASE-094.md)) act on a failed search as if it showed absence. One find shows presence, but absence needs a check of everything, which a deadline forbids. First filed as negation as failure on a time bound, with Reiter and Clark unread. Both readings corrected it. The two assumptions are separate. Reiter's closed-world assumption is that the database is complete about the world. Clark's negation as failure is licensed only by finite failure: "the exhaustive but unsuccessful search for a proof". A search cut off by a deadline meets neither, so the rule is stronger than both. It assumes a closed world and that the unsearched part would have failed. It is safe when the full pass finishes in time. In [CASE-085](../../record/cases.d/CASE-085.md) the EMT knew the drug was stocked, which is exactly where Reiter shows the closed-world assumption inconsistent. So there it was a decision to stop, not an inference. | Superseded — by [CLAIM-312](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-312.md) |
| [CLAIM-307](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-307.md) | Retrieval pressure and evidence are different criteria for carving a category, and they move in opposite directions with copy count: evidence that a distinction exists grows with its copies, the search a bin saves shrinks with them, so the bins a deadline forces are often the ones the data least supports | From [MOM-CLM-166](https://github.com/dmarx/mathematics-of-meaning/blob/main/record/claims.d/CLM-166.md), which reads the owner's LEGO parable ([CASE-094](../../record/cases.d/CASE-094.md)) against a formalism that cited it with the sign reversed. [CLAIM-154](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-154.md) says when a distinction is real enough to name. Its evidence grows with the number of copies. The parable says when a distinction must be built to act in time: a single brick among similar ones. Expected search falls as copies grow. The two answer different questions, "is it there?" and "can it be reached in time?", and come apart in both directions. MOM's table was re-run here and reproduced, and a simulation of the search agrees. | Active |
| [CLAIM-308](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-308.md) v2 | Where reuse cannot be forecast, recurrence weighted by reconstruction cost is the filing criterion, not only evidence for one: filing once the reconstruction already paid for equals the cost of filing is within twice the hindsight optimum on every sequence of uses, and no deterministic rule without foresight does better, under fixed known costs and with staleness left out | Filed at the owner's request, from the rent-or-buy result of Karlin et al. 1988 ([THEORY-348](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-348.md)) set against [CLAIM-303](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-303.md)'s clause that recurrence is evidence of reuse, not the criterion. In [CLAIM-303](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-303.md)'s own terms the break-even rule needs no forecast and is worst-case optimal; [ADR-032](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-032.md)'s second-occurrence rule is that rule when filing costs about one reconstruction. It does not reach staleness, which the theorem forbids rather than prices, or drifting costs, and where reuse can be forecast an expected-reuse rule can do better, so [CLAIM-303](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-303.md)'s criterion survives there. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-309](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-309.md) | How good a mixture is and how discriminable it is from its diluent come apart: under linear mixing a smoothie's distance from milk only shrinks as milk is added, so a preference that rises and then falls with milk is over the ratio and cannot be a d′, and the signal-detection reading of the owner's smoothie line reverses one of its directions | The smoothie note in the owner's LEGO monograph reads the owner's line ([CASE-096](../../record/cases.d/CASE-096.md)) as signal detection. Milk is the noise, "Less Good" is d′ falling as the mixture approaches milk, and "More Good" is d′ rising as a little milk is added. Under linear mixing the mixture's taste is (1 − f)s + f·m, and its distance from milk is (1 − f)\|s − m\|. That falls monotonically from the first drop, so discriminability from milk cannot rise and then fall. The note's own first model is right: goodness is a single-peaked function of the ratio, which is a preference, not a discriminability. [MOM-CLM-166](https://github.com/dmarx/mathematics-of-meaning/blob/main/record/claims.d/CLM-166.md) found the same kind of reversal in the formal reading of the LEGO parable. MOM's record never mentions the smoothie note. | Active |
| [CLAIM-310](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-310.md) v2 | Sorting schemes over objects with determinate properties never conflict as descriptions, since the product of any sortings refines them all; they conflict as arrangements, since a fixed number of bins realizes one partition when each item is stored once, and which costs least depends on what is searched for and on what stands out within a bin | What is directionally right in the owner's LEGO monograph, as Mathematics of Meaning graded it. Its contextuality claim is empty: any sortings of bricks share a joint refinement ([MOM-CLM-137](https://github.com/dmarx/mathematics-of-meaning/blob/main/record/claims.d/CLM-137.md)), and the events they generate stay Boolean ([MOM-ARG-047](https://github.com/dmarx/mathematics-of-meaning/blob/main/record/arguments.d/ARG-047.md)). Its intuition that builders cannot share a sorting is true of arrangements. One set of bins realizes one partition, the product may need more bins than there are, and the cheapest choice depends on what each builder searches for and on what stands out within a bin ([CASE-095](../../record/cases.d/CASE-095.md)). The disagreement is a trade, not a failure of global truth. Sorting on one attribute also turns a search for a combination of features into a search for one feature within each bin, which Treisman and Gelade (LIT-1082, read) show is the difference between a serial scan and one found at a glance. | Active |
| [CLAIM-311](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-311.md) | A search cut off before an exhaustive pass can establish absence when the arrangement certifies that the unsearched remainder cannot hold the item: a sorted table answers non-membership in a logarithmic number of probes, and the Threshold Algorithm stops early with its answer proved, so absence needs a certificate about the remainder, not exhaustion; similarity search in high dimension is where no such certificate exists | Filed at the owner's request, from the database readings set against [CLAIM-306](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-306.md)'s asymmetry: presence by one find, absence "only by exhausting the collection". Sorted order and the Threshold Algorithm each establish absence from part of the collection, because the arrangement bounds what the rest can hold. That meets [CLAIM-306](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-306.md)'s own defeat condition, and the claim is conceded: [CLAIM-312](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-312.md) restates it. Where the arrangement gives no bound, as in similarity search in high dimension, [CLAIM-306](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-306.md)'s asymmetry stands. | Active |
| [CLAIM-312](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-312.md) | "If I can't find it, it isn't there" asserts operational unavailability, that the item will not be put to use before the deadline, not absence: a search that fails by the deadline establishes unavailability outright, and licenses absence only with a certificate that the unsearched remainder cannot hold the item, from exhaustion, from an invariant of the arrangement, or from a closed-world default | Restates [CLAIM-306](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-306.md) at the owner's request, after [CLAIM-311](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-311.md) showed that absence can be established without exhausting a collection. The owner's own wording in [CASE-094](../../record/cases.d/CASE-094.md), "it may as well not be there", is hyperbole for operational availability ([TERM-055](../../record/terms.d/TERM-055.md)), not a claim about inventory. Read so, the rule reports an outcome and needs no default. [CLAIM-306](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-306.md)'s analysis survives as the account of the literal reading: what a failed search must be joined with before it licenses absence. The gap between the two readings is [CLAIM-306](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-306.md)'s Bayes term, and it matters when the statement is passed to someone who will act on presence. | Active |
| [CLAIM-313](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-313.md) v2 | A fixed number of bins realizes one partition only when each item is stored once: with copies, overlapping partitions coexist, as hashing-based similarity search stores each item in many partitions and pays for it in space, so sortings conflict as arrangements only under a one-copy constraint, which binds bricks but not index entries | Filed at the owner's request, from the readings on locality-sensitive hashing (Indyk and Motwani 1998; Andoni, Laarhoven, Razenshteyn and Waingarten 2017) set against [CLAIM-310](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-310.md)'s premise that "a set of k bins realizes one partition of the collection at a time". That holds when each item is in one place. An index can hold many copies, and hashing-based similarity indexes do exactly that, trading the space of the copies against query time on an exact curve. It narrows [CLAIM-310](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-310.md) to collections whose items cannot be copied, which includes the LEGO case; it does not touch the claim about descriptions. | Active |
| [CLAIM-314](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-314.md) | Reusing a filed argument has a recognition cost that a fixed per-use price leaves out: certifying that stored answers cover a new question is NP-complete even for conjunctive queries, grows with the number and size of the stored definitions rather than being a constant per item, and is undefined for an item filed without a statement of what it answers, so each filing raises the cost of later reuse and per-item break-even decisions are not independent | Filed at the owner's request, from the readings on answering queries using views (Levy, Mendelzon, Sagiv and Srivastava 1995; Chandra and Merlin 1977; Halevy 2001). [CLAIM-308](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-308.md)'s guarantee needs a fixed, known per-use cost c_a; recognizing that a stored answer covers a new question is a search whose cost depends on everything stored, so filing one item changes the price of using the others. It undermines the premise [CLAIM-308](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-308.md) states, not its arithmetic. The hardness is worst case and over short definitions, partial reuse is cheap, and nothing here is measured on the record. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-315](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-315.md) | In comparative anchoring tasks, considering an anchor shifts the later numerical estimate without adding evidence about the target, and the shift is carried by anchor-consistent knowledge made more accessible; the shift is established, the accessibility step is inferred and has not been measured | The empirical half of [CLAIM-298](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-298.md), split off under [ADR-043](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-043.md)'s pilot, which found that claim compound: an empirical claim about judgement tasks and an interpretive extension to communication in general. This half is scoped to the comparative-question paradigm. Its first part is established ([THEORY-337](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-337.md)); its second is the selective-accessibility account ([THEORY-335](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-335.md)), whose direct test failed to replicate with a measure that also missed direct priming. [CLAIM-298](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-298.md) now rests on it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-316](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-316.md) | A person's drive profile is time-indexed: who drives changes with state, such as hungry or sated and stressed or calm, with task and with regime | Split from [CLAIM-166](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-166.md) under [ADR-043](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-043.md): the first of the owner's two parts of 2026-10-11, "'who drives' isn't a constant", stated on its own. It is argued from the drive-profile term and the will line's account of which regime is effective, and no observation the record holds estimates a person's drive profile over successive windows, so it is interpretive. The microbiome part, which the reviews support, stays in [CLAIM-166](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-166.md). | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-317](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-317.md) v2 | Self-presentations that differ by audience do no harm while the audiences are kept apart; people keep them apart and script the occasions where they must meet, and broadcast and networked media bring them together unchosen, which is when the presentations collide | Split from [CLAIM-196](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-196.md) under [ADR-043](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-043.md): the empirical half of that claim, the sociology of audiences on which its sheaf reading rests. Its evidence is boyd (2008, read in part in [NOTE-710](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-710.md)), after Meyrowitz (1985), and one documented collapse, [CASE-064](../../record/cases.d/CASE-064.md). boyd's claims about collapse are documented with interviews and examples, not measured, and the Carmichael case reaches the record secondhand, so it stays Proposed. It says nothing about overlaps, covers or gluing; that is [CLAIM-196](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-196.md)'s. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-318](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-318.md) | Within a shared observational scenario, exact structure-preserving symmetries are the invertible members of a nested family of stochastic transports, and among Markov kernels between finite sets the invertible ones are the permutation matrices, so the invertible core is the relabellings, a groupoid in which objects joined by an arrow have isomorphic automorphism groups | Split from [CLAIM-103](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-103.md) under [ADR-043](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-043.md): the nesting and its invertible core, stated on their own so that their warrant can be. A110 §25 nests exact symmetries inside structure-compatible transports inside stochastic communicative transports, within a shared scenario, and the exact symmetries are the invertible ones. For Markov kernels between finite sets, a stochastic matrix with a stochastic inverse is a permutation matrix, because a nonnegative matrix with a nonnegative inverse is monomial, so the invertible core is the relabellings. In that groupoid an invertible u : A → B gives Aut(A) ≅ Aut(B), which corrects A198 §8. It does not say what symmetries are for: that they supply the yardstick for non-symmetric transports is [CLAIM-103](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-103.md)'s, and interpretive. | Active |
| [CLAIM-319](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-319.md) | Over a scenario of n contexts in an odd cycle, changing the support of one context takes the contextual fraction from 1 to 0 while the other n − 1 contexts and every marginal are kept exactly, at a weighted observational distortion of 1/n, so within a scenario the fraction's Lipschitz constant against that distortion is at least n; and any change of global extension needs a contextual model on one side | Split from [CLAIM-201](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-201.md) under [ADR-043](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-043.md): the derivation that claim called "decisive and checkable", stated on its own. For odd n, an n-cycle of ±1 observables in which every context anticorrelates is strongly contextual; making one context correlate gives a model with a global distribution. The two agree on n − 1 contexts and on every marginal, differ by total variation 1 in the last, and so lie at weighted D_obs = 1/n with contextual fractions 1 and 0. For n = 3 it is [CASE-037](../../record/cases.d/CASE-037.md) with one observable relabelled. It answers how small the local change can be; it says nothing about whether any data are contextual. | Active |
| [CLAIM-320](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-320.md) | Within one fixed measurement scenario, two no-signalling empirical models can agree on all but one of their contexts and on every single-observable marginal while their contextual fractions are 1 and 0, so keeping most local judgement distributions does not fix their global compatibility | Split from [CLAIM-105](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-105.md) under [ADR-043](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-043.md): the possibility that claim's headline needs, shown by the pair it lost and recovered in [CASE-037](../../record/cases.d/CASE-037.md). On the cover {AB, BC, AC} with binary outcomes, the parity triangle at 1/2 per allowed outcome is no-signalling and strongly contextual, with contextual fraction 1. Change only S_AC to {(0,0), (1,1)}: two contexts and every marginal are unchanged, and the model now extends to a global distribution, with fraction 0. It is an existence result by construction, and the weights are stipulated. It does not say reconstruction chains do this, which is [CLAIM-105](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-105.md)'s thesis and interpretive, and the context that changes moves by a total-variation distance of 1. | Active |
| [CLAIM-321](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-321.md) v2 | In text-to-image diffusion models each condition of a prompt can be met while their conjunction or relational binding fails: benchmarks find single objects rendered almost always and attribute binding and relations failing most, and composing by adding scores does worst on relations | Split from [CLAIM-123](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-123.md) under [ADR-043](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-043.md): the empirical part of that claim, that each condition can be met while their conjunction or relational binding fails, documented by GenEval, T2I-CompBench and T2I-CompBench++, by Attend-and-Excite's "catastrophic neglect", and by Composable Diffusion losing to an energy-based baseline on relations. It corroborates [CLAIM-123](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-123.md)'s derivation without carrying it, and does not say what causes the failures. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-322](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-322.md) | Agreement under a probe family is evidence that two objects share structure only to the degree that the family was chosen independently of the comparison and is evaluated on cases it was not chosen on, so a family selected to make two objects look alike shows only that it can be selected | Split from [CLAIM-264](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-264.md) under [ADR-043](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-043.md): its evidential half, stated on its own so that its warrant can be. [CLAIM-264](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-264.md)'s exact half shows that agreement on a restricted probe family does not fix identity; this claim draws the rule for reading such agreement as evidence. It is an argument, not a theorem, so it is interpretive. It does not say which probes to choose, or that held-out probes are neutral. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-323](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-323.md) v2 | Where source and rendering are experiments about one common situation, a rendering that depends on the situation other than through the source is not a garbling of it and can be incomparable with it in Blackwell's order: with Z uniform on {1, 2, 3}, a source that tells whether Z = 1 and a rendering that tells whether Z = 3 each have the lower 0–1 risk on one of the problems of saying whether Z = 1 and whether Z = 3, so neither dominates | Split from [CLAIM-258](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-258.md) under [ADR-043](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-043.md): the worked case that objection uses, stated on its own so that its warrant can be. Over a common situation Z uniform on three values, U reports whether Z = 1 and V whether Z = 3. V is not a garbling of U, since given U = 0 it still varies with Z, and the risks on "say whether Z = 3" (0 from V, 1/3 from U) and "say whether Z = 1" (the reverse) show neither experiment dominates. It is an existence result by construction. It does not say this is the normal translation case, which is [CLAIM-258](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-258.md)'s reading and is interpretive. | Active |
| [CLAIM-324](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-324.md) | In the What Survives Translation? exchange, the manuscript drafted straight from the crystallized argument changed the thesis with no turn arguing the move and dropped 24 of that argument's items, none on request, and the second crystallized argument announced its change of question but left items out without saying which had dissolved and which were lost | Split from [CLAIM-178](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-178.md) under [ADR-043](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-043.md): the documented case on which that claim's normative conclusion rests, scoped to the one exchange it comes from. The evidence is the workbench reconstructions of that exchange (2026/10/09/025539, 2026/10/10/040457). One exchange, read by the record's own readers, so Proposed; it says nothing about drafts in general. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-325](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-325.md) | The behavioural data the record has read show no contextuality proper: none of the five kinds of data Dzhafarov, Zhang and Kujala re-analyse is contextual, Wang et al. test none of their 90 language systems for it, and no documented test of pragmatic or meaning judgements finds it at adequate power | Split from [CLAIM-219](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-219.md) under [ADR-043](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-043.md): the empirical premise of that objection's dilemma, which [CLAIM-201](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-201.md) conceded. Dzhafarov, Zhang and Kujala ([LIT-264](../../record/literature.d/LIT-264.md), read in [NOTE-235](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-235.md)) find none of five kinds of behavioural data contextual, two of them previously published as contextual; Wang, Sadrzadeh, Abramsky and Cervantes ([LIT-851](../../record/literature.d/LIT-851.md)) report signalling in 69 of 90 language systems and test none for contextuality; [CASE-053](../../record/cases.d/CASE-053.md) collects the documented tests on elicited judgements and finds no contextual pragmatic data. It is scoped: [CASE-053](../../record/cases.d/CASE-053.md) records a reliable positive result on instructed matching, so "none in behavioural data" is too broad, and the absence is [LIT-264](../../record/literature.d/LIT-264.md)'s working hypothesis, not a proof that such data cannot exist. | Active |
| [CLAIM-326](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-326.md) v3 | With losses in [0, 1] and a stated prior, the positive part of the Bayes-risk increase on the same decision problem, D⁺_Q(U → V; π), is directed, is zero from a garbling back to its source, vanishes for every finite problem and prior exactly when the rendering is at least as informative as the source in Blackwell's order over finitely many states, and has A86's D_Q, with the identity on tasks, as its symmetrization | Split from [CLAIM-259](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-259.md) under [ADR-043](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-043.md): the properties of the restated decision term, which [CLAIM-259](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-259.md) called "elementary and checkable", stated on their own so that their warrant can be. Define D⁺_Q(U → V; π) = sup over q in Q of [r_q,π(V) − r_q,π(U)]⁺. A garbling never gains; with Q all finite problems and π all priors, the term vanishes exactly when V is at least as informative as U, by the Bayes form of Blackwell's theorem for finitely many states ([THEORY-156](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-156.md)); it is directed; and A86's D_Q is its symmetrization. It does not say this is the right decision term, which is [CLAIM-259](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-259.md)'s proposal, and it does not state the prior-free relation to Le Cam's deficiency. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-327](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-327.md) | A slowly changing collective variable, such as a reputation, a rivalry or a standing, is part of a collective's dynamics and not only an observer's average when its members read it, condition what they do on it, and by doing so regenerate it | A9 §3 and §10 of the exchange of 2026-10-16, after Flack 2012 (LIT-1122, unread). Three stages: an observer finds a stable statistic of fast interactions; members recognize it and use it; their use preserves or regenerates it. Only the third makes the variable an endogenous level. It is the general form of [CLAIM-277](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-277.md), where trust is the temporal regularity of endorsement that then steers endorsement. It does not say that such a variable individuates one collective: five slow variables sharing a referent may be five processes. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-328](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-328.md) | When the continuities that ordinarily come bundled in a collective (legal franchise, civic role, interpersonal transmission, narrative and place) are separated, several later collectives can each be a genuine successor of the earlier one in a different respect, without any of them, or the earlier collective, having branched in numerical identity | A4's H5 of the exchange of 2026-10-16, in its weak form, on the Washington Senators. The league's successor to the original club is the Twins; the city's is the expansion Senators; the city's present team claims its history through place and narrative. Promoted from a section of [QUESTION-118](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-118.md) at the owner's word. It concerns plural successor relations, not branching identity, and rests on one documented case with no data on how supporters divided. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-329](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-329.md) v3 | A collective that replaces all its members persists, when it does, by causal transmission of the organization that produces it, not by resemblance: an organization of the same kind that arises independently is not its continuation, and a lapse in its coherence need not end it | A5, A8 and A9 of the exchange of 2026-10-16. A9 §9 separated three persistences that dissociate: state persistence (a variable changes slowly), dynamical persistence (characteristic responses continue) and historical persistence (the producing processes are transmitted and reconstituted across successive populations).
