A5: "a system's ability to reproduce itself as the same kind is not automatically sufficient for its numerical persistence as the same individual." The second time the record meets "causal-organizational continuity": [CLAIM-280](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-280.md) noted A10 §4.3's of 2026-10-13 and did not file it. It does not say what must be transmitted, or settle branching successors. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-330](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-330.md) | In the coupled meaning–influence model linearized at its symmetric state, with each field stable alone and nonnegative couplings, the coupling destabilizes symmetry exactly when βγ > (1 − α)(1 − δ): only the loop can do it, and the direction that grows mixes meaning and authority | A14 §2 of the exchange of 2026-10-16, checked. For J = [[α, β], [γ, δ]] with nonnegative entries and α, δ < 1, the leading eigenvalue exceeds 1 iff βγ > (1 − α)(1 − δ). If either coupling is zero the eigenvalues are α and δ. The leading eigenvector is (β, λ+ − α), positive in both components. A partial answer to [QUESTION-100](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-100.md)'s "when the symmetric state is unstable", for the linearization only; it says nothing about the nonlinear attractors or about fidelity. | Active |
| [CLAIM-331](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-331.md) v7 | A level of organization has a temporal facet as well as a compositional one: higher-level variables are characteristically slower and longer-lived than the activity that produces them, though slowness is a mark of a level and not its definition | The owner's, U8 of the exchange of 2026-10-16: "'higher level' doesn't just mean in terms of abstractions over abstractions, we should also expect 'higher level' effects to be on slower timescales than 'lower level' effects." A8 took it and qualified it: a market can crash and a stadium can chant in seconds, so timescale is "a facet of organizational level, not ... an ordering that must hold in every case". Simon's near-decomposability already ties weak coupling between blocks to slow aggregate dynamics ([LIT-905](../../record/literature.d/LIT-905.md)). The claim does not say that a slow variable is thereby a level, a system or an individual. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-332](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-332.md) | A loosely organized population can change what an outside institution does through its members' separately motivated acts of making, selecting and passing things on, without forming a collective intention or judgment, when the organized pattern of those acts, and not only the acts of identifiable individuals, earns its place in explaining the change | A7's "distributed cultural uptake", of the exchange of 2026-10-16, on the owner's Juggernaut case. On A7's ladder (aggregate behaviour, amplification, collective causal efficacy, collective intention, corporate agency) it asserts the third rung and denies that the third needs the fourth. Promoted from a section of [QUESTION-119](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-119.md) at the owner's word, with A7's defeater priced by [CLAIM-154](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-154.md), which A7 said it needed. It does not say the fandom was the efficacious unit. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-333](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-333.md) | Measuring every relevant variable is necessary but not sufficient for a Granger-causal analysis to say anything about interventions: the intervention must also be an isolated exogenous change and no influence may act within one sampling step, and then Granger noncausality implies no intervention effect and the effect is identified from the observational predictor, but a Granger-causal relation is still only potentially causal | The record's answer to [NOTE-010](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-010.md)'s C5, "if all relevant variables are measured, Granger- and Pearl-type analyses coincide", which the ΦID review asserts without conditions. Eichler and Didelez ([THEORY-704](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-704.md)) give sufficient conditions, and complete measurement is one of them. A two-variable process with nothing hidden shows it is not enough: a acts on b within one step, a is Granger-noncausal for b, and setting a(t) changes the mean of b(t + 1) by ργ. Checked by hand and by simulation. | Active |
| [CLAIM-334](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-334.md) | Granger causation from a macro variable to the micro variables it supervenes on, Seth's downward Granger causation, is not evidence of a macro-to-micro intervention effect, even with every micro variable measured: supervenience leaves no intervention on the macro variable that holds its micro base fixed at the same time | Seth measures downward causation as Granger causation from the flock's centre of mass to each boid, and calls it "ontologically innocent" ([THEORY-703](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-703.md)). The record files the boundary so that the stronger reading has an address. If M(t) = f(m(t)), setting M(t) means changing m(t), so the intervention Eichler and Didelez's Assumption 2.3(i) requires does not exist ([THEORY-704](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-704.md)). Measuring every boid does not help, by [CLAIM-333](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-333.md). | Active |
| [CLAIM-335](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-335.md) v3 | Transfer entropy measures directed predictive improvement, not information flow: as a conditional mutual information it is the source's unique information about the target's next value plus its synergy with the target's past, so it can credit a source with a bit the source carries only jointly and read zero from sources that jointly fix the target, and conditioning on more series does not remove the synergy | The record's position on whether transfer entropy measures information flow. James, Barnett and Crutchfield show it does not, if flow is information attributable to the source alone ([THEORY-707](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-707.md)). Williams and Beer's decomposition shows why, for any partial information decomposition satisfying the consistency equations ([THEORY-712](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-712.md)). Both papers' examples were recomputed exactly. This is a different failure from [CLAIM-333](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-333.md)'s, prediction against intervention. It leaves transfer entropy standing as a predictive quantity. | Active |
| [CLAIM-336](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-336.md) v2 | The pragmatic-transport and will-organization lines describe one model, an organization of operators that act on a shared interpretive state under a context while allocating one another's influence: a frame is the context under which operators act, a judgement is the response they yield, an action-generating regime is an operator or coalition, and a will-organization is the dynamics that allocates influence among them | The owner's, U2 of the exchange of 2026-10-16: the pragmatic-transport model and the will-organization model are "both manifestations of the same underlying model". The mapping is the assistant's, A6, filed at the owner's request ("address the still open stuff"). At U8 the owner endorsed it as filed ("Yes, as filed"). It reads both lines into [CLAIM-297](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-297.md)'s operator model. The sharp edge: it says the lines share one model, not that either line's results transfer to the other unchanged. Each mapping is a hypothesis until a result on one line is shown to hold, through it, on the other. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-337](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-337.md) | Active inference already runs an internal loop: mental action selects among higher-level cognitive states and sets the precision of lower-level observations, structure learning reshapes the generative model without action, and a judgement is behaviour, so a judgement–observation loop may be action and perception one Markov blanket inward | The assistant's, A1 of the exchange of 2026-10-16, on active inference's behalf, strengthened at A4. Sandved-Smith et al. cast mental action as policy selection over higher-level cognitive states; Brouillet and Friston make felt fluency an inference about the precision of one's own processing; Smith et al. let a generative model grow its own structure by inference and learning alone. With Chalmers's point that judgements are behaviour, the owner's judgement is active inference's action relocated. What it does not reach: the diagram. A redescription with no action on the world concedes that action on the world is not part of the loop. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-338](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-338.md) v2 | The selectivity active inference gives action, sampling what one expects to experience, is played in the coupled meaning–authority model by the authority space: influence decides which operators retrieve and so what the next judgement observes, which makes Friston's self-confirming sampling the model's authority-to-interpretation arrow and his agent a coupled loop run without independent scoring | The owner's, U10 of the exchange of 2026-10-16, on Friston et al.'s "sampling selectively what one expects to experience": "I think this selectivity maps to the authority space in my coupled model." Worked out by A10. Selective sampling is the β arrow of [CASE-099](../../record/cases.d/CASE-099.md), authority shaping interpretation through what it retrieves. Policies scored on current beliefs are the γ arrow. So Friston's agent runs both arrows, and his "this weakness becomes the strength" accepts capture, with the anchor moved outside the loop to selection on whole agents. The sharp edge: the mapping relocates sampling from the world to retrieval, which is [CLAIM-337](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-337.md)'s move reversed, and it holds only if the agent's own store can surprise it as the world can. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-339](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-339.md) | A judgement–observation loop cut off from action loses one route for evidence it did not select, and the free-energy principle's reason for minimizing surprise, that a persisting system must keep its states in bounds, needs action on the world, though a loop can confirm itself with action available too | The assistant's, A1 of the exchange of 2026-10-16, narrowed at A4. Friston et al. (2010) say active sampling is "mandated by ergodic arguments based on the very existence of adaptive agents", and the enactive account puts a system's own norms in its capacity to act against tendencies toward its viability boundary. Kübler and Birbaumer's extinction hypothesis is the empirical form. As first filed it said a loop without action has a self-confirming minimum. [NOTE-766](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-766.md) shows the minimum arises with action available, so the counter now claims only that losing action removes one source of evidence the loop did not select. [CLAIM-342](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-342.md) answers it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-340](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-340.md) | Scoring operators on predictions committed before the evidence is not by itself Regime B: in the linearized operator model the coupling of interpretation to endorsement has the sign of κ′ − κ̄, so commitment removes capture only when the interpretation bends the reading of the evidence no more than it bends the predictions, and it keeps the learning that revising after the evidence would lose | The formal question [QUESTION-121](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-121.md) asked, answered in [CASE-099](../../record/cases.d/CASE-099.md)'s linearization ([CASE-106](../../record/cases.d/CASE-106.md)): is committing predictions before the evidence equivalent to Regime B? No. The coupling γ is 4ηs(κ′ − κ̄), where κ̄ is how far the shared meaning bends the committed predictions and κ′ how far it bends the reading of the evidence. It is corrective, null or capturing as κ′ is below, at or above κ̄. Strong correction can fail by oscillation when \|βγ\| ≥ 1 − αδ. Revising predictions after the evidence sets γ = 0 but also stops authority from tracking who predicts well. Linear, at the symmetric state, for squared-error scoring. | Active |
| [CLAIM-341](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-341.md) v2 | The loop that keeps a lucid agent's model in touch with what it models runs between judgement and observation: each judgement reshapes the observer whose later observations it then judges, and action on the world is not part of that loop, so active inference's action–perception cycle misplaces where the agent is active | The owner's, U1 of the exchange of 2026-10-16: Friston's free-energy approach is "generally correct, but the cybernetic model is wrong"; action can be cut out of the loop, which decomposes into "Judgement (qua measurement that generates interpretations) <--> Observation". At U2 the owner held that the pragmatic-transport and will-organization models are "both manifestations of the same underlying model". The claim is stated in that model's terms, as [CLAIM-297](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-297.md)'s circuit, and in the diachronic form A4 proposed and the owner asked to be filed at U5 ("make the revisions"): a judgement need not produce the observation it judges, but it reshapes the observer that produces the next one. The sharp edge: the synchronic form, in which each judgement produces its own outcome, is not claimed. That is the open route of [TERM-039](../../record/terms.d/TERM-039.md) and [CLAIM-127](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-127.md), and the record demoted it once before ([CLAIM-109](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-109.md)). | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-342](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-342.md) v2 | What keeps a judgement–observation loop from confirming itself is evidence whose arrival or scoring does not pass through the interpretation it tests; action is one source of such evidence, not a necessary one, and a loop that selects its own evidence can lock in with action available | The assistant's synthesis, A3 and A4 of the exchange of 2026-10-16, filed at the owner's request at U5. Three results the record already holds. [CLAIM-330](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-330.md): capture in the operator model needs both arrows of [CLAIM-297](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-297.md)'s circuit, and scoring operators independently of the dominant interpretation removes it. [CLAIM-141](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-141.md): a loop always has a distributional fixed point, so what binds it is a constraint that prescribes marginals. [THEORY-285](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-285.md) ([NOTE-766](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-766.md)): an active-inference agent locks in with action available, because its own sampling policy withholds the evidence that would correct it. The claim does not say action is unimportant, only that it is not what does the anchoring. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-343](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-343.md) | For three or more parts, no ΦID can use a redundancy function that both satisfies the identity axiom and yields nonnegative atoms, because on the Williams–Beer lattice the two are inconsistent from three sources up; a ΦID verdict on three or more parts therefore rests on which axiom the analyst drops, and should name it | The record's answer to the gap left by the PID filing: no held measure gave ΦID an identity-respecting redundancy for more than two sources. Rauh et al. show none can, on the lattice ΦID uses, without a negative atom ([THEORY-713](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-713.md)). The record's measures split the difference: I_min keeps atoms nonnegative by calling independent copied bits redundant, and Ince's I_ccs keeps an independent-sources identity and reports a −1-bit atom ([THEORY-714](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-714.md)). Two-part ΦID is untouched. | Active |
| [CLAIM-344](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-344.md) | Resolve is a maintained organization of precedence, commitment and revision that keeps a temporally extended policy effective across changes in locally dominant evaluations while keeping the capacity to revise it; mere slowness or persistence is not resolve | The owner's frame, U37 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: "I think emergence of "resolve" could be an interesting frame within which to examine our will-organization framework." A37 defined it, and A39 to A47 revised it each turn: retention and revision as separate grades, fragility against rigidity (A41), access against authority (A42), and governance that makes its own repeated exercise unnecessary (A45, A47). The record already holds a theory of resolve, Ainslie's recursive staking of future choices ([THEORY-067](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-067.md)); this is its organizational and revisable form, placed in [CLAIM-159](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-159.md)'s will-organization cell. The sharp edge: the standard for when revision is warranted is not supplied, and is [QUESTION-087](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-087.md)'s open problem. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-345](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-345.md) | Equal controllability rank does not fix the cost of control: for x_{t+1} = x_t + b·u the least quadratic effort for a displacement Δ is Δ²/b², unbounded as b → 0 while the system stays controllable, and in general the least effort is Δxᵀ W_T^† Δx, set by the Gramian and not by its rank | A9 §2 and A12's Proposition 1 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, the step the owner's U9 "controllability of a system isn't binary" needs. For a scalar step x_{t+1} = x_t + b·u the cheapest input costs Δ²/b², so two systems that are both controllable can differ in cost by any factor. The general statement is the minimum-energy formula with the controllability Gramian. A9: "This is already a standard result from linear control theory." Proved here because the record holds no source for it. The sharp edge: the cost is relative to a fixed input norm, and rescaling the input makes every b cost the same, so comparing costs across systems needs inputs in common units. | Active |
| [CLAIM-346](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-346.md) | Feasibility, aggregation and epistemic coordination are independent obstructions to an organization's realizing its demands: each can occur with the other two absent | Owner-seeded, U36 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: Arrow's theorem, then cryptography and "the two generals problem". A36 placed them: "Arrow's theorem belongs in the theory of endorsement, not directly in the geometry of feasible updates", and the two generals add "epistemic frustration". Three elementary constructions, each with one obstruction and not the others, are given in the body. The sharp edge: the independence is relative to fixing the demands as the constituents' own acceptance conditions; that no remedy for one removes another is A36's further reading, argued in the body and not part of the claim. | Active |
| [CLAIM-347](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-347.md) v2 | Frustration among an organization's demands is an ordinal fact before it is a quantity: the jointly satisfiable sets of demands form a downward-closed complex whose minimal non-faces are the minimal conflicts, relief is strict enlargement of that complex for the same declared demands, reorganizations that relieve different conflicts are incomparable, and dropping or suppressing a demand is not relief | The owner's correction, U37 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: "We can do quite a lot with access to an ordering, even if it doesn't give us a true measure." A37 granted it ("I agree with your correction") and built the acceptance sets, their intersection and the compatibility complex. Corrected here: acceptance conditions sit on attainable outcomes, a demand's form depends on whether it demands improvement or maintenance, and A37's weak-preference sets fail only as representations of improvement demands. The definitional facts are shown in the body, including that every pair of demands can be jointly improvable while the triple is not. The sharp edge: relief compares complexes for the same declared demands, so a reorganization that deletes a demand is not compared at all, and two reorganizations can be incomparable. It is the move [CLAIM-128](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-128.md) and [TERM-043](../../record/terms.d/TERM-043.md) made for translation, in a new line. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-348](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-348.md) v2 | No coarse-graining is normatively adequate in general because it preserves stability, prediction or aggregate performance: a description adequate for an institution's performance can merge states its members' claims must distinguish, so adequacy is fixed by the evaluative target, and the target cannot be justified by the description that preserves it | A21 §18's Candidate 2 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, with A21 §5's clause against circularity and A22's Construction C ([CASE-110](../../record/cases.d/CASE-110.md)). A21: "no coarse-graining may be treated as generally normatively adequate solely because it preserves organizational stability, predictive accuracy, or aggregate performance." The case is demonstrated: total output preserves an institution's every judgment and merges a member's claim. The general conclusion is interpretive. It is [CLAIM-152](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-152.md)'s target-relativity carried from individuation to evaluation, and [CLAIM-060](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-060.md) in the language of coarse-graining. Filed in distributed-agency because its case is a whole against its members. The sharp edge: it does not say which target is right, and it denies that the description can settle it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-349](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-349.md) v2 | An organization's access to a signal, the signal's authority over action, and action on it are separable, and adaptive regulation keeps the first while making the second conditional on evidence; overcontrol, which buys freedom from harmful acts by cutting access or making inhibition unconditional, is a maladaptive regime and not more control, so flexibility is a context-sensitive property of maintained influence relations, not of how much is inhibited | The owner's idea, U42 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, on A41's line that an organization "could become extremely resistant to internal changes while losing its capacity to learn, correct errors, or act at all": "useful connections to the ACT and DBT frameworks here, which provide us with accounts/ontologies of maladaption and productive flexibility". In A42's form: recognizing a signal, granting it authority and acting on it are three steps; ACT's defusion is a mechanism for [CLAIM-156](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-156.md)'s decoupling; DBT adds bounded stabilization before correction; RO-DBT names overcontrol as maladaptive. The sharp edge: these are analogies from human treatment, the organizational classes are A42's proposals, not diagnoses, and RO-DBT's own evidence is "promising but not definitive". | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-350](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-350.md) v2 | A coarse-graining preserves a deterministic judgment exactly when the judgment is constant on its fibres: the judgment then factors through the coarse description, uniquely on its image, and otherwise two states the judgment tells apart have been merged | A21 §3 and §18 (Candidate 1) of the exchange of 2026-10-17, and A22's Construction A: for a judgment J and a coarse-graining C on the same states, J = j∘C for some j exactly when C(x₁) = C(x₂) implies J(x₁) = J(x₂). Granted, because it is elementary (the universal property of a quotient) and Ak's; the proof is in the body. It is [CLAIM-152](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-152.md)'s judgment-valued special case with the converse added: [CLAIM-152](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-152.md) says a partition sufficient for one target need not be for another, and this says which partitions serve a deterministic judgment, those whose fibres lie inside its level sets, so the coarsest is the judgment's own partition. The sharp edge: it says when a description keeps a judgment, never whether the judgment is right. A21's probabilistic and causal versions are recorded in the body, not claimed. | Active |
| [CLAIM-351](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-351.md) | A zero aggregate gradient does not show that the demands it aggregates are reconciled: with positive weights it holds both when every demand's own gradient vanishes and when nonzero gradients cancel, and in the second case no update improves them all to first order; even the first is only joint first-order stationarity, which a demand's maximum or saddle also has | A33 §5 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: "Zero aggregate gradient does not imply absence of frustrated demands." With L₁ = (θ − 1)² and L₂ = (θ + 1)² at θ = 0 ([CASE-115](../../record/cases.d/CASE-115.md)), the equal-weight gradient is zero and the aggregate is at its global minimum, while each loss is 1 and wants the opposite move. In general a vanishing positive combination of gradients is either every gradient zero or a certificate that no update improves them all, and the scalar cannot tell which. The sharp edge: it is a fact about a weighted sum, not about what a monitor reading it represents. A29's "the unity of the distress signal need not arise from the unity of the underlying causes" is the interpretive reading, kept apart in the body. | Active |
| [CLAIM-352](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-352.md) v2 | Sen's two aspects of freedom divide along the record's line between what an agent can viably reach and the organization that selects among it: viable reachability can stand for the opportunity aspect only through a map from trajectories to valued functionings, which control theory does not supply, and will-organization gives a causal account of part of the process aspect, not of its fairness or legitimacy | The owner's connection, U11 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, quoting A9 and A10 on viable reachability: "This feels reminiscent of ideas from moral philosophy, e.g. Amartya Sen capabilities account." Worked out by A11. Sen's opportunity aspect is what reachability can represent, once a map says which reachable trajectories are valued functionings. His process aspect is in part what will-organization models: whether the agent's own governance selected the outcome. Read first-hand in [NOTE-263](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-263.md) (Sen's lecture) and [NOTE-259](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-259.md) (Robeyns). The sharp edge: reachable states are not capabilities, and an efficacious governance is not a fair or legitimate process. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-353](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-353.md) | A will-organization that gives two operators decisive jurisdiction over a pair of options each, respects unanimous preference and admits any profile of operator rankings cannot always choose, and unlike Arrow's impossibility this is not escaped by giving endorsement richer, cardinal or comparable, information, so what distributed governance must give up is jurisdiction, unanimity or the domain, not an informational restriction | From U36 of the exchange of 2026-10-17. The owner raised Arrow; A36 answered "Sen's liberal paradox may be even closer than Arrow" and asked whether a will-organization can "guarantee coherent joint decisions while preserving specified domains of constituent authority and respecting unanimous preferences". [THEORY-027](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-027.md) says no in general, and its title holds the difference A36 cited without stating: "unlike Arrow's theorem, the conflict is not removed by interpersonal comparability". So A36's remedy, letting endorsement use richer information, answers Arrow but not jurisdiction. The sharp edge: it holds only if operator jurisdiction is pairwise decisiveness, which [THEORY-027](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-027.md) leaves open even for persons. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-354](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-354.md) v2 | Freedom of will and autonomy come in degrees along several dimensions, among them viable reachability, control cost, robustness to disturbance, governance reconfigurability, revisability and motivational integration, and agents compare by inclusion of what they can viably reach under matched conditions: a partial order with incomparable pairs, not a scalar, and a gradient only of a declared measure | The owner's, U9 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: "controllability of a system isn't binary, and neither is sensitivity to perturbation", so can the account speak of "gradations (perhaps even gradients/spectra?) of freedom of will/autonomy". Worked out by A9 §3 and §7, A10 §7 and A15's central thesis. The dimensions vary separately, and comparison is by inclusion of viably reachable sets under matched conditions, a partial order, the shape [CLAIM-128](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-128.md) has in the transport line. The sharp edge: it does not measure metaphysical free will or responsibility, and reachable-set size is not monotone with autonomy, since a precommitment can shrink the set while making an endorsed project realizable. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-355](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-355.md) v3 | In a distributed agent the binding of appraisal, readiness and governance into one response can be real while the single owner a self-model or collective self-description attributes it to is a representational construction, not a further entity; the illusion, where there is one, is in the attribution and belongs to whichever system adopts the representation, and this deflates the self without settling whether experience is real | The owner's, U26 and U27 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: at U26 whether, "adopting an illusionist/fictionalist position", the record can make claims about subjecthood; at U27, "illusory binding-type phenomena in distributed agents", starting from the record's emotion material. A26 answered with a selective fictionalism, "the imagined substantial owner, not the organized agent"; A27 located it: "The illusion might concern the owner, not the binding", and an illusion requires "which system constructs or adopts the misleading representation". The two are filed as one claim. It rests on no claim: the organizational-affect claim it would rest on, [CLAIM-039](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-039.md), is Deferred, and whether that line reopens is the owner's decision, not made here. The sharp edge: it says nothing about whether the integrated state is felt. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-356](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-356.md) | An objective that originates outside a learning system can become a goal of the training organization, through its causal role in selecting, stabilizing and reproducing the learned structure it rewards; exogenous origin does not disqualify a goal, but it does not show that the network governs or endorses the objective, and what is selected is performance on the updating distribution, not generalization | The owner's, U35 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, against A34's guardrail that "Loss minimization does not automatically mean the model has its own goals": the externally assigned objective "can motivate identification and persistence of learned features", so the training process can be described as "having endogenous goals". A35 granted it: "Yes, in a qualified but important sense", and weakened the generalization half, since selection is for performance on "the encounters and feedback distribution actually used for updating". It objects in part to [CLAIM-288](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-288.md): it grants that a modeller-fixed objective does not show self-constitution, and denies that exogenous origin bars a goal from becoming the training organization's own regulation. The sharp edge: the goal belongs to the training organization ([CLAIM-367](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-367.md)), not to the network, and nothing here says the network endorses or governs it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-357](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-357.md) v2 | The frustration residual and governance regret separate structural infeasibility, realized violation and minimax suboptimality, and identify none of them as thwarting: a positive residual says no admissible update meets every declared requirement, a positive regret only that the actual update's worst residual exceeds the best achievable, which can hold while every requirement is met, and actual violation needs regret above the negative residual; aggregate performance does not tell infeasibility from realized violation, and the multipliers that certify infeasibility are not the weights governance assigns | A34 §4 and A35 §3.3 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, restated at A39 §2C and A40 §3.4, from A33's "irreducible conflict" and "manufactured frustration". With m(d) the worst residual, Φ its minimum and Γ the actual update's excess over Φ ([CLAIM-365](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-365.md)): Φ > 0 is structural infeasibility; when Φ ≤ 0 the actual update violates a requirement exactly when Γ > −Φ. Positive governance regret establishes excess worst-case shortfall relative to the optimum, and can hold while every requirement is met. Neither quantity identifies an obstructing mechanism, a governance failure or active thwarting; governance-induced frustration needs an argument or intervention showing the allocation of influence responsible. A35: "Importantly, these dual multipliers are not endorsement weights." | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-358](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-358.md) | Learned local accommodation can reduce the amortized cost of recurrent arbitration: where a recurring conflict among an agent's processes admits a stable accommodation, settling it among the processes can cost less over repeated encounters than escalating each instance to a higher-level process that mediates or overrides, though local negotiation and the relations it needs are not free | The owner's, U45 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: "processes which can navigate disagreement amongst themselves require fewer resources from the system than processes which need to activate additional subsystems to resolve disagreement." A45 put it in conditional form: where a recurring conflict admits a stable local accommodation, learning it amortizes the cost of higher-order mediation. It refines [CLAIM-261](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-261.md)'s "pays for its unity in effort": the divided agent pays for escalation. The title stated the owner's thesis unconditionally until the owner's review of the filing put it in A45's conditional form: local negotiation, and keeping up the relations among specialists it needs, have costs of their own. The sharp edge: it does not say local settlement is always cheaper, that an integrated agent works less hard, or anything about authenticity. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-359](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-359.md) | A self-governing organization's robustness to compromised constituents is a property of the processes that allocate, audit and revise authority, not only of its constraints on harmful acts; safeguards against capture can also restrict the disagreement and revision that self-governance needs, and where they do security and self-governance trade off, while others, such as audits independent of the audited that protect dissent, serve both, so the two are distinct achievements and not opposed ones | The owner's question, U41 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: "is literature on organizational robustness to insider threats potentially useful to us?" A41's answer, condensed: an insider can aim at the organization's future allocation of authority rather than at the present decision, so robustness must protect the processes that allocate, audit and revise authority, and the same protection can silence the opposition that corrects the organization, though some safeguards protect both. Insider threats and internal opposition can look alike. The sharp edge: Byzantine fault tolerance protects a fixed specification; this claim is about protecting the capacity to change it, which no protocol of that kind supplies. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-360](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-360.md) | The deep-versus-shallow learning results separate three effects that aggregate loss conflates: a larger realizable function class, a larger set reachable within a fixed data and compute budget, and a different internal organization with the same attainable outcomes; the deep linear results show the third, the random-hierarchy results the second, and the depth-separation result for products of inputs the first, at fixed size | The owner's, U38 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, on A37's proposed experiment: "I think we may already have an empirical demonstration that would be useful here in the wide vs deep literature." A38 refined it into three effects, (A) enlarged realizable function class, (B) enlarged budgeted reachable set, (C) new internal organization without better attainable outcomes, filed Saxe et al. under C and the random hierarchy model under B, and said A "needs a separately established expressivity restriction". The record has one: Rolnick and Tegmark ([THEORY-206](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-206.md)), a separation at fixed neuron count. The sharp edge: which effect a result shows depends on the budget, and A38 warned against "depth produces individuation while width merely provides redundant capacity". | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-361](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-361.md) | Relief of an organization's frustration must be judged for an identified organization against a fixed reference set of its original demands, under an explicit correspondence between earlier and later demands: a demand excised or suppressed has changed the comparison and has not been satisfied, an endogenous revision of what the organization maintains is a transformation of it and not relief, and learning can change which processes bear the demands | The owner's prompt, U38 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, on A37's line that deleting a demand "has changed the comparison problem": "it might be helpful to connect back to our notions of individuation". Worked out by A38 and restated by A39 §2B and A43. Relief, as enlargement of the compatibility complex ([CLAIM-347](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-347.md)), is judged over a fixed reference set of demands, for an organization identified before and after, under a stated correspondence. Watson's comparison ([THEORY-232](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-232.md)) is already relative to a decomposition; what this adds is the comparison across time, when the decomposition itself changes. The sharp edge: it does not say transformation is worse than relief, only that it answers a different question. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-362](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-362.md) | Non-domination of an agent's subcomponents is a candidate constituent of authenticity as an organizational achievement, not a sufficient criterion of ownership: higher-level processes need not impose the outcome because the lower-level ones already support it or settle their disagreement among themselves, and a process that rarely decides keeps access to the roles in which it is consequential | The owner's, U45 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: "'authenticity' should be treated as an organizational achievement characterized by non-domination of subcomponents". A45 granted it as "an important correction to our developing theory", against A44's "An authentically maintained goal may demand more effort, not less". A46 refined non-domination to "continued access to consequential roles". A candidate constituent of authenticity and a candidate repair of [CLAIM-016](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-016.md)'s "integrated", not an operational test of ownership: the title said "Authenticity is ..." until the owner's review of the filing narrowed it. The authenticity line was set aside on 2026-10-05; filing this does not reopen it, and whether it reopens is the owner's call. The manipulation objection stands ([CLAIM-364](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-364.md)). | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-363](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-363.md) v2 | A normative judgment is well-defined only once its relata are fixed (the organization or standpoint it concerns, the standard, the scale and the context), and that dependence does not by itself make it relative: once they are fixed, the judgment can be false | The owner's, U17 and U18 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: "maybe part of what we've elucidated is that normative judgements necessarily rely on contexts and relations?", then "so maybe something like a frame/order/scale/perspectival-relative normative realism?" A17 put it as "Normative judgment is contextually constituted and relationally indexed, without necessarily being arbitrary or merely subjective", and A18 named the relata and split order into organizational and reflective order. The record held it only in pieces: [CLAIM-152](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-152.md) for individuation, [THEORY-220](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-220.md) for function, [CLAIM-075](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-075.md) for viability. Filed in will-organization, where the question of what organizes an agent's standards sits and where [CLAIM-075](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-075.md) marks the limit, rather than pragmatic-transport, which holds the transport of judgments between contexts ([CLAIM-115](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-115.md), [QUESTION-130](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-130.md)) and not their indexing. The sharp edge: it is the constitutive half only. That the standards themselves can be objectively better or worse is not claimed. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-364](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-364.md) v2 | Low supervisory cost cannot show non-domination: processes conditioned never to object also coordinate without override, so whether an organization dominates its processes must be read from what happens when a constituent dissents, and from its history | A45's, U45 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, against the owner's non-domination criterion ([CLAIM-362](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-362.md)) read through its economy: "a system whose subordinate processes have been conditioned never to object might exhibit perfect local harmony", and "A quiet subordinate process isn't necessarily a satisfied one." It is [THEORY-040](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-040.md)'s manipulation objection, and [QUESTION-071](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-071.md), applied to the new criterion. It does not say the criterion fails, only that cost cannot certify it. The authenticity line was set aside on 2026-10-05; whether it reopens is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-365](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-365.md) | On a norm-ball update set the local frustration residual Φ_r = min over ‖d‖ ≤ r of max_i(ε_i + g_iᵀd) equals the maximum over the simplex of Σλ_iε_i − r‖Σλ_ig_i‖_*, so infeasibility of the declared improvements has a certificate, and any admissible actual update exceeds Φ_r by a nonnegative governance regret | A34 §4 and A35 §3 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, checked. The residual is the smallest worst-case shortfall of the declared local improvements ε_i over updates of size at most r. Minimax for the bilinear problem on two compact convex sets gives the dual over the simplex, so Φ_r > 0 exactly when some λ certifies that no update meets all demands (A34's form). Governance regret Γ is the gap between an actual admissible update's shortfall and Φ_r, and is never negative. For L₁ = (θ − 1)², L₂ = (θ + 1)² at θ = 0, Φ_r = ε for every r. The sharp edge: per A37 it is a cardinal refinement of the ordinal fact ([CLAIM-347](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-347.md)), and its magnitude depends on units (A36 §2D). | Active |
| [CLAIM-366](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-366.md) | Whether several objectives share a strict first-order descent direction, 0 ∉ conv{g_i}, is invariant under smooth invertible reparameterization, while the sign of a Euclidean gradient inner product is not, and a negative inner product does not exclude common descent; a budgeted version is invariant only with the metric pulled back | A35 §2 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, at the owner's U35 "double click" on whether functionally equivalent parameterizations give equivalent frustration diagnoses. Gradients transform as g′ = Jᵀg and directions as v′ = J⁻¹v, so directional derivatives, and with them the common-descent test 0 ∉ conv{g_i}, do not depend on coordinates. A Euclidean inner product does: g₁ = (1, 1), g₂ = (1, −½) have product ½, and under θ = diag(½, 2)ψ their transforms have −7/4, while d = (−1, 0) lowers both throughout. The sharp edge: it covers coordinate changes of one parameter space at first order, not function-preserving symmetries or training trajectories ([QUESTION-127](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-127.md)). The practice corollary, not to diagnose task conflict by gradient cosine, is the anthology's. | Active |
| [CLAIM-367](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-367.md) v2 | Whether a training-time regulatory condition belongs to a network depends on which unit is individuated for the training dynamics and whose jurisdiction governs the updates; in conventional training most of that governance falls to the optimizer–model–data system, so a condition of training is not thereby a condition of the network | The owner's correction, U31 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: "it might help clarify things if we apply our individuation and volition frameworks to this system", the system being a deep network during training (U30). A31 granted it: "applying individuation first, then volition, then affect exposes a problem in our previous discussion". Individuated for the training dynamics, the unit is at least the network–optimizer pair; mapped through the six relations of will-organization, most of the governance of updates is outside the network's jurisdiction. So optimization difficulty in conventional training is real and belongs to the training system. The sharp edge: it does not say the governance is unreal, or that a network cannot come to hold some of it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-368](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-368.md) v2 | The primitive for an organization's regulatory difficulty is frustration, not distress: a relation between maintained, consequential demands and the incompatibilities, constraints or countervailing processes that limit their realization, independent of strain, the burden of sustaining activity, and distinct from distress, a possible integrated response to either | The owner's, U33 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: "maybe we should focus less on distress and more on frustration". A33 granted it: "Frustration is the most promising theoretical primitive of the three." Frustration is a fact about demands, admissible transformations and governance, so it can be stated without feelings and without a common scale. It supersedes in substance the distress-first formulations of A28–A32, which were never filed. The sharp edge: it says what to start from, not that a frustrated organization is distressed or feels anything. The claim bears on the organizational-affect line, which is Deferred ([CLAIM-039](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-039.md)); whether that line reopens is the owner's decision, not made here. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-369](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-369.md) | In symmetric resource-mediated competition with interaction matrix A = UᵀU and growth rates r = Uᵀb − c, Lotka–Volterra population dynamics are multiplicative gradient flow on the convex feature-selection objective ½‖b − Un‖² + cᵀn over the nonnegative orthant: the invasion fitness of an absent component is the negative partial derivative of that objective, and the uninvadable equilibria are its KKT points | From a synthesis the owner relayed on 2026-10-18 (workbench entry of that date), checked here and given a proof. When competition between components is mediated by shared resources with feature or niche vectors u_i, so that A = UᵀU and r = Uᵀb − c, the Lotka–Volterra dynamics are ṅ_i = −n_i ∂_i L with L(n) = ½‖b − Un‖² + cᵀn: entropic mirror descent on the positive orthant. A rare component's invasion fitness is −∂_i L, the value of adding that feature, and uninvadable equilibria are the KKT points of the convex program. The sharp edge: the identity needs a shared scalar objective, and it fails for asymmetric interactions or processes with competing objectives, which is where an organization stops being an optimizer. Mehta et al. (2019) and Marsland, Cui and Mehta (2020) give the general duality for consumer–resource models. | Active |
| [CLAIM-370](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-370.md) | For a symmetric positive-pair distribution with marginal p, the conditional-expectation operator on L²(p) has the density ratio K⁺ as its kernel, and it is Hilbert–Schmidt exactly when the χ²-divergence of the pair from the product of its marginals is finite, in which case it is compact and self-adjoint with an orthonormal eigenbasis | [THEORY-005](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-005.md)'s derivation, stated as the record's own and with its proof. Johnson et al. ([LIT-258](../../record/literature.d/LIT-258.md)) write the operator on a finite space without an integrability condition. HaoChen et al. ([LIT-249](../../record/literature.d/LIT-249.md)) reach the Hilbert– Schmidt property from a uniform bound, which is stronger. Here the condition is exact: Hilbert–Schmidt if and only if finite χ². It says nothing about what trained features span. | Active |
| [CLAIM-371](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-371.md) | Under the componentwise order on distortion profiles, two profiles are incomparable exactly when they trade off, and then positive weightings can rank them either way, while every positive weighting agrees with a dominance | The order-theoretic part of [CLAIM-128](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-128.md), on its own so that it can carry its own warrant. It says nothing about translation, what the components measure, or which weighting a task fixes. Only that, under the componentwise order of [TERM-043](../../record/terms.d/TERM-043.md), a trade-off is incomparability, that a trade-off can be resolved either way by positive weights, and that a dominance cannot. | Active |
| [CLAIM-372](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-372.md) | Within a fixed measurement scenario, the contextual fraction is a piecewise-linear and Lipschitz-continuous function of the empirical model's probability table, with a constant that depends only on the scenario | The record's own derivation, from the linear programme of Abramsky, Barbosa and Mansfield ([LIT-265](../../record/literature.d/LIT-265.md), Eq. 3), which [THEORY-165](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-165.md) holds. The non-contextual fraction is the minimum of the dual objective over finitely many vertices, so it is concave and piecewise linear in the probability table, and the contextual fraction is Lipschitz in it. The constant is the largest ℓ₁ norm of a dual vertex, which depends only on the scenario and is not bounded here. Nothing here crosses scenarios or applies to signalling data. | Active |
| [CLAIM-373](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-373.md) | If a map approximately intertwines two operators, with defects E_A = ΦA − A′Φ and E_B = ΦB − B′Φ, then Φ[A,B] − [A′,B′]Φ = A′E_B + E_A B − B′E_A − E_B A, so the commutator is carried to within (‖A‖ + ‖A′‖)‖E_B‖ + (‖B‖ + ‖B′‖)‖E_A‖ | The commutator-distortion identity of the manuscript's §9, on its own and proved. A map that nearly intertwines each of two operations carries their commutator to the commutator of the images, up to an error that is linear in the two intertwining defects. [ARG-006](../../record/arguments.d/ARG-006.md) is the exact case. It says nothing about whether the defects can be identified from data ([CLAIM-253](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-253.md)), or what bounds an observed order effect ([CLAIM-231](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-231.md)). | Active |
| [CLAIM-374](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-374.md) | On a consistently connected system, Contextuality-by-Default and the sheaf criterion agree: a maximally connected coupling exists exactly when a global law has the contexts' laws as its marginals | The record's derivation of what [NOTE-600](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-600.md) marks as its own inference. In a consistently connected system, every observable has one distribution in all the contexts that contain it. Then each connection's maximal coupling makes its copies equal with probability 1 ([LIT-777](../../record/literature.d/LIT-777.md), Theorem 3.3), so a maximally connected coupling is a joint law of one variable per observable, which is a global section in Abramsky and Brandenburger's sense ([LIT-016](../../record/literature.d/LIT-016.md), [THEORY-012](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-012.md)). Conversely, a global section gives such a coupling. The two criteria come apart only when marginals shift with context. | Active |
| [CLAIM-375](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-375.md) | Under one-hop allocation within an epoch, an operator that endorses an ally whose policy is nearer its own than the collective decision is raises how far its preferred policy is realized while its own authority stays fixed, so the two conditions of the endorsement scheme hold together | The demonstrated core of [CLAIM-285](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-285.md). Under A3's one-hop rule I_j = I_j⁰ + Σ_(k≠j) b_k E_kj, with budgets and baselines fixed within an epoch ([CLAIM-282](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-282.md)), an operator's authority does not respond to its own endorsements. If the collective decision is the authority-weighted mean of the operators' policies, then endorsing an ally whose policy is nearer one's own than the decision is moves the decision strictly toward one's policy. So the reward for realized policy, and not for held authority, pays for delegating. It does not say operators behave so, or that any rule beyond one hop keeps both conditions. | Active |
| [CLAIM-376](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-376.md) | A linear system that is stable at every instant can have a positive Lyapunov exponent: shifting any operator with exponent λ > 0 by −δI lowers its exponent by exactly δ and makes every instantaneous operator stable, so for 0 < δ < λ the shifted system is stable at every instant and unstable | The case [THEORY-724](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-724.md)'s source asserts and does not show. If A(t) has first Lyapunov exponent λ > 0, the propagator of A(t) − δI is e^(−δt)Φ(t), so its exponent is exactly λ − δ, while every eigenvalue of every A(t) moves left by δ. Applied to the undamped oscillator switching ω between 0.5 and 3 every unit of time: λ = 0.1955, and with δ = 0.1 the system is stable at every instant, with eigenvalues −0.1 ± 3i and −0.1 ± 0.5i, and has exponent 0.0955. | Active |
| [CLAIM-377](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-377.md) | For any differentiable model under squared loss with Gaussian noise of precision β, the model's Fisher on n inputs is the Gauss–Newton matrix βJᵀJ, and its non-zero eigenvalues are exactly β times those of the empirical NTK Gram matrix JJᵀ, with the same multiplicities, its eigenvectors being Jᵀ applied to the Gram matrix's, normalized | [THEORY-084](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-084.md)'s Step 1, stated as the record's own and proved. Through the SVD J = UΣVᵀ, JᵀJ and JJᵀ share the non-zero eigenvalues σ_i² with equal multiplicities, and Jᵀu/σ is the eigenvector of JᵀJ for a unit eigenvector u of JJᵀ. Under y \| x ~ N(f(x; θ), β⁻¹), the expected outer product of the score is β∇f∇fᵀ, which summed over inputs is βJᵀJ, the Gauss–Newton matrix. "Fisher" means the model's Fisher, not the empirical Fisher of observed residuals. It holds at every width, in every regime and on any data. It says nothing about what the spectrum looks like, which is [THEORY-084](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-084.md)'s. | Active |
| [CLAIM-378](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-378.md) | A representational similarity index invariant to every invertible linear map depends on a representation only through its column space, so it takes one value on all column-centred representations of rank n − 1, as it does on all representations of rank n | The generalization of [THEORY-236](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-236.md)'s theorem. If col(X) = col(Y), write X = BC and Y = BD with B a basis of the common column space and C, D of full row rank, extend C and D to invertible matrices, and the quotient maps X onto Y. Since XA always has the column space of X, the GL(p) orbits are exactly the sets of n × p matrices sharing a column space. Theorem 1 is the case of column space ℝⁿ. Column-centred matrices of rank n − 1 all have column space 1^⊥, so an invariant index cannot tell them apart either. | Active |
| [CLAIM-379](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-379.md) | On a finite set of stimuli, regularized linear readouts with ridge b > 0 cannot tell apart two centred representations of the same width exactly when one is an orthogonal transformation of the other, and the unregularized CCA member cannot tell apart representations with the same column space, which differ by an invertible linear map | The proved core of [CLAIM-269](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-269.md). What a regularized linear readout decodes is a function of K_X = X G⁻¹ Xᵀ/M, with G = aC_X + bI ([THEORY-008](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-008.md)). For b > 0, K_X and the Gram matrix XXᵀ determine each other, by a monotone map on the shared eigenvalues; and equal Gram matrices mean an orthogonal transformation ([CLAIM-290](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-290.md), [THEORY-004](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-004.md)). For b = 0, K_X is the projection onto the column space, which fixes X only up to an invertible linear map, as for a softmax layer ([THEORY-723](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-723.md)). | Active |
| [CLAIM-380](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-380.md) | With finitely many states and finitely many outcomes, the positive part of the Bayes-risk increase on the same decision problem is directed, is zero from a garbling back to its source, vanishes for every finite problem and prior exactly when the rendering is at least as informative as the source, and has A86's D_Q as its symmetrization | The finite-outcome part of [CLAIM-326](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-326.md). Define D⁺_Q(U → V; π) = sup over q in Q of [r_q,π(V) − r_q,π(U)]⁺, with losses in [0, 1] and a stated prior. A garbling never gains. The term is directed. A86's D_Q is its symmetrization. And with every finite problem and prior, it vanishes exactly when V is at least as informative as U, by Blackwell's Theorem 6 ([THEORY-717](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-717.md)). It does not cover outcome spaces that are not finite, or say this is the right decision term. | Active |
| [CLAIM-381](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-381.md) | Over a finite class of items with finite judgement outcomes, a constant outcome kernel fits every item only when the target readers' averaged responses do not vary with the item, an exact fit is a garbling with the item as common parameter, and Le Cam's deficiency and its restriction to finitely many decision problems are finite computations | The derivational core of [CLAIM-228](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-228.md). Over a finite class of items, the target term is the readers' response averaged over a procedure's output, and one outcome kernel family is fixed across the items. Then a constant kernel fits only target responses that carry no information about the item. An exact fit is a garbling, with the item as the common parameter. And Le Cam's deficiency between the two finite tables is one linear programme. Its restriction to finitely many decision problems is a finite computation. It does not say the measure is informative, which is [CLAIM-228](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-228.md)'s empirical question. | Active |
| [CLAIM-382](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-382.md) | In an autoencoder initialised with i.i.d. Gaussian weights and zero biases and trained by SGD or momentum SGD on a loss of its output, the law of each hidden layer's learned representation is invariant under every orthogonal map of that layer that commutes with its activation, so under a radial activation no basis is privileged in law, while elementwise tanh or Leaky-ReLU yields invariance only under the signed permutations or the permutations, which leaves the standard axes free to be privileged | [THEORY-288](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-288.md)'s symmetry argument, stated as the record's own and proved. Transform the parameters by orthogonal maps Q_l of the hidden spaces that commute with the activation. The network then computes Q_l h_l and the same output, so the loss is invariant. Gradient steps commute with the transformation, and an i.i.d. Gaussian initialisation is invariant under it, so the law of the trained representations is too. For radial activations this is all of O(n), and no basis is privileged in law. For elementwise ones it is a finite group, so the axes may be privileged. Whether they are, and how strongly, is [THEORY-288](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-288.md)'s empirical content. The argument needs an orthogonally equivariant optimiser, and fails for Adam. | Active |
| [CLAIM-383](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-383.md) | In plane Couette and plane Poiseuille flow, measured in the energy norm, the linearized operator about the laminar profile has transient growth at least cR and resolvent norm at least cR² with c independent of R, so wherever it is eigenvalue-stable a perturbation of norm at most CR⁻² makes it eigenvalue-unstable | [THEORY-304](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-304.md)'s scalings as lower bounds, derived. At streamwise wavenumber zero, the cross-stream velocities obey an R-independent Stokes problem in the time t/R. They force the streamwise velocity through −U′v, so the streamwise response is R times an R-independent field. In the energy norm this gives transient growth at least cR, and in the steady forced problem a resolvent norm at least cR². Through pseudospectra, a perturbation of norm O(R⁻²) then moves an eigenvalue into the unstable half-plane wherever the flow is eigenvalue-stable. The sharp constants, and that the growth is no more than order R, come from the computation and are not here. | Active |
| [CLAIM-384](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-384.md) | For a network linearised at its initial parameters, with an isotropic Gaussian prior of precision α on the parameter displacement and Gaussian noise of precision β, the log-determinant part of the evidence is −½ Σ log(1 + βλ/α) over the non-zero eigenvalues λ of the empirical neural tangent kernel's Gram matrix on the training inputs, so at most n directions are priced however many parameters the network has |  | Active |
| [CLAIM-385](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-385.md) | If a symmetric operator on ℝⁿ commutes with an orthogonal representation of a compact group, each eigenspace is a sum of real irreducibles and each multiplicity a sum of multiples of their dimensions, so SO(2) and ℤ/n for n ≥ 3 force paired eigenvalues; conversely the spectrum fixes neither the group nor a basis inside an eigenspace |  | Active |
| [CLAIM-386](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-386.md) | For x(t+1) = (A* + εC)x with A* block-diagonal, the Simon–Ando aggregate Â = QAV has one-step closure defect exactly QA − ÂQ = QA(I − VQ); the part from the blocks' own dynamics vanishes iff every block has equal column sums, and then the whole defect is ε·QC(I − VQ) | Derived in the reading of Shpak, Stadler, Wagner and Altenberg (2004), [NOTE-977](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-977.md), which states the Simon–Ando aggregate without an error term, and re-checked here. It splits the closure defect of block aggregates into a part from coupling between blocks, which is exactly first order in ε, and a part from the blocks' own dynamics, which vanishes exactly in the equitable case of [THEORY-755](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-755.md). Deterministic dynamics, one step; the post-transient orders reported below are numerical, not part of the claim. | Active |
| [CLAIM-387](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-387.md) | Optimal deterministic information-bottleneck partitions at two budgets need not be nested: with X uniform on four states and binary Y with P(Y=1\|x) = (0, 0, 1/8, 3/8), the optimum is {0,1,2 \| 3} for 6.91 < β < 9.26 and {0,1 \| 2,3} for 9.26 < β < 16.06, two partitions neither of which refines the other | Found by brute force in the reading of Equitz and Cover ([NOTE-988](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-988.md)) and re-run here from scratch. It answers the deterministic half of [QUESTION-220](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-220.md) in the negative: raising the budget can reorganize the optimal partition rather than split it. The two optima are not even laminar, so no fixed tree holds both as prunings, and [NOTE-676](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-676.md)'s nesting is bought by its tree restriction. It says nothing about stochastic encoders, where finite-β optima generally live ([THEORY-743](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-743.md)). | Active |
| [CLAIM-388](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-388.md) v3 | Exact dynamical closure of a collective variable does not require a nearly decomposable block: in a linear Gaussian VAR(1) with uniform coupling among n units, the sum of all units is exactly dynamically independent at every stable coupling strength, while every partition of the units into two or more groups couples as strongly across its edges as within them | From A5 of the exchange of 2026-10-19 on coarse-to-fine processes, which gave the two-unit continuous-time case (ż = (b − a)z for z = x₁ + x₂) and the boxed slogan "Exact aggregation ⇏ near-decomposability". Made exact here for n units with [THEORY-733](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-733.md)'s sufficiency condition, and checked numerically. It answers the converse half of [QUESTION-221](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-221.md). It does not say the closed sum is one system ([QUESTION-218](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-218.md)), and it is about partitions of the units: in the eigenbasis of A the same system is exactly decomposable, which is the point. | Active |
| [CLAIM-389](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-389.md) v2 | In a stable linear Gaussian VAR(1), exact dynamical independence of a linear coarse-graining implies that its Mori–Zwanzig memory vanishes, but not conversely: memory needs coupling out of the coarse variable and back, and dependence needs only the way back, so a coarse variable can be Markov in its own law with zero memory and still be dynamically dependent | Derived in the reading of Chorin, Hald and Kupferman (2000), [NOTE-989](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-989.md), and re-checked here. It settles, for the linear Gaussian VAR(1) class, the relation [QUESTION-222](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-222.md) left open between memory in the reduced dynamics and [THEORY-257](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-257.md)'s dynamical independence: memory is the round trip Y → rest → Y, dependence the inward leg alone. Not about VAR(p ≥ 2), where the reading reports that an independent variable can carry memory, nor about nonlinear systems. | Active |
| [CLAIM-390](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-390.md) | For a block of coordinates in a stable linear Gaussian VAR(1), dynamical dependence is at most ½ log det(I + Σ_YY⁻¹ A_YR C A_YRᵀ), with A_YR the coupling into the block and C the covariance of the rest given the block's present state, so it is of second order in the coupling into the block, whatever the coupling out of it | First found in the reading of Chorin, Hald and Kupferman (2000), [NOTE-989](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-989.md), as a by-product; derived and checked here. It answers the forward half of [QUESTION-221](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-221.md) for a block's full state: weak coupling into a block makes its dynamical dependence small, to second order. It is about the block's full state, not its aggregate. For the aggregate, the block's own internal dynamics add a defect of their own ([CLAIM-386](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-386.md)). | Active |
| [CLAIM-391](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-391.md) | For any classes with weights π_c and per-class factor matrices H_c and Δ_c, the class-wise Kronecker product Σ_c π_c H_c ⊗ Δ_c exceeds the product of the weighted averages H̄ ⊗ Δ̄ by exactly the weighted between-class covariance Σ_c π_c (H_c − H̄) ⊗ (Δ_c − Δ̄), so the two agree when either factor is the same in every class |  | Active |
| [CLAIM-392](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-392.md) v2 | Stochastic information-bottleneck optima at two budgets need not be nested: for X uniform on four states and binary Y with P(Y=1\|x) = (0, 0, 1/8, 3/8), the unique optima at β = 8 and β = 12 admit no Markov chain X → T₂ → T₁, so raising the budget reorganizes the optimum even with noisy encoders | Computed in the reading of Charvin, Catenacci Volpi and Polani (2023), [NOTE-981](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-981.md), on the source of [CLAIM-387](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-387.md), and re-run here. With [THEORY-753](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-753.md)'s criterion (refinable iff some pair of optima forms the chain, iff the coarse decoder points lie in the fine decoder hull when that hull is non-degenerate), and optima certified unique, the hull test fails. It answers the stochastic half of [QUESTION-220](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-220.md) in the negative for this source. The certificate is numerical, not interval arithmetic, and the loss is small: 0.49% of the larger budget, in unique information. | Active |
| [CLAIM-393](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-393.md) | Ordinary and legal attributions of agency to an artifact cannot serve as the independent marks that would defeat the record's agency test, because they turn on whether its mechanism is understood: a known mechanism invites "This is all just programmed!", an inscrutable one invites fear, whatever the description lengths | From Boyle's "inscrutability paradox" in The Line ([LIT-1288](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1288.md), read in [NOTE-1014](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1014.md)): if an apparently general AI comes from well-understood programming, people will doubt it has autonomous consciousness, since it is "merely doing or saying that which we have programmed it to do"; if its workings are inscrutable, they will find it "more mysterious and potentially more threatening" (p. 30; pp. 54, 246, 272). [CLAIM-174](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-174.md)'s defeater counts a test-passing factor that is "by independent marks, not an agent". For artifacts, the marks to hand, public and legal judgement, are not independent of what is known about the mechanism. The objection does not say [CLAIM-174](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-174.md)'s test is wrong, only that this route to defeating it is not open. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-394](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-394.md) | In a stationary linear Gaussian VAR(1) that is time-reversible (AΠ = ΠAᵀ, Π the stationary covariance), every slow-feature direction is a left eigenvector of A, so any span of slow features is an exactly dynamically independent coarse-graining; off reversibility a slow feature is in general neither independent nor free of memory | Derived in the reading of Schüler and Wiskott (2025), [NOTE-1015](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1015.md), and re-checked here. It says when slowness, the criterion of slow feature analysis ([THEORY-746](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-746.md)), picks out a closed coarse-graining in [THEORY-733](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-733.md)'s sense: always in the reversible linear Gaussian VAR(1), which is the class of noise-free square mixtures of independent AR(1) processes, and not in general otherwise. Linear features, linear Gaussian dynamics, lag one. | Active |
| [CLAIM-395](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-395.md) | Under Montévil and Mossio's definition of closure, a closed set of constraints has at least two members, and if each constraint is produced at the time scale of a constraint in the set that generates it and not at the scale at which it is itself conserved, the set includes at least one slow and one fast dependence, so a system with a single constraint of its own cannot realise closure |  | Active |
| [CLAIM-396](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-396.md) | Directional information measures between reciprocally coupled units follow the path of a one-sided drive rather than the symmetry of the coupling, so the observed direction of information flow does not by itself reveal which way, or whether both ways, the units are coupled | The record's generalization of one study (Das, Manikandan and Banerjee, [THEORY-275](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-275.md)) beyond its system, filed under [ADR-047](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-047.md) v4 as the record's own claim, since the source makes no general claim. It bears on the claims that read peer or hierarchical architecture from the direction of coupling. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-397](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-397.md) v2 | For a scalar stationary process with autocovariance C, the Mori–Zwanzig memory kernels for the projection onto the current value are fixed by C alone, through C(m+1) = Σ_{k≤m} c_k C(m−k); the memory vanishes iff C is geometric, and when C has finite Hankel rank d the memory has Hankel rank exactly d − 1 | Derived in the reading of Lin and Lu (2019), [NOTE-1029](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1029.md), and re-checked here. It settles the minimal-order question [CLAIM-398](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-398.md) left open, for every scalar stationary process with rational autocovariance, and gives the structural reason for [CLAIM-389](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-389.md)'s "not conversely": the memory is a functional of the coarse variable's own law, so it cannot register dependence on the rest of the system. | Active |
| [CLAIM-398](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-398.md) | In a stable linear Gaussian VAR(1) on ℝⁿ, a scalar linear coarse variable Y = LX is, in its own law, an ARMA process of orders at most (n, n − 1); so its Mori–Zwanzig memory, though non-zero at every lag in general, is carried exactly by finitely many coordinates that are functions of Y's own past and innovations | Derived in the reading of Chorin and Lu (2015), [NOTE-1028](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1028.md), and re-checked here. Their NARMAX closures are fitted, with no error bound; in the linear Gaussian class the same idea is exact. A coarse variable with memory admits a finite Markovian augmentation built only from its own history, which is the augmentation [THEORY-257](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-257.md)'s criterion conditions on. Scalar Y, VAR(1), Gaussian noise. | Active |
| [CLAIM-399](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-399.md) | Causal abstraction in the senses of Rubenstein et al., Beckers and Halpern, and Beckers, Eberhardt and Halpern does not secure governance-response sufficiency: exact transformation, τ-abstraction and zero approximate-abstraction distance all accept the hidden-bit coarse-graining, which loses ρ(h = 1) bits of the response to h, because each lets high-level noise carry what the coarse state drops; for one outcome and the identity correspondence, sufficiency is their compatibility condition with the high-level context fixed to the coarse state, and strong abstraction rejects the case only through interventions on the hidden parameter | The record's join, on 2026-10-21, of its readings of the causal-abstraction papers ([NOTE-1037](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1037.md), [NOTE-1032](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1032.md), [NOTE-1045](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1045.md)) with [CLAIM-407](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-407.md). A25 of the exchange of 2026-10-20: "We should not present governance-preserving coarse-graining as a new general theory of causal abstraction." That stands. But it is not an instance of these papers' notions either: their criteria compare interventional laws and let the high-level context be any map that works, so they accept the hidden governance bit, whose coarse state keeps nothing of the response to h. Re-checked step by step against the three THEORYs. It corrects [TERM-067](../../record/terms.d/TERM-067.md)'s first wording, which read the profile as [TERM-043](../../record/terms.d/TERM-043.md)'s causal component with the identity correspondence. | Active |
| [CLAIM-400](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-400.md) v2 | In the meaning–authority model linearized at symmetry, a third, maintained legibility state that authority moves (ε) and that moves interpretation (ν) or endorsement (μ), with persistence λ < 1 and nonnegative couplings, destabilizes the symmetric state exactly when the two-variable condition holds with β + νε/(1 − λ) in place of β and δ + με/(1 − λ) in place of δ: at the threshold the regime is indistinguishable from stronger two-variable couplings, and the slower it is the more it adds, while away from the threshold it adds a mode that no two-variable model has, so a lean in the regime can outlast the advantages it was built from | [QUESTION-253](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-253.md) asked whether authority's effect on what becomes legible as evidence is a third channel with its own instability condition, or [CLAIM-340](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-340.md)'s κ′ renamed. Both, in different respects. With a maintained legibility state r, det(I − J₃) = (1 − λ)[(1 − α)(1 − δ) − βγ] − ε[νγ + μ(1 − α)], which is (1 − λ) times [CLAIM-330](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-330.md)'s expression with β_eff = β + νε/(1 − λ) and δ_eff = δ + με/(1 − λ). So the instability threshold is a two-variable one with stronger couplings, and a slower regime (λ near 1) adds more. Away from the threshold, r is a third mode: a lean in the regime can hold interpretation and authority off symmetry after they would have relaxed, which no two-variable parameters reproduce. | Active |
| [CLAIM-401](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-401.md) | The conditions an effective description is commonly asked to meet are not of one kind: predictive sufficiency and approximation fidelity are adequacy relative to a declared target, metric and horizon; tractability restricts the operations under which adequacy is assessed; dynamical independence and memory closure are relations of the representation to the system, memory relative to a chosen projection; so a matrix over them as independent binary axes conflates kinds | A4 and A5 of the exchange of 2026-10-20, on the owner's U4 ("it sounds like considering it from this angle has potentially already altered our characterization of those 'virtues'"). A4: "(P) and (A) look increasingly like evaluation criteria, while (D) and (M) describe different aspects of system–representation structure. (C) describes resource accessibility." A5: approximation fidelity "may specify the degree to which a prediction or dynamical relation is preserved", and tractability "can instead restrict the operations over which preservation is evaluated". The record adds what A5 missed: memory is relative to the projection too ([QUESTION-222](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-222.md), [THEORY-752](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-752.md), [CLAIM-397](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-397.md)). It is [CLAIM-348](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-348.md)'s "adequacy is fixed by the evaluative target" applied to the axes of a comparison. It does not say the five reduce to three. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-402](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-402.md) v2 | Predictive success of a category across contexts warrants treating it as a real effective kind only when the evidence in those contexts does not pass through the category's own use, or when the effect of that use is modelled: a category that speakers or institutions act on can produce the regularities that confirm it, and contexts that share its use are not independent | A27 §1 of the exchange of 2026-10-20, on the owner's U27 ("are we perhaps better positioned now to address [QUESTION-007](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-007.md)? -009? -011? -028?"), after A26 §2's performative toy. It is the one criterion A27 added to A191's four on [QUESTION-028](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-028.md)'s page, and [NOTE-769](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-769.md) anticipated it from Hacking's interactive kinds. [CLAIM-342](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-342.md)'s condition on evidence, moved from a judgement–observation loop to the warrant for a kind.
