The sharp edge: it defeats one inference and is not a criterion of kindhood, so [QUESTION-028](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-028.md) stays Open. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-403](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-403.md) v2 | A contribution's admission to an organization's inquiry, its recognition in apt terms, its credibility, its uptake into judgements, and its contributor's part in revising the standards of uptake are separable, each possible without the later ones, so an organization can widen access while every channel by which a less authoritative contribution could change the criteria of authority stays closed | A24 §6 of the exchange of 2026-10-20, on the owner's U24: "Someone's contribution may be received and politely acknowledged yet have no mechanism through which it can change institutional understanding." A25 §5 repeated the split from the side of visibility, and A27 §6 used it of consultation. [CLAIM-349](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-349.md) has the three-step form for an organization's own signals. This carries it to contributors and adds the top rung, Longino's tempered equality and [QUESTION-041](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-041.md)'s channel. It does not say that every contribution is owed uptake. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-404](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-404.md) v2 | An organization can be governed from outside without any of its decisions being made for it, through the evidence its own procedures receive or through the criteria by which they weigh evidence; the two routes are distinct and each is possible without the other, so informational and evaluative independence do not imply each other; and where the organization's role is to evaluate another that affects its evidence or resources, the interpretation–authority loop can close between the two institutions, so that formal independence of the evaluator's procedure does not make its evaluation independent | The owner's cases, U20 ("maybe cases like reflexive control and regulatory capture could be informative here") and U21 (news-media capture) of the exchange of 2026-10-20, with A26 §4's two constructions and A27 §6. A20: "Control over an organization need not consist in issuing its decisions. It can consist in shaping the conditions under which the organization determines its own decisions". A26: "Neither guarantees the other." [CLAIM-342](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-342.md) holds the informational half and [CLAIM-348](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-348.md) the evaluative half. The sharp edge: it names possible mechanisms. It does not say that any regulator, newsroom or expert body is captured. The replies' empirical works, read on 2026-10-21, ground the two routes and give nothing to the loop between institutions. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-405](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-405.md) | An individuation is well defined only once its criterion is fixed (the target it serves, the transformations its unity must survive, the scale), and that dependence does not make it arbitrary: once the criterion is fixed a verdict can be false, and differently fixed criteria can pick out units of one system whose boundaries cross rather than nest, none of them less real for it | The individuation counterpart of the owner's normative thesis [CLAIM-363](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-363.md) ("once they are fixed, the judgment can be false"), as A8 and A9 of the exchange of 2026-10-20 put it, on the owner's U8 question about "contextuality/relativity/subjectivity". A8 §9: "the underlying system is objectively real, while some scientifically meaningful properties of its descriptions are objectively relational." A9: "an individuation claim can be objectively assessed once its relevant organizational criteria are specified", and "A system may exhibit several objectively grounded forms of unity that need not share the same boundary." [CLAIM-152](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-152.md) gives the relativity; this claim adds that relativity is not arbitrariness. It answers [QUESTION-065](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-065.md) in part. It does not say which criterion is right. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-406](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-406.md) v2 | Structural, pre-emptive testimonial injustice has the form of the record's trust loop: an arrangement that decides whose word is sought can keep an early exclusion going through the indicators of competence that only being sought produces, with no continuing prejudiced act; but the loop alone cannot tell perpetuated privilege from merited expertise, because that needs the prejudice condition the dynamics do not contain | The owner's, U24 of the exchange of 2026-10-20, on A23's closing thesis: "this seems to me reminiscent of themes in feminist philosophy, e.g. perpetuation of privilege". A24 granted it. [CLAIM-277](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-277.md)'s loop already runs from early advantage to "undeserved authority", and its note of 2026-10-18 from the side that loses. Fricker's un-refreshed list ([THEORY-327](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-327.md)) is a case of it in which some people's views "are not sought". The sharp edge: the loop is the same whether what it entrenches was earned, and only a prejudice makes it an injustice. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-407](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-407.md) v2 | A coarse-graining exactly informationally closed under a process's ordinary dynamics need not preserve its responses to an intervention on the mechanism: for a declared intervention distribution ρ, independent of the state, the deficiency Γ = I(S; Y^(I) \| T(S), I) can be positive, as in the hidden governance bit, where it is ρ(h = 1) bits; and Γ = 0 exactly when, for every intervention ρ charges, the response kernels are constant on T's fibres over the states of positive probability | A16 §3–4 of the exchange of 2026-10-20 and A17 §4.2–4.3, its paper's Proposition 1, reused at A18 and in A23's audit. A16: "a representation can be perfectly informationally closed under the ordinary dynamics while failing to preserve a governance-relevant counterfactual distinction." The witness is [CASE-125](../../record/cases.d/CASE-125.md); the proof is in the body, checked exactly on 2026-10-21. The qualitative point was on file: [CLAIM-350](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-350.md)'s "What it does not say" (A22's Construction B of 2026-10-17, [CLAIM-127](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-127.md)), and [CLAIM-122](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-122.md)'s supervisory test, whose note of 2026-10-18 has it without parameters. New here are the parameters, the deficiency Γ and the exact condition for Γ = 0. It is an application of ordinary causal sufficiency, not new information theory, and it does not say the system governs itself. | Active |
| [CLAIM-408](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-408.md) v3 | In ḋ = (α − κ)d − βd³, with d the difference between two parties' shares of authority, α their self-reinforcement and κ a constraint that bears on both alike, the equal allocation is the only equilibrium and is stable for κ > α, and for κ < α it is unstable and the unequal allocations ±√((α − κ)/β) are stable: a constraint that favours neither party can, by weakening, destabilize the equal allocation and make persistent unequal allocations stable, of any size near the threshold (\|d*\| → 0 as κ → α), without determining which party gains the larger share, and in this model restoring κ > α restores the equal allocation | A19 of the exchange of 2026-10-20, on the owner's U19: does the account "capture 'deregulation' as a disguised effort to induce organizational symmetry breaking?" A19's model is a supercritical pitchfork, [CASE-112](../../record/cases.d/CASE-112.md)'s normal form with no tilt and μ = α − κ. The stability analysis is checked here, algebraically and numerically. A19: "A policy can be neutral with respect to the identities of participants while being non-neutral with respect to the kinds of power distributions its dynamics produce." The claim is about the model. It says nothing about any reform, or about anyone's intent, and it does not show that a tipped allocation outlasts the constraint's return: in this model it does not. | Active |
| [CLAIM-409](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-409.md) v2 | For one state law, one intervention distribution and one outcome, a deficiency of sufficiency cannot increase under refinement: if T₁ = g∘T₂ then Γ(T₂) ≤ Γ(T₁); informational closure, by contrast, is monotone in neither direction, since in the three-bit chain of Rosas et al. (2024) A is closed, its refinement (A, B) is not and the full state refining that is closed again, while A⊕B, a coarsening of (A, B), is not closed either; closure fails to be monotone because refining a description changes the future it must predict as well as the evidence, so Blackwell dominance, which every deterministic refinement has over its coarsening, does not order closure | Two readers' drafts of one point, from A6, A7, A16 and A17 of the exchange of 2026-10-20. A16 §5 and A17's Proposition 2: a fixed-target deficiency is refinement-monotone; "Adding information cannot hurt exact sufficiency for an unchanged question, but changing what the description is expected to explain can expose new dependencies." A6 §6 and A7's Proposition 3: "Increasing the information retained by a representation can destroy a desirable dynamical property", and "There is no general data-processing inequality ordering these quantities, because both the predicted variable and the conditioning information change." This claim joins them, and corrects A7's slogan "When More Information Reveals Less Autonomy": closure is not reversed by refinement, it is unordered by it. Checked exactly on 2026-10-21. | Active |
| [CLAIM-410](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-410.md) v2 | Removing a constraint on an organization's members does not move it along one scale of more or less governance: it changes a profile of who holds rule-making, monitoring, enforcement, appeal and revision, in which the authority the constraint held can be eliminated, distributed or transferred, and the members' first-order options and their share in revising the conditions of their action can move in opposite directions | The owner's question, U18 of the exchange of 2026-10-20: "what does this suggest about e.g. "deregulation" as an ideological end?" A18: removing a regulator "does not remove governance", and the question is whether a change "eliminate[s] an exercise of authority, distribute[s] that authority, or transfer[s] it to another locus". Its five-part profile generalizes [TERM-017](../../record/terms.d/TERM-017.md)'s three relations, which "can vary independently" ([CLAIM-057](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-057.md)), and its two freedoms are [CLAIM-086](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-086.md)'s distinction carried from a constraint to its removal. The sharp edge: it says nothing about whether any deregulation is good, or about what any actual reform did. A18: "This doesn't establish that deregulation is good or bad." | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-411](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-411.md) v4 | Under the broad, nonphenomenal definition of interpretation the constitutive thesis uses, substantial processing that is not independently shown to be experiential meets the definition, blindsight's apparently unconscious visual discrimination among it, so interpretation alone does not establish experience, and the thesis must say which further organizational properties, if any, make an interpretation experiential | A3's "most serious challenge" to the owner's thesis, which every later reply accepted. Once interpretation is defined broadly and without experience, as the thesis requires, it admits much processing that is not shown to be experiential: "Interpretation ⇏ Conscious experience. At least, not under an unrestricted definition of interpretation." The objection is that the thesis is incomplete, not that it is false. A7 kept it as the theory's "open sufficiency condition". The record already separates access from experience ([QUESTION-048](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-048.md), [THEORY-577](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-577.md)), and the candidate conditions the exchange named (global availability, integration, higher-order or recursive modelling, valuation, perspective) are the question [QUESTION-254](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-254.md) asks. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-412](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-412.md) v4 | Experience is constituted by interpretive organization, not produced by it: an appropriately organized process in which signals acquire significance for a system is the experience, so whether a system can have experience becomes whether it can sustain that organization; the illusions and reappraisals that motivate it show that experience is constructed, not that construction is experience | The owner's, U1 and U2 of the exchange of 2026-10-21. At U1 the owner argued that if sensory apparatus is necessary for grounded experience, ungrounded experience is possible and "experience is fundamentally an interpretive act", and proposed recasting the possibility of experience as the possibility of interpretation. A1 asked whether experience is constituted by interpretation or produced by it; at U2 the owner chose "former reading" and named the brain in a vat, the rubber hand illusion and CBT. A1–A7 refined the thesis without changing it. Interpretation is defined without experience, and it is organization, not judgement. The step from organization to experience is an identity to be defended, not a theorem. Its open edge is the sufficiency condition, which [CLAIM-411](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-411.md) presses and [QUESTION-254](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-254.md) asks for. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-413](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-413.md) v2 | Grounding is not a property of an experience but a relation among an interpretation, what it purports to be about, and a process that constrains it independently; in its current, developmental and epistemic senses it comes apart from whether the interpretation occurs, so ungrounded is not unconstrained, and what makes an interpretive organization experiential need not be what makes its contents veridical | The owner's premise at U1, that sensory apparatus is necessary for grounded experience, as A1, A3, A5 and A7 restated it. A1: "grounding is fundamentally relational, whereas interpretation can, in principle, be internally realized." A5, after reading [CLAIM-342](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-342.md): "Sensory grounding may concern the independence of the constraints on interpretation, rather than the existence of interpretation itself", and "Ungrounded ≠ Unconstrained". A7 separated current, developmental and epistemic grounding. The epistemic sense is [CLAIM-342](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-342.md)'s, so the claim rests on it. The occurrence half is the owner's. A7: "What makes an interpretive organization experiential is not necessarily what makes its contents veridical." | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-414](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-414.md) v3 | Matched ordinary trajectories can conceal differences in an interpretation's efficacy, in the allocation of that efficacy, and in the maintenance of its enabling constraints: in two finite constructions, systems that agree under ordinary operation differ in each, and a specified intervention exposes the difference that passive observation of the matched trajectories does not | A10 of the exchange on experience and interpretation, re-run here. Three one-bit systems behave alike when the interpretation is 0. Under an intervention that sets it to 1, the first never acts on it, the second acts at once, and the third acts one step late, because its own interpretation opens the gate. Two two-constraint systems have the same trajectory, (1,1) throughout. A single perturbation runs one into (0,0) and leaves the other restored to (1,1): one maintains its constraints by [CLAIM-395](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-395.md)'s minimal slow–fast closure, the other is reset from outside. These are [CLAIM-407](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-407.md)'s lesson about governance and [CLAIM-194](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-194.md)'s about boundaries, applied to interpretation. | Active |
| [CLAIM-415](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-415.md) v3 | A person can be organized by a judgement they attribute to a collective that does not hold it: the audience in a self-model can be a false unity as the owner can, and a judgement can shape a person who rejects it, because resisting it makes it consequential | From the owner's U39 of the exchange of 2026-10-22: how an individual's perspective is shaped by "the perception of a plurality of opinion where one may not exist (e.g. because of a loud minority)", and the phrase "I am whatever you say I am". A39: "An individual's perspective can be reorganized by what they believe other people's perspectives are—even when those other perspectives have been misperceived or scarcely exist." A40 drew the extension of [CLAIM-355](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-355.md): "The imagined social audience may also be organized into a false unity." The edge: the attributed collective attitude itself is already held (List's falsely attributed common attitudes, [CASE-104](../../record/cases.d/CASE-104.md)); what is new is its place in a person's own organization. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-416](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-416.md) v2 | Competing perspectives compete over the tests that measure them as well as under them: success under an evaluative regime is evidence of competence relative to that regime, a fair test need not be neutral across competences, and a perspective that gains influence over the test can change what later success shows | From the owner's case at U35 of the exchange of 2026-10-22, rival schools of martial arts vying for dominance, and A35–A37's development of it. A35: "Competition selects perspectives, but perspectives also shape what competition selects for." A37: "Being effective under a test is not the same as possessing authority to determine what the test should measure." It is the one mechanism in the second half of the exchange the record did not already hold in some form. The edge: it does not say that tests are unfair or that rules are changed in order to entrench, which A36 kept apart as a further claim needing its own evidence. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-417](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-417.md) v2 | What the record explained by "self" in its dispositional, persisting, self-governing and integrated senses is better explained by organizational perspectives, with a perspective, its carriers, its lineage and its representations kept apart; the subject sense stays a separate question, and narrative, presented and modeled selves involve representations that may describe a perspective, attribute it to a bearer or construct an owner, none of them the perspective itself | The owner's, at U31 of the exchange of 2026-10-22: the record's earlier "selves" are "better described as 'organizational perspectives'". A31 agreed with a narrowing that this claim keeps. Selfhood "was carrying several explanatory burdens that we can now separate", and the subject sense is "A separate question". The sharp edge is what the replacement must keep apart: a perspective as realized, in one carrier or distributed across several, a recognizable character shared by many, a causal lineage of realizations, and representations of any of these. The exchange ran these together; the claim does not. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-418](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-418.md) | Possessing, enacting or being shaped by an organizational perspective does not establish an independent agent, and perspectival, agential and institutional boundaries need not coincide: several agents can share a perspective, one agent can act through several, and a perspective can be reproduced with no agent governing it; where a perspective contributes to an agent's activity it can enable and constrain the exercise of agency, and that exercise can reproduce or transform it, while whether agency universally requires such a perspective stays open | From the branch of the exchange of 2026-10-22: the owner's question at U38, "what's the relationship between perspective and agency?", A38's answer, and A39's request that it be recorded. A38: "Perspective concerns how a situation becomes meaningful and actionable. Agency concerns how an organized system can make a difference to what happens." The claim takes the half A38 called "substantially safer": not every organization of practical significance is an agent. It leaves A38's other half, that every rich exercise of agency involves such an organization, as open. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-419](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-419.md) | The record's grammar is fixed at any moment but amendable by decision, and in practice a move that fit no scheme or relation has been treated as a defect of the grammar and answered by amending it, so a move the grammar cannot hold is not only forced or lost; whether every such move is caught is not shown | The owner's, at U18 of the exchange of 2026-10-22: "when we've encountered representational challenges, we've generally interpreted those as a symptom of a represntational weakness of the record and used that opportunity to mature the luria configuration, featureset, or both." It answers [CLAIM-181](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-181.md), which had no reply on file, with the record's own decisions as evidence. It narrows [CLAIM-181](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-181.md) rather than defeating it: the record shows moves that were caught and the grammar amended, and one case ([ADR-032](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-032.md)'s literature roles) where a recurring move was left in prose. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-420](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-420.md) v2 | A historical figure's influence can persist in three separable ways, as a story about them, as a perspective transmitted from them, and as later organizations that descend from their activity, and causal descent is not perspectival inheritance: a movement founded to oppose someone descends from them without continuing their perspective | From the owner's U38 of the exchange of 2026-10-22, corrected. The owner contrasted "immortality" through positive influence that reinforces itself across generations with notoriety whose influence is "self-undermining", the figure's story persisting while their perspective does not. A38 corrected the example and declined the tidy mapping of positive to self-reinforcing and notorious to self-undermining. What survives is the distinction: "The persistence of a story about someone is not equivalent to the persistence of that person's perspective." | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-421](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-421.md) v2 | The cessation of an organizational perspective's enactment need not imply the loss of the dispositions, capacities or reproductive relationships from which it can be reinstated: an interruption can be of activation (the disposition is held but not engaged or retrievable), of enactment (held, but the material, institutional or social conditions for exercising it are gone) or of reproduction (held by some bearers, but the processes that pass it on are damaged), and temporary unavailability is not extinction, though prolonged interruption of reproduction can end a tradition | From the branch of the exchange of 2026-10-22, at the owner's U37: the doorway effect as perspective meeting grounded experience, and the perspectival interruptions of the Chinese Cultural Revolution. A37 drew the distinction the record's account of perspective lacked, between the conditions under which a perspective is acquired or reproduced and the conditions under which it stays available. The edge: a doorway and a political revolution are not the same kind of forgetting, and the claim says only that both separate a capacity's existence from the availability of the conditions for exercising it. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-422](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-422.md) v2 | A successful corrective act, an executable procedure and a maintained organizational capacity are three different things, so an organization can record a correction, even rewrite its rules, without the corrected capacity being maintained | From A24 and A25 of the exchange of 2026-10-22, on the owner's cases of a fireground mayday that changed a safety culture (U23) and NASA's learning before and after Challenger (U25). A24: "A successful act, an executable procedure, and a maintained organizational capacity are three different things." A25: "Institutional learning is not complete when an organization changes its rules." It rests on [CLAIM-025](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-025.md), which separates the operative regime, the governing organization and the capacity to transform it, and grounds in the record's reading of the Columbia board ([THEORY-140](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-140.md)). The edge: a recorded correction is evidence of a correction, not of its maintenance. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-423](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-423.md) v2 | In a political collective, components that change at different speeds (attention, coalitions, elected terms, administrative careers, law and treaties, generational memory) are not only nested by timescale: under a given institutional arrangement their different temporal horizons affect which forms of influence each can accumulate, exercise and keep, and so which of them holds authority over the others, so a state can stay continuous as an actor while the organization producing its positions changes under it | The owner's, at U28 of the exchange of 2026-10-22: domestic and international politics as the domain where "regime ecology" becomes concrete, with "separation of timescales in elections, durations of tenure in elected and appointed positions, lifespan, generational memory, etc." creating "amorphous collectives of varying orders and degrees of coupling". A28 stated the claim in its strongest line. It rests on [CLAIM-331](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-331.md), that higher-level variables are characteristically slower, and denies that slowness alone settles which level governs. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-424](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-424.md) v2 | Reflexive governance of an organization's perspectival arrangements, making the conditions that govern its own interpretation, evaluation and action available for effective examination and revision, is a capacity some organizations of perspective have and others lack, not what makes something a perspective or a self; and the capacity to revise those standards is not the capacity to revise them well | A37's definition, from the exchange of 2026-10-22, re-scoping [CLAIM-284](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-284.md). [CLAIM-284](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-284.md) says that a self, in the self-governing sense, is an organization that governs the conditions of its own persistence. Every reply from A31 on read it as "a proposed special case": a perspective can be reproduced, persist and adapt without governing its own reproduction. The edge is A37's: "the capacity to revise the standards of evaluation is not equivalent to the capacity to revise them well", which keeps [CLAIM-288](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-288.md)'s objection standing. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-425](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-425.md) | A perspective governs itself with respect to social judgement to the extent that it keeps access to others' judgements of it while making their authority over its conduct depend on their grounds, not on their visibility, repetition or attributed plurality; neither absorbing every judgement nor resisting every one meets this, since both let the judgements set the terms of response | A39's closing thesis and its fourth reading of "I am whatever you say I am", in the exchange of 2026-10-22, filed at the owner's request. A39: "I can recognize the judgment, investigate its grounds, and refuse to let its apparent popularity substitute for its actual credibility." It is [CLAIM-349](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-349.md)'s keep-access, condition-authority applied to judgements of oneself, with [CLAIM-415](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-415.md)'s false audience as what it guards against. The edge: the opposite of conformity is not independence, since resisting every judgement is governed by the judgements as much as obeying them. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-426](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-426.md) | Where a judgement's circulation helps bring about what it says, its visibility, repetition and attributed plurality are evidence about what it will bring about, so the rule that a judgement's authority should come from its grounds and not its visibility holds for judgements of what already is, and not without qualification for judgements of what someone or something will become | The owner, on 2026-10-23, after [CLAIM-425](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-425.md) was filed: "this is reminding me of 'hyperstition'". Land's definition ([LIT-1396](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1396.md)): "hyperstitions – by their very existence as ideas – function causally to bring about their own reality." [CLAIM-425](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-425.md) says not to let a judgement's popularity substitute for its credibility. For a judgement whose popularity helps make it true, popularity is evidence, about the future it is helping to make. The claim narrows [CLAIM-425](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-425.md), it does not reverse it: visibility still does not show what was the case. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-427](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-427.md) | Evidence for the organizational-perspective framework comes in three grades: that different organizations produce the same ordinary behaviour, that they respond differently to independently specified interventions, and that a model representing the coupling of significance, authority and reproduction predicts those responses better than the strongest existing alternatives at matched complexity; only the third bears on the framework's defeater, and no grade counts if success is defined in the framework's own terms | From the exchange of 2026-10-23, "Framework Overview Request": the owner's U3, "what's the value add here? be it achieved or just intended", and U4, on experiments. A3: "Its strongest achieved contribution is conceptual disentanglement", and the test is "whether it changes what we can infer, distinguish, explain, or discover". A4 set the grades and the rule "not to use the framework itself to define experimental success". [CLAIM-414](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-414.md) already meets the first grade for two constructions. Nothing in the record meets the third. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-428](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-428.md) | Material interdependence couples parties' outcomes without fixing the perspective that organizes the coupling: the same dependence can be organized as mutual opportunity, as competitive vulnerability or as coercive leverage, so interdependence makes for peace to the extent that cooperation also changes which interests the parties' institutions recognize and who holds influence over them, and payoff coupling alone does not settle which | From the exchange of 2026-10-22, "Ben Franklin Effect Explained": the owner's U6, "maybe this is a stretch, but this seems like it could be connected to the hypothesis that trade promotes peace by coupling interests through coupled economies". A6: "economic coupling is not the same thing as cooperative perspectival integration." The claim is the commercial-peace debate read through [CLAIM-429](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-429.md): trade changes the state of a relationship, and the question is when it changes the coupling. | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-429](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-429.md) | Where interpretation decides the occasions for cooperation and cooperation's outcomes revise interpretation, an intervention can change the loop's state or its coupling: a favourable exception changes the state and leaves the rule that judges exceptions as it was, while a change in how far outcomes may revise the governing interpretation changes the coupling, and only the second changes how later evidence is taken | From the exchange of 2026-10-22, "Ben Franklin Effect Explained", A5: "One could ask whether a perspectival invitation changes only the initial state of the system, or also changes the coupling parameters—the degree to which cooperative outcomes acquire authority over future interpretations." And: "It is the difference between introducing a positive exception and changing how exceptions are allowed to revise the rule." The owner took the line up at U6. The claim puts it in the terms of the record's coupled meaning–influence model ([CLAIM-297](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-297.md), [CLAIM-330](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-330.md)). | Proposed |
| [CLAIM-430](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-430.md) | An interaction can make available a way of relating that its participants' operative perspectives did not hold, by giving each a role to enact in it, so that taking part can come before, and help produce, the reinterpretation it later seems to express; whether the change stays with the partner, generalizes to a category, or is reproduced beyond the encounter are further questions | From the exchange of 2026-10-22, "Ben Franklin Effect Explained": the owner's U2, asking for Franklin's favour to be read as a "perspectival invitation", and U4, connecting it to intergroup contact. A3: "A favor request invites its recipient to enact a relational perspective that may not yet be part of their established way of interpreting the requester." It is [CLAIM-275](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-275.md)'s intervention before interpretation applied between people, and a candidate answer to the first half of [QUESTION-259](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-259.md), how perspectives are generated. The phrase was coined in A3; the record had not used it before. | Proposed |
